# Decisions

The choices that shape the code, with their reasons. Most were settled with the owner in the design plan (`~/.claude/plans/nous-allons-creer-un-mighty-perlis.md`, "Décisions verrouillées"); the rest came out of building M0–M5. Revisit one only deliberately, and record the change here.

| # | Decision | Why | Consequences in the code |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **One persistent colony per player**, no "new game" | The Clash-of-Clans-like loop: a single place that grows for weeks | Saves are sacred: no silent reset, A/B slots, migrations (M7) |
| D2 | **Unlimited offline production**, bounded by "world time" (per-tile caps, minimum research durations, digging in tiles/hour, growing waystation costs, market depth) instead of a cap on hours away | Fairness without punishing absence | An exact catch-up engine ([offline.md](offline.md)); every mechanic must be offline-exact |
| D3 | **Flow-based, integer, deterministic simulation**, the same online and offline, replayable | Exactness, server validation later, no float drift | Units in milli-items / kW / ‰ / centi-ocelles; the determinism guard; commands logged and replayable |
| D4 | **Engine purity**: `src/sim`, `src/content`, `src/market` compile without the DOM | Run the catch-up in a Worker, and the same code on a Node server | `src/sim/tsconfig.json`; the shell supplies time; the sim returns events instead of calling the UI |
| D5 | **PixiJS 8.21.0, pinned**, with a custom loop (no `Application`) and texture GC off | WebGL performance for tens of thousands of sprites; full control of render order and lifetimes | Pixi stays in `src/render`; textures are owned and freed by hand |
| D6 | **Isometric 2.5D drawn view by default; top-down plan view while building**; the board morphs between them (0.5 s) | Iso looks like a place (Anno, Spice Wars), not like Mindustry; plan is clearer for laying ribbons. The owner chose this at the M2 art review, then asked for plan-on-build | One projection `P(t)`; flat content in tile space, volumes in iso space; picking differs per view; tools act only on the plan side |
| D7 | **Art generated in code**, a Moebius-like ink style pushed to contrasted mechanical sci-fi (cold steel / teal / cyan / violet machines with LEDs and cables on warm pastel desert) | A distinctive look; no asset pipeline; every sprite consistent | Canvas2D recipes → atlases; terrain painted in workers; the dev art board for review |
| D8 | **Relief as terraced columns**: chamfered by their 8 neighbours, nested slabs, depth-sorted with buildings; painted into the ground texture when zoomed out | Mountains you dig must read as volumes and hide what is behind them | `relief.ts`, `relief-view.ts`, `DynAtlas`; a level per tile used by overlays |
| D9 | **Simulated NPC market first** (40 sister colonies) behind a `MarketService` contract | Trading matters from the first hour; a real server can come later without touching the sim | `src/market` is pure; the sim settles once per epoch, online and offline, through the same function |
| D10 | **Signature feature: "Économie visible"**: a planet / orbital view of the Crier with sister colonies, freight routes and cargo launches | Chosen by the owner at the art review; it makes the market tangible | Delivered with M6 (render + UI) |
| D11 | **Anno-style influence zones** around the Hearth and waystations; dug or filled land touching a zone joins it | Space is the mid-game pressure; digging is the slow, contiguous alternative to expansion | `world/influence.ts`; placement rules per kind; remote construction delays |
| D12 | **French source strings, English parity**, typed keys, French typography enforced | The owner plays in French; quality typography | `fr.ts` is `as const`; the parity and typography tests |
| D13 | **No combat; desktop first; AZERTY native** (physical codes for movement / pipette / digits, mnemonic letters otherwise) | Scope; the owner's keyboard | `input/bindings.ts` |
| D14 | **Commands as the only mutation path**, each logged with its tick | Replay, validation, future undo | `src/sim/commands.ts`; `exec`, `replay`, tested |
| D15 | **Sim facade + systems** (since the M5 architecture pass): `Sim` holds state and API, `src/sim/systems/*` hold the rules | Keep the 900-line god class from growing with every milestone; give each domain one home | New domains (trade in M6, quests in M8) get a systems file |
| D16 | **Layering enforced by a test** | Architecture that is only written down drifts | `src/architecture.test.ts`; `ARCHITECTURE.md` lists the same table |
| D17 | **Long processes as pseudo-flows with an allowance** (research, digs / fills, freight): payments are ⌊total · elapsed / duration⌋, path-independent | They live outside the flow graph, yet the catch-up must reproduce them exactly | They attach to `ReservoirNode`s; the catch-up adds their in / out rates to reservoir keys and cuts segments at their completions |
| D18 | **Excavator reach 18 tiles** (the plan said nothing; the zone is 24 and the Crown starts around r 40) — *superseded by D54* | From inside the starting zone you must be able to reach the Crown | `BAL.digReach`; documented in the memory notes |
| D19 | **No commits without the owner's request** | The owner's rule | Agents never run state-changing git commands |
| D20 | **Mindustry-style logistics from the first minute** (owner's playtest, 2026-10-11): router, sorter / inverted sorter, overflow / underflow gates, unloader, junction, bridge, all `start` (since D33: cheap plans of the tree's logistics lane, the router first, unlocked in a click); the 2×2 distributor with dn5 | Laying out a factory is the fun; routing blocks are its vocabulary and should not wait behind research | Routing blocks (kind `fork`) link to whatever surrounds them, not to fixed ports; ribbon bends are drawn from the compiled graph |
| D21 | ~~**Humans vs machines in the art**~~ (owner, 2026-10-11; **superseded by D29** the same day: no humans at the base): glass, domes and signs of life only where people live or work (the Hearth first — a glass dome / habitat —, the observatory, waystations, the trade post's office, the skydock's crew cabin); machine-only blocks (extraction, processing, storage, energy, logistics) are purely industrial — no domes | The colony reads at a glance: where the people are vs the machine fields around them; avoids "everything is a dome" | Art recipes in `render/art/recipes/buildings*.ts` follow it; new buildings pick a side first |
| D22 | **One item per ribbon square** (owner, 2026-10-11): a ribbon tile holds one item; a full ribbon blocks; items never overlap. Conduits keep 4 per tile (water is not drawn) | Readable lines: each item is its own square, a jam reads at a glance | `BAL.beltDensity = 1`; throughput is unchanged (ribbons move > 1 tile per second); the items view draws whole items only |
| D24 | **Replacement placement** (owner, 2026-10-11: "place some things over some other things, replacing them"). A building may be placed over buildings of its own replace group that lie wholly inside its footprint: a router over a ribbon, a swift ribbon over a ribbon, a distributor over four routers, a pylon over a pole, a deep bore over a drill. Groups: `transport` (ribbons, routing blocks, junction, bridge), `fluid` (conduits), `power` (masts), `drill` (drills); everything else, the Hearth included, has none. Ribbon drags keep the routing blocks they run through and lay a junction where they cut straight across another ribbon line (Mindustry's auto-junction) | A factory evolves by being re-laid in place: demolishing first is busywork. Mindustry's rules are the ones players already know. Re-placing the very same building is still refused, and so is turning a block whose rotation means nothing (router, mast…): there would be nothing to do | Content `replaceGroup`. `systems/build.ts`: `replaceable` and `turns`, `PlaceCheck.replaces` (ascending ids), affordability counting the refund, and `place` removing (refunding) before paying, then logging one `place`. A same-def replacement keeps its `cfg`. `planBelts` is the one plan for `placeBelts` and the ghost; it shares one budget, and each placement is logged on its own, so a replay needs no planner. The preview's `doomed` and net cost are in `input/tools.ts`. Tests: `replace.test.ts` |
| D25 | **Explicit power wiring** (owner, 2026-10-11: "the electricity should be linked to things that require electricity or produce it… you put pillars, and click to link the pillars to the generator, and then to things that use it. By default, the heart should have a solar panel… The pillars can link up to a limited length, and we need to see the wire visually. It can go above other buildings"). Mindustry's power nodes: a wire is an explicit, saved pair of buildings, one end a pillar (pole, pylon) or the Heart, the other a pillar or anything that makes, uses or stores power; grids are the wires' connected components, nothing is powered by proximity. Reach is footprint edge to edge, the longer range of the two ends: pole 6 tiles / 8 wires, pylon 24 (+16 with xp4) / 4, the Heart 8 / 6. A new pillar wires itself to the nearest pillar or Heart in reach with a free slot; generators and consumers are wired by the player (the « Relier » tool, L). The Heart's power is now a built-in solar panel: 240 kW at full sun, nothing at night | Supply squares powered whatever stood near a pole, so power was invisible and never a decision. Wires make the network a thing you lay out, see and reason about (which grid, which pillar is full, what a cut isolates), as in Mindustry; the reach and slot limits make pillars matter. A solar Heart runs a couple of machines by day and leaves the night to wind, coal generators and accumulators: power planning starts on day one | `sim.links` (`power/links.ts`: `PowerLinks`, `linkCheck`), saved (`SaveData.links`, optional) and hashed; `link` / `unlink` commands (logged, replayed); `Sim.place` carries the wires of what it replaces over (link points first, then by id; a pylon keeps 4) and auto-wires a new pillar; `Sim.remove` cuts a building's wires. `buildGrids` (union-find over built, awake, linkable members; ids by smallest link point). Content `pole: { range, slots, tall? }`; `BAL.hearthSolarKw`. Generator output through `weatherAt` / `producerKw` (one place for temperature next). `input/link-tool.ts`, `render/world/wires-view.ts` (+ `wire-geom.ts`), the `wires` / `wiresPlan` layers, the power overlay (P), the inspector's power rows (the grid module since D76). Tests: `power/links.test.ts`, `input/link-tool.test.ts`, `render/world/wire-geom.test.ts`; test layouts are wired (`testkit.wire`), and the exact offline tests use `steadyPower` (a geothermal tap) since the Heart's panel follows the sun |
| D26 | **Day/night look and climate** (owner, 2026-10-11: "night/day visuals, and effect on solar panels and temperature"; machine lights only since D29) | The day (36 minutes: plan §3) must be seen, not only read in the HUD: a keyframed grade that never goes black (lilac / indigo night, peach and rose dawn, a neutral warm day, coral and amber dusk, plan §9) makes the time and the sun power legible at a glance; the night belongs to the machines (LEDs, work lamps, landing lights, the Heart's uplink beacon; amber only where a machine runs hot); solar panels open in the sun and close into a bud at night; a desert temperature (2–6 °C at the end of the night, 34–40 °C mid-afternoon) will weigh on solar output and on the base's survival heaters | The look reads the sim's world clock (`render/daynight.ts`): one multiply tint on the `lit` render group, lighter in the plan view; night glows on the additive `lights` layer, found in the art itself by a spy context (`art/recipes/fx.ts`), so recipes only draw lights with the LED tokens and solar cells with `PV` (`art/solar.ts`); cloud shadows by day. The climate is a pure integer function of the world clock and `WORLD_SEED` (`sim/world/climate.ts`, `BAL.climate`), one value per epoch for power; `solarHeatPermille` and `heaterKw` are prepared for the power model. `FL.dayPhase` shows any hour (look and HUD only) |
| D27 | **Main menu over the live colony; locked constructions hidden** (owner, 2026-10-11: "tutorial and main menu"; "don't display constructions and things that haven't been unlocked yet, not even in transparent"). The menu (the title, the base's name, Jouer / Paramètres / À propos) opens at boot and on Esc once nothing else is open, over the running world: a paper veil, a slow camera drift, the HUD hidden, the sim ticking. The build bar shows only unlocked buildings; a category with nothing unlocked has no tab | A persistent world never stops, so its title screen is the world itself; no « new game » (D1) and, until M7, no save to continue. Greyed-out entries read as clutter and spoil what research brings; showing something when it unlocks makes the unlock an event | `installMenu` and `MenuDrift` in `app/screens.ts`, `ui/screens/menu.ts` + `settings.ts` (preferences in localStorage via `src/settings.ts`), `Screen.modal` / `back()`, `ScreenHost.escapeTo`. Automation (`navigator.webdriver`) and `?nomenu` never open it by themselves (`?menu` forces it; `FL.menu(true)`). The bar: `unlockedTray` / `shownCategories`, hotkeys over what is shown (Shift+n = n-th tab shown), a « Nouveau : X » notice and tags when something unlocks. M7 hook: load + catch-up before the menu, « Jouer » → the absence report |
| D28 | **Tutorial as quests 0–11** (owner, 2026-10-11: "tutorial"): the plan's §8 « Prise en main », adapted to the game as it is — ordinary early materials, wires from the first drill (D25), the Crier (M6) — run in order, one at a time; CrierBelt sets each objective and pays its reward (D29); a guide points at what to use next | The first hour teaches the game's verbs in the order they matter (extract, wire, smelt, power, research, sell, buy, dig); rewards are real materials, so the tutorial also paces the start; a persistent colony must never be blocked or double-paid by its tutorial | Content `content/quests.ts` (objectives, rewards, guide steps); engine `sim/systems/quests.ts`: quest state saved and hashed, objectives read state or counters since the quest began, completion checked every second in `step`, in the `name` command and after a catch-up (`checkQuests`), each completion logged as a `quest` command that a replay refuses (it completed on its own); rate objectives over whole game minutes; fees waived through `systems/trade.ts`. UI `ui/quest-log.ts`, `ui/guide.ts`, the naming modal (never under automation). Tests: `quests.test.ts`. [quests.md](quests.md) |
| D29 | **No humans at the base; the player pilots it from afar** (owner, 2026-10-11): the player is an investor living in Verrière (the planet's only human city) who partnered with CrierBelt (the company that runs the Crier Ring and its cargo lifts) to open a remote, fully automated extraction claim. Supersedes the human side of D21 | "No humans here, let's drop the human stuff": the player does what that investor would do — decide what to extract and how to build the base | [lore.md](lore.md) is the source for every story text; nothing at the base shows people (no lit windows, doors, gardens, crews: machines only); the Hearth is displayed « le Cœur » / "the Heart"; the market's actors are « concessions sœurs » / "sister claims"; science items are data; code ids unchanged |
| D30 | **Generative audio, no files** (owner, 2026-10-12: "let's launch an agent to put some background music and sound design. can download music if needed."): every sound is synthesized in code with Web Audio, like the art: an ambient score that never loops (a seeded generative composer: pads, a modal / pentatonic melody in phrases, pulses that grow with the machines running), turning with the world clock (Lydian dawn, Ionian day, Mixolydian dusk, Dorian night, all on D); an ambience of wind (the gust level), machines in view and ribbons up close; short, soft cues for the interface and the game's events. Nothing is downloaded | The game already makes all of its pictures in code; recorded music would be the only asset, would loop audibly in a game left running for hours, and could not follow the time of day or the base. Generated sound weighs nothing, needs no licence, and can be tuned in numbers | A new layer `src/audio` (core and content only; only the shell imports it; Web Audio is used nowhere else: `architecture.test.ts`); `app/sound.ts` wires it (gesture unlock, hidden tab, the world snapshot, the cues); cues travel on bus `sfx` (`SfxCue` in `core/bus.ts`); three live volume sliders (general, music, effects and ambience) in the settings page and `src/settings.ts`; the levels are set by offline measurement (music ≈ −18 LUFS at the default sliders, every cue under −6 dBFS at full; [audio.md](audio.md)); silent until the first gesture, muted when the tab is hidden |
| D31 | **The intro sequence and the company name** (owner, 2026-10-12: "a real sequence when the player starts the game: small lore animation with the human investing his money into creating his own mining company where he'll pilot everything. He receives the confirmation from the massive company that owns the ring… that he's allowed to use their service (fret, commerce…). He selects the name of his company (the player chooses it, should be unique). And then we see the expensive heart being deployed in the cirque"). A new base opens on a comic strip after « Jouer »: Verrière at dusk, the investor, CrierBelt's holographic contract (« Accordé »), CrierBelt's register where the player names the company — the claim takes the same name —, then the Heart landing in the empty cirque under a CrierBelt sky-crane; the tutorial follows. Skippable (« Passer », Esc) up to the register, never past it. The name must be free: sister claims (either language), CrierBelt's and the world's own names, compared without case, accents or dashes | The first minute should say who the player is and why the base is empty before asking anything of them ([lore.md](lore.md)); naming the company is the one decision of the intro, so it is the one step that cannot be skipped; the Heart arriving makes the base an event rather than a given. There is no server yet, so uniqueness is checked locally, through a promise that a server can answer later | `ui/screens/intro.ts` (DOM, the strip, the register; modal screen `'intro'`), `intro-art.ts` (inline SVG drawings, the CrierBelt mark on a canvas), `intro-name.ts` (`checkName` → `Promise<NameVerdict>`, `NameRegister.remote` the server hook, `nameVerdict` also used by the quest log's naming modal), `styles/intro.css`; `render/world/landing-view.ts` (`LandingView` on `fxTop`, flames on the new untinted `fxGlow` layer); `app/intro.ts` (when it plays: an unnamed base, not under automation / `?nomenu` / `?demo`; the Heart hidden until touchdown through `BuildingsView.hide` and `LightsView.hidden` — the sim is untouched; the camera; `nameColony` after touchdown so quest 0's stamp shows); bus `intro:panel`, `intro:stamp`, `intro:land`, `intro:done` for the audio; `FL.intro(open?, at?)`. The naming modal stays the fallback when the intro does not run |
| D32 | **The interface grows with the tutorial** (owner, 2026-10-12: "during the tutorial, don't display any data that isn't used at all by the player yet") | A new player sees only what the current step needs; money, coal, temperature, the Crier… appear when the tutorial introduces them | `QuestDef.reveals` + `revealed(sim)`; HUD chips, the build bar (one `build` reveal since D33: its tabs follow what is unlocked), the brush, the Build button and hotkeys read it; « Passer le tutoriel » (`skipQuests`) or finishing it shows everything; the demo starts skipped ([quests.md](quests.md)) |
| D33 | **One unlock tree: plans and research** (owner, 2026-10-12: "you should have a tree, visual with icons, where you unlock everything you use, even during the tutorial. And so the tutorial would encourage the player to unlock and try things such as the router.") | Unlocking becomes a decision and an event rather than a given: the base's vocabulary grows with the player, Mindustry-style, from a bare starting kit (drill, ribbon, pole, the Link tool) through cheap **plans** paid from the Heart at once to **research**, the tree's advanced part, unchanged in its rules. One visual tree with icons shows what each node gives, costs and needs; the tutorial has the player take each plan before using it (telemetry, then the smelter and the router…), so the router is tried, not just read about | Content `PLANS` beside `RESEARCH` ([research.ts](../src/content/research.ts)): `lane`, `prereq`, an item `cost`, optional `effects` (telemetry is a plan, no longer tr1's); buildings' `unlock` names a plan; research's roots hang from the observatory's plan; one id space, one `sim.done` (saved, hashed), `sim.unlocked` and `computeMods` cover both. The `unlockPlan` command ([systems/research.ts](../src/sim/systems/research.ts): paid from the Heart, refused `unknown` / `done` / `prereq` / `unaffordable`, logged, replayed) and the `plan` event. Validation: one DAG, every node and every building reachable, the root plans affordable from the starting stock together, the tutorial unlocks before it uses. The tech tree screen ([ui.md](ui.md#the-tech-tree-screen)) and the HUD's tree chip; the quests' `unlocked` objective, `feeds` (D34) and the guide's `unlock` / `branch` targets; the bar's tabs follow unlocks, the tutorial's `tab:*` reveals became one `build`. `testSim()` learns every plan unless `{ plans: false }`; the demo and `FL.unlock` learn them through commands |
| D34 | **The Heart takes, it never gives** (owner, 2026-10-12: "let's delete the ability to output anything from the heart. and adapt the tutorial to that.") | The Heart was a universal source: any line could start from its stock through its red outlets or an unloader, which short-circuited the logistics game (why route a coal line when the Heart hands coal out?). Now machines are fed by production lines, and the Heart is where things end: it pays for construction, and its stock sells through a trade post set against it | The Heart's six ports are all inputs (`buildings.ts`); the compiler gives unloaders no virtual port on it; `setPortItem` refuses any port that is not an `out` port; its inspector has no outlets panel; the demo feeds its smelter from drills. Tests that needed a stocked source use `stockedSilo` ([testkit.ts](../src/sim/testkit.ts)). The tutorial teaches feeding the smelter straight from the drills (D33) |
| D35 | **A directional router you can configure** (owner, 2026-10-12: "the router should have some configuration available: where to send which entry... and should only accept things coming from one side") | The Mindustry router took from every side, so it pulled back from the lines it fed and merged anything into anything; and its per-side filters waited for Long Bridges (dn2). A router now behaves like the other directional blocks: it is a branch you can read at a glance and set up from the start | `canIn` / `canOut` in compile.ts: the router takes from its back only and gives to its front and sides; the distributor (logic `distributor`) keeps the all-sides rule. Per-side filters are always on for both (`filtersOn`; dn2 now only lengthens bridges). `placementRot`: a one-tile directional block dropped onto a ribbon follows it (the ghost shows it). The inspector names the router's way in and offers a filter per way out; its sprite marks the intake (turquoise lip, inward chevron) and is drawn per rotation |
| D36 | **CrierBelt's restart reserve: a base can always rebuild its production** (owner, 2026-10-12: "the tutorial lets the user hard lock itself if he unlock the wrong techs, and then has no more of a given item and nothing that produces it. Make sure this cannot happen, and can never happen in the future.") | The Heart only takes (D34) and plans are paid for good (D33): a new base could spend on plans the plates, glass or copper wire its production is made of, and be left with nothing that makes them and nothing to build it with. A free bootstrap would empty the early economy, and a CrierBelt hand-out could be farmed and needs a time rule for the catch-up. A guard on the early game's one permanent spending (plans) prevents the dead end, adds no state, and can be proved from content | Content [reserve.ts](../src/content/reserve.ts): `BOOTSTRAP`, the stages that rebuild production from the bare land (1: three drills, a smelter, two poles → plates and glass; 2: a drill, a wire mill, two poles → copper wire, its plates and glass waited for from stage 1). `reserveNeed` is what the stages cost from the Heart's stock: 59 plates, 6 glass and 4 copper wire at founding, 49 plates once the smelter's plan is taken. `reserveProblems` (in `validateContent`) checks that each stage makes what it says from the cirque's deposits through plans alone, that the reserve keeps only what the bootstrap makes, that every plan and the starting kit are paid in it, that the starting stock covers the reserve with the whole first ring of plans bought, and that the Heart's core is above 0 kW. Engine [systems/reserve.ts](../src/sim/systems/reserve.ts): for each reserved item, the invariant stock + the cost of every building standing ≥ the reserve. `planBlock` → `reserve` (after `unaffordable`). Placing and removing cannot break it (full refunds), and production, rewards and deliveries only raise it. No command, nothing saved or hashed, nothing for the catch-up. UI: « Réserve de relance » in the tree's header, a held plan reads `poor` with its shortfall, CrierBelt's toast. Tests: [reserve.test.ts](../src/sim/systems/reserve.test.ts) (a base holding exactly the reserve rebuilds on the real cirque for 24 players, by day and by night; a fuzz of 24 × 150 random actions, spending and building players, export streams included, keeps the invariant at every step, rebuilds from its tightest and its last state, and replays). Export streams from the Heart are safe by a content check (what they leave plus the post's own cost covers the reserve). Not guarded yet (hooks ready, keys `reason.reserve`, `trade.refused.reserve`): spot sales from the Heart's stock (reachable through the trade post's plan), Heart levels, remote ribbon surcharges ([engine.md](engine.md#crierbelts-restart-reserve-d36)) |
| D37 | **Items move whole on ribbons** (owner, 2026-10-12: "the items should be actually simulated while the game is opened… They enter the heart and do not update the count, it's decorrelated") | The flow model moved milli-items: an item seeped into the Heart over a second, so its +1 showed at a moment unrelated to the item seen arriving; and a merge of different items interleaved thin slices that left the head one per tick, choking mixed lines to a third of their throughput (found by the items-view work). Whole items fix both and keep the one engine for online and offline (the catch-up still extrapolates this very step) | `Chain.offer` / `split` / `take`: a head item leaves whole, all or nothing, once all of it has arrived (across runs of that item); `inCredit` / `outCredit` carry the ribbon's throughput (saved in `serialize.ts`, hashed, copied on recompile). Routing blocks deal out per item (`ForkNode.turn`). The HUD writes the stock counts every frame (`Hud.counts`). Conduits stay continuous |
| D38 | **Sand is the ground: a sand collector, not a drill** (owner, 2026-10-12: "the sand could be recolted with something else, not a drill, and should be obtainable anywhere. It's just sand, it's on the ground. Think it through") | The cirque floor and most of the desert are sand tiles already; a "sand deposit" to drill made no sense, and tied glass to one spot of the map. Sand anywhere moves the limit to space and power, which is the game's theme, and frees the glass line's layout | `sand_collector` (kind `drill`, `drill.ground: 'sand'`, 15/min, 30 kW, 5 plates + 2 copper wire — no glass, so glass can always be rebuilt from nothing), anywhere on bare sand (`needs_sand`); no sand deposit in the cirque or the outer world (`COMMON_DEPOSITS`, mapgen); a plan after the smelter; the glassworks quest places two collectors by the glass smelter; the restart reserve's stage 1 is 2 drills + a collector (D36; `BAL.tradeHearthReserve` 20 → 25 so export streams still cannot reach it) |
| D39 | **Direct insertion for everyone** (owner, 2026-10-12: "two machines who are next to each other should be able to directly send the items to each other if they're connected well, no need for ribbons") | It was the Hive City keystone (dnK); a player who lines up an output against an input expects it to work | compile.ts adds an edge from an out-port to the in-port it is set against, always (machines, drills, stores, the Heart); dnK keeps crafting +25 % and a research slot |
| D40 | **The cirque is a third of its first area** (owner, 2026-10-12: "reduce the size of the cirque, the tutorial's area. Let's divide the total area by about 3") | The tutorial was played in a basin of r 40 under a Crown out to r 56: long lines, long walks, much empty floor. Radii × 0.58 divide the area by 3. The wires did not shrink (the Heart 8 tiles, a pole 6), so the tutorial's distances, not the scale, place the deposits: the coal must stay beyond the Heart's wire (quest 3 lays a pole to it) and its drills inside the Heart's first zone | `CIRQUE`: floor 40 → 23, Crown 40–56 → 23–32 (H1 to 28, H3 from 37; Closed Cirque 74 → 43, Wide Steppe 50 → 29), rim jitter ±3 → ±2, lake r 6 → 3.5 at (14, 8); iron north (0, −10); coal due west (−16, 1) as a 5×5 block (radius 3.1 → 2.9), 9 tiles or more past the Heart's footprint for every player; the tired vein (4, 15), a pole away, unchanged in size (6.75 ore/min); second iron (−12, −12), astride the first zone's edge. `BAL.hearthRadius` [24, 30, 36, 42] → [18, 21, 23, 26] (level 1 holds the coal's and the copper's drills, so it shrank less; level 4 is still the whole floor); `startReveal` 64 → 44; `digReach` stays 18 (D18 holds: the Crown is 5 tiles past the first zone). Quest 11's « Passe-Cols » follows `crownOuter` (56 → 32). A lease the purse cannot pay lapses: its buildings go dormant, nothing lost. Engine: `producerOf` (network.ts) builds a building's `Producer` for the compile and the inspector alike (an unwired producer shows its rate, now 0); `windSite(grid, e)` gives the local wind ‰ and its reason: `BAL.windHeight` [1 400, 1 600, 1 800, 2 000] by level 1–4+ (`windRock` by hardness retired), else the cirque (250), the plain (1 000) or the mesa (1 600). The building card lists the height factors. No save change (the land is not saved; the factor is compiled). Hooks: `[data-power-card]`, `[data-pw=formula|draw|charge|wires]`, `.pw-coef`, `.pw-why`, `[data-pw-viz=wind|sun]`; smoke step « inspect: the grid module ». Tests: mountains.test.ts (by height) |

A skybridge is not checked (it leaps to the next one on its level, whatever lies between); a ribbon with its back or its side to a cliff stays allowed (it is fed from its sides). Lines laid before stay as they are (the compile still ends them at the cliff). Telling it: a drag lays what fits and leaves the step tiles out, and the toast counts them with the first reason (`ToolResult.left`, `toast.left`: « 4 tuiles laissées — Une marche : seul un ruban à traction… »); the ghost draws a `needs_lift` piece as the traction ribbon that would take it, over the coral footprint; the tip adds the traction ribbon's icon and its digit in the tray (T, the tree, while it is still to learn). Tests: levels.test.ts **Amended by D114:** a conduit's water may pour down one terrace (its tile ahead one level lower), never up and never two at once — `cliff` refuses only those. |

Si un poteau a une tolerance de +-1 de hauteur, alors il ne peut pas y avoir plus d'un de hauteur entre deux poteaux. So two poles on the floor cross a one-terrace ridge, not a two-terrace one; two pylons on the floor clear three terraces, never a mountain; a pylon wires to another three terraces up, and a pole hangs on a pylon three terraces up. `powerLine` and `pylonLine` (dev.ts) plan their hops with the same two functions, so the debug bases' lines go round mountains. | D92 | **The renderer holds up on big bases: a structural change re-draws only the entities it touched, as a full rebuild would draw them; buildings off screen are culled; the big static layers re-batch apart** (lead's perf share, 2026-10-15; owner: « trouve des choses à améliorer, et fais-le ») | Measured first ([scripts/render-bench.mjs](../scripts/render-bench.mjs), headless Chrome): the late base ran at 60 fps everywhere (≈ 2 ms a frame), but on base20k (the stress colony, 25 201 entities) placing a ribbon cost a 440 ms frame — 40 000 sprites torn down one by one, each `removeChild` searching its layer — the render call took 3–3.8 ms a frame by day (the shadows' container alpha, set every frame, re-walked and re-packed 13 000 shadows), the items view walked every ribbon cell of the base each frame, and the wires re-tessellated thousands of strokes on every recompile. The late base's week of catch-up took 26.5 s of CPU (the docs said 16 s and 0.65 s) | **buildings-view.ts**: a record per entity with what it was drawn from (`sigOf`), dropped and laid again only when that changes (`rebuild`; `rebuildAll` from scratch); ties among equal depth keys by rank (`tie`: just under the key, the id then the laying order), so an update draws what a full rebuild draws; many sprites leave a layer in one pass (`removeMany`); 16-tile squares culled with Pixi's `culled` flag when the view's range of them changes (`cull`), moving parts off screen swap no texture; the shadows' alpha set when its 8-bit value changes. The floor ribbons, the shadows and `plan` are render groups. **cell-flow.ts**: lines whose box lies 8 tiles or more from the view are not read. **lights-view.ts** likewise, building by building, its glows sorted by rank (added light rounds differently in another order). **wires-view.ts**: no redraw when the wires are drawn as before (`sameWires`). **Engine, bit-exact**: `Chain.steps` kept for its tick, `StateHasher.int` without a division (same hash, report and save on five catch-ups). Numbers: base20k 5.5 → 1.1 ms a frame (iso near), 6.6 → 3.3 (plan mid), a structural change 440 → 70 ms; the late base's change 16 → 5–12 ms; its week of catch-up 26.5 → 23.5 s ([render.md](render.md#frame-costs-on-big-bases-d92), [offline.md](offline.md#performance)). Pictures: [scripts/render-shots.mjs](../scripts/render-shots.mjs) pixel for pixel against HEAD (identical, but for the few spots where two runs of the same code differ too: an unlit picture baked on a real-time budget, an item by the tarn's pump), an update against a full rebuild (always identical); smoke step 25 sprite by sprite; cell-flow.test (a window draws what the whole base draws in it). Left: the graph's compile (~25 ms on base20k), the late base's approximate regimes re-stepped at each end (half its catch-up: changing that changes the approximate results, the owner's call) |
| D93 | **Expertises: earned by what the base did, two held at a time, the only percentages of the game; the tree's arc runs Tier 1 whole → the Core's level 3 and its Archive → two expertises → Tier 2** (owner, choosing among proposals, 2026-10-16: « Les expertises, c'est très bien. On va commencer par ça »; the arc and the rules agreed on 2026-10-14, roadmap's design backlog) | The tree unlocks what can be built (D70) and costs the same whatever the claim owns (D72): nothing rewarded a claim for what it had done well, and nothing made two claims differ but their buildings. The Core's level 3 came with Land Registry (`tr4`, a Tier 2 node) and its corner piece, « l'Archive des expertises » (D86), was a placeholder; Tier 2 opened with Mechanics (`tr1`), the first Tier 1 node | **The arc**: a new Tier 1 trunk node, the **Synthesis** (`tr_synth`, `TIER1_WHOLE`: 60 Survey Data, 30 000 tokens, 15 min) needs every other Tier 1 node and gives the Core's level 3 (paid in Tier 1's parts: 150 gears, 60 circuits, 30 oscillators; ceramic was Tier 2's), which mounts the Archive; `TIER_GATE[2]` is the Synthesis and Tier 2 also waits for two expertises chosen (`researchBlock` → `expertise`; the tree's Tier 2 head says « et deux expertises »). Mechanics keeps the press and the Core's level 2; Land Registry keeps leases and pylons. validate.ts checks the Synthesis needs all of Tier 1. **Content** ([expertises.ts](../src/content/expertises.ts)): 12 expertises, one production and one research per family, each with a feat of its own — Energy: Steady Grid (the Core's grid at 95 % for 2 h / 24 h / 7 days in a row → generators +10/15/20 %), Power Engineering (kW standing → Energy research data −15/25/35 %); Extraction: Ore Cutting (raw matter extracted → drills and cutters, their rate and their deposit's cap), Geology (tiles dug or filled → research time); Machines: Fabrication (materials and parts made → machines' pace), Methods (machines standing → research time); Commerce: Procurement (◉ spent at the Crier → deliveries' delay −15/25/35 %), Trading (◉ of sales → research tokens); Analytics: Archiving (data made → data makers +10/15/20 %: the Core's outlet, the collectors, the autonomous labs), Inference (tokens used → research tokens); Expansion: Estate (parcels bought → land price, and so rent, −10/15/20 %), Haulage (ribbon tiles → research data). Ranks I–III by thresholds measured on the debug bases (Tier 1's own 530 data and 280 000 tokens earn Archiving and Inference I by themselves); `BAL.expertise`: the Archive's level 3, 2 slots, a réorientation 1 500 ◉ then ×2 each time, 24 h without, 950 ‰ for the grid. **Engine** ([systems/expertise.ts](../src/sim/systems/expertise.ts)): `expertiseEpoch` at every minute's boundary, online after the tokens' bill and in the catch-up at the same place — the feats read off what the colony keeps (`produced`, the Crier's lifetime `earned` / `spent`, `tokenStats.totalUsed`, `defCounts`, `land.bought`, `terrainDiff`) plus one counter of its own, the Core's grid's run of minutes at 95 % at a minute's end (online `ctx.sat`, offline the segment's grid mode); ranks rise, never fall, announced (`expertise` event) from the Archive on; a slot comes into place at the boundary its 24 h end (`on`). The held expertises enter `computeMods(done, held)`: `mod` targets ×‰ (`genOut`, new `drillRate` and `dataRate`, `craftSpeed`, `deliveryDelay`, `landPrice`) and `fam[family]` {time, tokens, data} ‰ that `effectiveCost(id, mods)` takes off a node of that family when its project starts (a running one keeps its cost; the trunk is never touched). A change of the Mods at a boundary rebuilds the graph there, and the catch-up materializes and recompiles as for a lease lapsing. Commands `expertise` (choose into an empty slot, free, at once) and `reorient` (paid, the old one gone at once, the new one 24 h later), refusals `locked` `unearned` `held` `full` `empty` `funds` `slot` `unknown`. Saved `expertise` (ranks, slots, réorientations, the grid's run and best; FIELDS `plain`), hashed; **save v11** (`expertiseArc`): a claim holding a node of Tier 2 or beyond gets the Synthesis, and `tierTwoOpen` counts such a node as open, so no older claim loses its Tier 2. **UI**: the Expertise window ([screens/expertise.ts](../src/ui/screens/expertise.ts), E, the HUD's chip with the two slots' pictures and a badge of those to choose, the Core's inspector, a click on the Archive in either view: `Picker.heartPieceAt`) — the two slots as big cards (picture, rank pips, the effect as « −15 % » beside what it improves, « En place » or the 24 h bar), the twelve by family with their feat as a meter towards the next rank, « Choisir » or « Réorienter · 1 500 ◉ » which arms the slots' « Remplacer »; a notice when a rank is earned or a slot comes into place. **Debug bases**: the late base chooses two earned ones by commands, in an order of preference (Estate, Methods, Power Engineering, Haulage…: none changes what its lines carry, so D85's checks are unchanged). Tests: [expertise.test.ts](../src/sim/systems/expertise.test.ts) (the arc, feats and ranks, choose and reorient, effects, saves and replay, online ≈ offline bit for bit with a rank rising and a slot coming into place during the absence); the smoke's expertise step; [scripts/expertise-shots.mjs](../scripts/expertise-shots.mjs) **Amended by D118**: Tier 2 waits for the Core's level 3 itself (`researchBlock` → `core`, before `expertise`), and the tree says so on its band. |
| D94 | **Space at stake: map 4 puts obstacles past the Crown — massifs in sheer cliffs, chasms, rivers — and a parcel's price and its gauge follow its usable floor** (owner, 2026-10-16: « il faut que la place soit parfois un enjeu critique, donc pour ça on va rajouter des obstacles. Montagnes énormes avec falaises, précipices, cours d'eau... »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable in content) | Past the Crown the land was open: the mountains climbed a level a step from every side (D45: a two-level step only far out, facing east), water was a lake to fill, and the parcels just past the starting square held nearly all floor (median 897–996 ‰ of usable floor on map 3). Space was never short, so where to build was never a choice | **Map 4** (a new `MAPS` entry, `MAP_VERSION` 4; maps 1–3 pinned tile for tile in mapgen.test.ts): map 3's cirque, Crown, tarn, quartz vein, sites and vents unchanged (tested: the floor and the Crown tile for tile). Content `OBSTACLES` ([world.ts](../src/content/world.ts)): **massifs**, an elevation of their own (`massif`: scale 48, faded in from r 37 to 45) that stands at level 3 at once past its threshold — a sheer cliff — and a level more every 8 above, to 5; their tiles never settle (`settle4`, half levels: a massif's cliff never comes down) but in their **ramps**, where a second noise passes its threshold, which come down half a level a tile (terraces two deep: an elevator and its flat tile, D65); a lone cliff tile with fewer than two massif tiles beside it settles as the mountains do (no chimneys). Over 120 tiles round the cirque: 1 000–1 450 steps of two levels or more, 200+ of three; 80 %+ of the high ground still reached by single steps. **Chasms** (`T.Chasm`, 13: « précipice ») and **rivers** (`T.River`, 14), appended to `T`, at level 0: traced once in the constructor from the seeds ([world/obstacles.ts](../src/sim/world/obstacles.ts): fixed-point walks on the integer trig table, segments bucketed by chunk, an exact integer distance test). 20 chasms, 12 of them in the rings just past the starting square (r 38–62): **crevasses** (owner, 2026-10-16: « Le précipice a plus l'air d'une marée de pétrole que d'un trou… Il faut que ça ressemble à une crevasse »), long narrow walks over broken ground (roughness ≥ 120), 1–3 tiles wide, pinching and widening along their length (`pinch`: the shared noise along the walk; a wider spot here and there wants Long Bridges or a detour), side cracks off them (`branch`: 140 ‰ a point, 3–5 steps, narrowing), never over their own course nor into another; **the rivers a drainage network** (owner, 2026-10-16: « les rivières doivent avoir du sens : être de petits ruisseaux qui partent de grandes montagnes au début, puis se rejoindre pour devenir un grand courant en bas… une mer, loin quelque part »; `OBSTACLES.drainage`, `drainage`): a lattice a node every 6 tiles out to r 420 (moved ±2 by a hash), each node's elevation the land's height ×4 + its massif's raw level ×80 + its mountains' ×30 + 3 a tile of distance to the sea's coast; a flood from the sea in (an integer binary heap on spill and order: a total order) gives each node the neighbour it drains to, through the hollows too, so all of it drains to the sea; rain (1 on the plains, 3 on raised land, 14 on a massif: its springs and snowmelt) gathers downstream, and a stream runs where 300 has gathered — so the streams begin up on the massifs — its half width by its flow (2 quarter tiles at 300, 15 past 11 000: a tile or two up there, a broad current of 5–8 tiles in the lowlands, ×1.6 at its mouth), meandering by up to 3 tiles on the floor; at the bottom of a hollow 60 deep or more a lake (4–9 tiles across). **Water never climbs**: up on a massif (not its ramps) a stream's water stands at the massif's level, its level profiled a tile along each stretch and never rising, the node it reaches no higher than the water arriving (a step down is a fall); off the massif it runs on the floor at 0; where streams overlap, the lower level (`lowestRiver`); such a tile is `T.River` at its level, fixed (it never settles). The network keeps 12 tiles off the Crown, the clearings and the vein; a river across a corridor is a ford (still shallows). **The sea** (`OBSTACLES.sea`, the shared world's): past a coast 360 tiles out along a bearing the world seed picks (±28 by the world's noise along it), shallows 4 tiles out then deep water a little bluer than a lake's, never over a unique site, a corridor crossing it on a causeway; surf lines along its coast. Measured (lands 1–3, 1000, 8, within r 400): 24 000–28 000 river tiles, 300–540 of them up on massifs, 28–80 falls, no water climbing along its way, 32–65 river tiles flowing into the sea; a network costs ≈ 40 ms once per land (kept for the next MapGen of the same seeds: `NETS`), a chunk ≈ 2.5× map 3's. A **corridor** from the Crown's foot to each guaranteed site (the copper field, the unique sites, the vents) keeps the massifs, chasms, rivers and the water off: a way there a level a step, never across a gap or a cliff (tested on 17 lands). Round the copper field the obstacles keep 26 tiles off (the first works past the Crown: the late base's Fabrique). **Space or ore** (owner, 2026-10-16: « Dans les grandes étendues de désert, il ne devrait pas y avoir beaucoup de minéraux, mais au moins il y a de la place. Alors que sur les parcelles où il y a des minéraux, il faut qu'il y ait peu de place »): a slow **roughness** field (`OBSTACLES.rugged`: scale 72, the world's noise near, the player's far) lifts the massifs' elevation by 44/64 and the mountains' by 36/64 a unit off 128 and sinks them below — stretches of open desert, and broken ground; the chasms keep to roughness ≥ 120. The common deposits (`OBSTACLES.ore`, in place of one blob a chunk) are 4 candidates a chunk, each read for how broken the ground round it is (of 8 points at 6 tiles and 8 at 11, how many stand raised, in a gap or in water, each counting two) and taken at 15 ‰ + 32 ‰ a broken count (at most 800 ‰; a vein hidden in rock at the same chance): open ground gets a thin blob now and then (1.5–2.2 tiles), broken ground full ones (2.5–4.5) 25 % richer, against the walls, in the valleys, under the terraces. Measured on the four rings past the starting square (112 parcels): ore tiles against usable floor correlate at r −0.47 to −0.63 on lands 1–5 (map 3: −0.09 to +0.14), 34–38 open desert parcels (usable floor ≥ 75 %, 6 ore tiles at most) and 28–35 rich cramped ones (25+ ore tiles, ≤ 55 %) a land, a few exceptions both rich and roomy (mapgen.test.ts checks lands 1–3: r < −0.4, map 3 |r| < 0.3). The copper field is one by design: within 26 tiles the land is never rougher than middling (the first works past the Crown, the late base's Fabrique). The rings past the starting square: median 706–875 ‰ of usable floor before the roughness, about 560 ‰ over four rings after. **Rules**: nothing stands in a chasm or a river (`canPlace` refuses `chasm` / `river` by name, « Un précipice : … »), nothing fills them (only still water and salt flats are marked), a ribbon drag leaps them with a skybridge pair (`LEAPABLE`), a wire passes over (level 0), a pump on a river's bank draws from it (`isWater` holds rivers; `isStillWater` the fillable kind). **Usable floor** ([world/floor.ts](../src/sim/world/floor.ts)): a floor tile where a 2 × 2 machine fits at one level; a parcel's price pays `tile.floor` (60) for it, `tile.sliver` (25, as rock) for a sliver, `river` 20, `chasm` 0 (`landContent`), and its ore at `depositPerValue` **250** (was 400): an open desert parcel ≈ 505–517 ◉, a rich cramped one ≈ 540–630 ◉ (medians, lands 1–5), both worth buying for what they give; the vein's parcel 469–504 ◉ on map 4. The land panel shows it as a gauge and a share (« Sol constructible ») and its ore as three pips and a count (« Minerai », `parcelOre`: tiers at 1, 13, 40 deposit tiles, `BAL.land.oreTiers`, in the colour of the ore it holds most); the Terrain view (I) as a pie at each parcel's middle, amber under a third, with the three pips beside it: « beaucoup de place, peu de minerai » or the reverse at a glance. **Look**: the mountains redrawn as Moebius mesas (owner: « les montagnes ne sont pas très belles, il faut faire mieux »; art/rock.ts, relief.ts, relief-view.ts, terrain.ts pass 4 — the Crown too): strata that run from tile to tile by absolute height (`BEDS`), one warm family of ochres, terracottas, siennas and a plum heartrock deepening with height (hardness read in the beds' texture too: thin beds, joints, fractures, facets and veins), caprock lips over shadowed overhangs bitten and rounded within their tile, notches and gullies, varnish streaks, grooves, talus at the foot, textured tops (sand pockets, slabs, cracks, a boulder), and in plan a hillshaded relief map with strata like contours; a raised stream a column topped by running water, its fall a sheet of streaks down the wall the camera sees, foam at the lip and spray at the foot (the relief's atlas grows about a third more frames, 7 → 9 pages over a mid-zoom sweep); a river's width shows its flow, a fall marked by foam across it and spray below in plan (the relief draws a raised stream on its column and its fall down the wall), the sea's coast in surf; a chasm a **crevasse** (art/gaps.ts `paintCrevasse`, one picture for both views, cached with the chunks): broken rims (outline points moved by a hash of their world position, the lips' ink cut and heavier on the near lip), the far wall (the side the iso camera looks into) lit in the Crown's ochres at its lip and darker band by band down the slope — the walls converging —, strata lines along it, cracks down the face, a pebble on a ledge, hatching down the slope in the shade (grooves in iso, a drop in plan, never a flat blot), a haze in the depth, only a thin near-black strip under the near lip; loose stones and side cracks on the floor beside it; a river a bluer running water with strokes along its way (the biome code `BIOME.river` + its eighth of a turn) and, at near zooms, dashes drifting downstream ([rivers-view.ts](../src/render/world/rivers-view.ts)). The wind field (D95) reads the land as generated: chasms and rivers are open ground. The late debug base's turbines are wired or moved (D95's `windyWired`): the massif tops past a sheer cliff were out of a wire's reach **River joins (2026-10-17; owner: « les rivières sont un peu cassées des fois, à la frontière entre eau bleue claire et foncée »):** all water is now one outline (art/gaps.ts `paintWater`: lakes, sea, tarn and rivers marched together, a river's own tiles kept in, `march`'s `keep` given per tile so the field is the same both sides of a chunk seam), filled with the shallows, then the deep heart, the sea's deep and the rivers' blue feathered over it in seven bands clipped to it (`FEATHER`): no seam, no sand between a river and its lake, no rim inside the water, no surf at a river's mouth, one inked shore. Before: two outlines with a hard seam and slivers of ground between, and a chunk whose window held a river traced its lake's shore differently from its neighbour. Test: water-seam.test.ts; shots: `node scripts/river-seam-shots.mjs`. **Open desert (2026-10-17; owner: « la carte devrait contenir un petit peu plus de grands déserts ouverts, plats, assez vides de ressources, sans eau ni crevasse ni montagne »; « le cirque notamment devrait être entouré de pas mal de sable, hormis le quartz et les rares ressources aux alentours »):** content `OBSTACLES.desert` {sand 84, rough 116, low 40, below 112, edge 40, drop 3, riseEdge 200, rise 12, ore 500, sandOre 60}. Round the Crown the roughness is held at 40 out to 84 tiles (the two rings of parcels round the starting square) and rises to the land's own by 116, so the broken ground begins past them; wherever the roughness is under 112 lies open desert: no massif or mountain (their elevation drops 40 + 3 a unit under it), no pond (the plains' low ground is sand), no chasm (they keep to roughness 120 and start past 84: `chasm.r` [84, 150], `rNear` [84, 30]), the drainage's nodes raised 200 + 12 a unit so the streams go round (crossing where they must; none within 116 of the centre), the deposits at half their chance; within 84 rare thin ones (60 ‰ a candidate). Kept: the quartz bluff (D87), the spring's stream (D115), the copper field and every corridor, the neighbouring bases' rings (18 bases on land 1, 17 before), the drainage from the massifs to the sea (its tests unchanged). Measured on lands 1–3, a parcel open when ≥ 90 % of it is flat dry floor at level 0 with ≤ 8 ore tiles: rings 1–6 69/504 (14 %) → 184/504 (37 %); rings 2–3 22/120 (18 %) → 101/120 (84 %; 95 % of their tiles flat dry sand, what is left three ring-3 corners where a massif rises at r ≈ 100, the quartz bluff and a few small deposits); rings 4–6 47/360 (13 %) → 83/360 (23 %); the largest group of open parcels side by side 6–15 → 41–57. The debug bases build as before, faster (late 1.9–3.2 s of CPU on lands 1–3). Tests: mapgen.test.ts « open sand round the cirque, the land cut up past it » (in place of « the rings … cut up »: rings 2–3 a median usable floor ≥ 950 ‰ and ≥ map 3's, rings 4–6 < 900 ‰, a quarter < 720 ‰, less than map 3), « open desert » (rings 2–3 ≥ 75 % open, rings 4–6 ≥ 15 %, a group of four or more, no water or chasm within 84), « space or ore » over rings 2–6. Shots: `node scripts/desert-shots.mjs` (`TAG=before|after`). |
| D95 | **The wind is simulated: a turbine catches the weather's wind × its place's exposure, read off the land through its own curve; the player sees the wind only through rare hints and what a turbine gives, until the Anémométrie plan opens the Vent view** (owner, 2026-10-16: « les éoliennes, on va retirer le coefficient actuel pour le remplacer par réellement une simulation du vent. Par défaut, le joueur ne saura pas où il y a du vent hormis quelques indices visuels rares (bourrasque, sable...)… avec quelques avancées en télémétrie, il pourra accéder à une vision du vent… il y a plus de vent au sommet des collines, ou dans les grands espaces sans obstacles ») — *the ratings since: D116, D132, D143 (a wind turbine 100 kW, the pitch-control 140 kW)* | A turbine gave its rating × a fixed factor by kind of ground (the cirque ×0.25, plains ×1, mesas ×1.6, ×1.4–2 by height: D44, D76) × a gust level: a table to read, not a place to find. The owner wants the wind found: plant a turbine, learn the spot is poor, then read the wind once telemetry allows. Defaults decided by the session (the owner: « décide, et continue ») | **Engine** ([world/wind.ts](../src/sim/world/wind.ts), pure, integer, never saved; `BAL.wind`). *The time*: `windAt` = the day's curve (the Sough rises at dusk, blows through the night, lulls in the late morning: it complements the sun) × the day's mood (650–1 350 ‰ a world day from WORLD_SEED, blended day to day) × the gust window (unchanged), rounded to 50 ‰. *The bearing*: one prevailing for the world (WORLD_SEED), ±4 steps of 64 on 20 % of days each. *The place*: a tile's exposure from the land **as generated** (the `TerrainSource` refilled into the field's cache: a dig never moves the wind, the field is the same after a reload, and works on every map — D94's massifs included): height above its 13×13 box's mean (+340 ‰ a level above, −120 below), +40 a level, the upwind fan's clear share over 32 tiles (+380), the bowl's share of eight bearings meeting higher ground within 32 (−480), a pass along the wind (+300; its walls then shelter nothing), × (1 − shelter), the shelter 1.8 × the steepest higher ground upwind in levels per tile (≤ 850 ‰) — Winstral's index. **Buildings shelter nothing** (an output never changes because a factory went up). Cached per chunk and bearing, read on demand (0.7 ms a chunk). A turbine: its footprint's mean, its foot at its real level. **Turbines** (`gen.wind`): nothing below cut-in, the cube of the wind to rated, a storm stop: Éolienne 360 kW (×0.4 → ×1.4, stops at ×3.6), à pas variable 480 kW (×0.3 → ×1.25, never stops), Grande 1 200 kW (×0.35 → ×1.3, stops at ×4.2). A basic turbine over a week: ≈ 305 kW on the Crown's crest (216 before), 130–190 on open plains (120), ≈ 13 at the cirque floor's median spot and 0 in a lee (30). A rule change for every base, no save field. **Offline**: `weatherKey` = sun:wind:veer; online ≈ offline test. base20k's week costs ≈ 6 more warm-ups a cell (its turbines brown its cells out on calm epochs): 1.3 → 2.0 s of CPU measured alone. **Hidden**: before the plan no figure about the wind — rare hints ([wind-hints-view.ts](../src/render/world/wind-hints-view.ts): ink gust streaks and sand puffs off the tops, downwind, the chance growing with the square of the local wind), a turbine's output (« Ses pales le disent : 8 % »), its blades spinning with the wind (barely below their cut-in); the clouds drift with the prevailing wind. **Anémométrie** (an analytics plan after the power log, flag `anemometry`, `ANALYTICS.wind`): the **Vent** view (V; [views/wind-panel.ts](../src/ui/views/wind-panel.ts), views-view.ts): the land washed by exposure, ink arrows downwind, the turbines' badges; its card: a compass, the next 24 h, the turbines least windy first; the inspector's wind reasons, curve and next 24 h; a turbine in hand shows what it would give over a day. No anemometer building (the plan is enough). **Tutorial**: « Le Souffle »'s arrow points at the Crown's windiest free spot (`{ windy: true }`), its hint at the crests. **Debug bases**: turbines on the windiest spots a wire reaches (a spot no line reaches — a plateau behind a cliff on map 4 — is taken down and the next tried: `windyWired`), sized so the grid holds through the next two days' known weather. **On the first draft** (owner, 2026-10-16: the sand « ne devrait pas être sur les montagnes, c'est pour le désert. Et il y en a trop », « les traits sont trop gros »): the hints redrawn as pen work — hairline ink flicks of a gust (1.1 screen px at any zoom, in uneven groups of 2–3, tapered), low streaks of sand with small ripples on open desert floor only — and made rare: about a try a second per 1 000 visible tiles, from a local wind of 1 500 ‰, high ground at a third and gusts only, four up at most. **The Électricité view in groups** (owner, 2026-10-16: « the grid view of electricity should differentiate in color between what produces electricity, what consumes it, what isn't linked, and what doesn't need electricity »; D88's spirit): the wash by what a building is to power — produces gold, stores turquoise (the accumulators: a fifth tone, they give and take), consumes slate blue, not linked coral (no wire or asleep: the trouble's colour), needs no power grey —, the grids keeping their colour on the wires and the pillars' rings; the legend and a row of chips in the card follow the groups (`PowerMark.group`, `needsNoPower`, `idleCount` in readouts/power.ts; `POWER_GROUP_COLOR`). Tests: [wind.test.ts](../src/sim/world/wind.test.ts), mountains.test.ts, links.test.ts (a sheltered turbine), moving-parts.test.ts, views-reveal.test.ts, readouts.test.ts (the groups); smoke: the Vent view (V), the grid card's wind; shots: `scripts/wind-shots.mjs` |
| D96 | **A responsive interface: from a 1920 px desktop down to a 360 px phone, the same game by mouse, keyboard or finger; the side panels become bottom sheets below 900 px, the HUD folds, the screens go full screen, touch pans, pinches, taps and long-presses** (owner, 2026-10-16: « il faut rendre l'UI responsive »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | The interface was drawn for a desktop window: below about 1 000 px the HUD's right cluster ran off the window, the panels sat over the HUD at a fixed `top: 76px`, the build tray stood taller than a phone held sideways, the tech tree and the Crier squeezed their main column to nothing; the canvas took no finger at all (the roadmap had put touch « after v1 ») | **Targets**: 1920–1280 comfortable, 1280–1024 everything fits, tablets 768–1024 both ways playable, phones from 360 wide readable and basically playable, heights down to about 600 (a phone on its side, 390). **Breakpoints** in one stylesheet, [responsive.css](../src/ui/styles/responsive.css) (repeated as numbers in [ui/fit.ts](../src/ui/fit.ts)): narrow < 900 px (sheets, the folded HUD, scrolling build bar, screens in one column), phone < 560 px (the HUD on three rows), short < 540 px tall (lower tray and sheets), touch = `pointer: coarse` (hit targets 46 px, no key caps or keyboard hints). **A fit factor**: the zoom of `#ui` and `#screens` is the UI scale × `uiFit` (1 at 1280 × 720 and more; less by the short side, down to 0.84 — 0.88 on touch, so a 46 px target stays ≥ 40 px), `--ui-zoom`, re-applied on every resize ([app/layout.ts](../src/app/layout.ts)); Chrome zooms vw / vh too, so inside them sizes go by % or the layout's variables (`--win-w/h`). **The panels follow the HUD**: `app/layout.ts` measures where the HUD ends (`--hud-b`, its right cluster `--hud-rb`) and how tall the open build bar stands (`--bar-h`); on a wide window the inspector, the views' card and the land panel stand under the right cluster and above the bar, `clamp()`-sized; toasts and the quest log under the HUD (the log's own JS placement, which measured a box the narrow HUD dissolves, gave way to the CSS; on a narrow window it steps aside while a sheet is up). **The Heart's stock behind a button** (owner, 2026-10-16: « n'affiche plus cette barre de ressource en haut à gauche par défaut, il faut que le joueur clique sur un bouton Stock pour le voir »): the bar of five stocks and its « +n » drawer gave way to « Stock » (an inked crate and the word, `[data-hud-stock]`); a click opens the Stock panel ([ui/stock-panel.ts](../src/ui/stock-panel.ts)): every item the Heart holds in the kit's sections by kind (raw, materials, parts, data, rare finds), its amount and, with the telemetry, its intake a minute; closed by the button, Esc (first in the chain) or a click anywhere else; the viewer's last choice kept in this browser (`filon.stock.v1`, a convenience only); under the HUD on the left (the quest log steps aside), a bottom sheet on a narrow window. What must stay in sight without opening it is a **badge** ([ui/stock-facts.ts](../src/ui/stock-facts.ts)), on a rule drawn from what the Heart pays for (D33, D34: buildings and plans): an item **ran out** when the Heart holds less than one and an unlocked building or an open plan costs it (coral), **short** when it holds less than an open plan costs (peach; prerequisites met, not taken; the reserve counted, D36); the panel tints the row and gives the shortfall, « −12 », its need in the tooltip. Money, energy and time stay on the HUD. The telemetry's reveal lifts the button; quest 2's last step points at it (`{ k: 'stock' }`). **Narrow**: the panels are **bottom sheets** ([ui/sheet.ts](../src/ui/sheet.ts), the shell gives each a grip): half-way open, folded to their header by a tap, pulled up to the HUD by a drag (a flick goes on), never over the HUD nor the bar; a phone on its side docks them right. The **HUD** dissolves its two groups into one wrapping row: the Stock button, the purse, the power and the time as icon + figure (rates, temperature, the power bar in the tooltips, the Stock panel and the views), the research row behind one chip (the tree's mark, its badge, the first project's ring) and the view selector behind a layers button, each dropping down under the HUD; a tap elsewhere closes them. The **build bar**'s tabs and tray each one row that scrolls sideways. The **screens** full screen with ✕ in the corner, their tabs scrolling; the tech tree's detail under the tree; the Crier one column (prices first, then the orbit and the Gazette); the statistics' ledger as figures on a phone. **Safe areas** (`viewport-fit=cover`, `--safe-*` in the zoomed px) on every edge. **Touch** ([input/touch.ts](../src/input/touch.ts), pure and tested): one finger pans or taps (select, the Link tool, the land mode), a building in hand is placed by a tap (a drag pans: no building laid by a finger that meant to pan), a ribbon, a box or the brush drag as the mouse does; a still half-second is the right button (a tick where the device vibrates); two fingers pinch-zoom about their middle and drop what one had started (`Tools.dropDrag`); the interface never zooms the page (`touch-action`); « Tourner » in the build bar is R for a finger, and the tutorial's arrow points at the research fold when the tree's chip is folded. Mouse and keyboard unchanged. **The canvas** follows a resize, an orientation change or the visual viewport on the next frame; the camera's centre is in tiles, so nothing stretches and the middle stays. The tip stays inside the window, above a finger (`tipPlace`). **Checked**: [scripts/responsive.mjs](../scripts/responsive.mjs) at 1920 × 1080, 1440 × 900, 1280 × 720, 1024 × 768, 768 × 1024, 390 × 844, 844 × 390 on the late base (the HUD, the inspector, the build bar, a view, the land panel, the tree, the statistics, the Crier, the menu: inside the window, no overlap, no sideways scroll, ✕ in reach, 40 px targets on touch; the folds; real touch events at 390 × 844); a smoke step at 390 × 844. Still to do: the tech tree's pinch, the building card on a long press, landscape tablets' sheets beside the world |

| D98 | **A routing block dropped on a ribbon takes the way the items enter it, and R wins; the guide never asks again for what is done** (owner, 2026-10-16: « the router placement on top of ribbons that are curved is broken. the entrance is not on the correct side. And I cannot even press R to rotate if I want », and « the tutorial is sometimes annoying, it keeps telling me what to do (put a ribbon) even though it's done already ») | A block took the ribbon's own `rot`, which on a curve is the way *out*: its back faced away from the feed, the line stopped at it, and `placementRot` overrode every rotation, so R did nothing over a ribbon. The guide shows the first step whose `done` does not hold, else the last: four quests (first_strike, glassworks, crier_calls, tired_vein) ended on a ribbon step, so once every step held and the quest waited for its numbers (10 ore, 10 glass…) the arrow went back to « Trace un ruban » | **Engine** (systems/build.ts): `entryRot` reads the tile's feed as the compiler links it — a ribbon pointing in (past junctions; a traction ribbon from one level off; a bridge leaping over the tile feeds nothing), a building's outlet facing it, a routing block that may hand on that way; straight behind first, then a side (clockwise from the back when both feed it), none: the ribbon's own way. `placementRot` gives a one-tile directional block (router, sorters, gates) that rotation, its outputs then covering the way out. `placementRot(…, turned)` and `place(…, cfg, turned)`: the player's rotation as asked; the `place` command carries an optional `turned: true`, logged only where the turn changed the result, so every other log is as before and a replay turns it alike. **Build tool** (input/tools.ts): R turns from the rotation the ghost shows and marks the tool `turned` while it stays in hand (a new pick, the pipette or Esc clears it); the ghost reads the same `placementRot`. **Tutorial**: the six quests that ended on a step with `done` end on a closing line (`target: 'none'`); `questProblems` asks every quest's last step to have no `done` nor `land`; the guide never points at a step whose `done` holds. The ribbon and link checks were already graph-based (`flows`: any ribbon tier, bridges, junctions, routing blocks), and steps done out of order are passed by (tests: the ribbon before the drill, the smelter built backwards). Tests: entry-rot.test.ts, quests.test.ts |

Quartz faces (D87) unchanged. **UI** ([deposit-card.ts](../src/ui/deposit-card.ts), [deposit-facts.ts](../src/ui/deposit-facts.ts), [ui.md](ui.md#the-deposit-card-d99)): with nothing in hand, 320 ms of rest on a known deposit (or a tap on bare ground) pops a kit card: the ore's glyph and name, a tile's yield a minute as a meter against the ore's best tile, the deposit's known tiles and their sum, the machines that work it read from the content (`drill` without `ground`/`anywhere`/`face`; excavators then drills for a vein under rock; `drill.face` for a face ore; `pump` for water), dim with a padlock while locked, and the parcel's state or price. | D100 | **A long session costs its frames, not a leak: the world is drawn 10 times a second under a full screen, and « Basse consommation » caps the game at 30 frames a second and asks for the economical GPU** (lead's perf share, 2026-10-16; owner: « I feel like playing this game for a long time is very difficult for my computer ») | Measured first ([scripts/soak.mjs](../scripts/soak.mjs), headless Chrome at dpr 2, the grown debug base played for 30 minutes, a garbage collection before each sample): the kept heap flat after five minutes (57–66 MB, 1.5 MB an hour), DOM nodes and listeners flat, Pixi's live textures 85–117 (the terrain's chunks under their 256 MB LRU), the statistics and the ledger capped; only Pixi's texture table (null slots, a few hundred KB an hour) and the command log (~40 KB an hour of building) grow, harmlessly. The cost is the frames: ~30 % of a core for the whole browser at 60 frames a second, playing or paused, and 39 % with a full screen open — the browser re-blurred the live canvas behind the screen's paper at every frame | **App.frame** (src/app/app.ts): under a full screen (`ScreenHost.covers`: a `.screen` open) `drawWorld` and the render run every 0.1 s (`COVERED_S`) with the time gathered since; the sim, its events and the DOM at their pace. **main.ts** + [core/pace.ts](../src/core/pace.ts): `FramePacer` caps the drawn frames when `settings.lowPower` is on (30 fps, read every frame) and waits on a timer until a frame is nearly due, so the frames between are never asked for. **Settings**: « Basse consommation » (fr/en), live; `createStage` asks `powerPreference: 'low-power'` with it at the next launch. Numbers (whole browser, alternating A/B windows, medians): playing 31 → 31 %, the tree open 39 → 21 %; with the battery saver playing 31 → 25 %, paused 30 → 24 %. Tests: pace.test.ts (even frames at 60, 120, 144 Hz; the timer never misses a due frame). Not done (each changes the look): a default cap on displays faster than 60 Hz, the main menu's blur over the live base, a lower resolution (the « Mode performance » setting does it) |
