Optional objectives (`SIDE_GOALS`): the views, the telemetry, one more parcel, the ledger, the wind survey — ticks in the log. The clock and the Électricité view come with « La fonderie » (three drills outgrow the Core's night power). **The starter contract** (`BAL.starter`: 40 copper ore at a fixed 2 ◉, 10 a minute, 2 minutes' delivery, off the market, once per base; `takeStarter`, the `starter` command, `trade.starterTaken`, [engine.md](engine.md#trade)) buys nothing while the post's exit leads nowhere; « La Criée » wants its ore in a wire mill, with a drill on the thin vein for after. **Guide** ([ui/guide.ts](../src/ui/guide.ts)): a guided step done once never comes back (`passed`, per base, localStorage); a goal shows nothing until the log's « Indice » or 5 game minutes without a new best on any objective (`QUEST_HINT_AFTER`), then one marker until that hint holds — never a stream; « all plans » points at one ready plan (`unlockNode`); the bubble keeps off what it points at, the card or panel the step needs (a post's card and its order form, the tree's node and detail, the inspector), the quest log, the side panels and the HUD's readouts, tries eight spots and takes the clear one or the least covering, follows resizes and scrolls, and tucks into a ☞ chip (`[data-guide-tuck]`); a branch with no router unlocked points at its plan; a post whose exit already leads elsewhere points at that ribbon (« La sortie du comptoir mène déjà ailleurs »). **Quest log** ([ui/quest-log.ts](../src/ui/quest-log.ts)): « objectif n sur 8 », each objective with its icon and bar, « Indice » for a goal (`[data-quest-hint]`), « Objectifs libres ». **Night notice** ([ui/night-notice.ts](../src/ui/night-notice.ts)): once per base, about one game hour before a sunset while the sun gives 25 % or more of the Core's grid and the night would leave it under 80 %: the sun behind the Crown, a turbine, a coal generator, two figures, one sentence (« ajoute des éoliennes ou un générateur à charbon » — the brief's accumulators are Tier 2's), « Vue Électricité (P) », « Compris »; it waits while a side panel or a screen is up. **Saves**: `QuestSave.table` (`QUEST_TABLE` 2); an older save's ids fold into today's (`QUEST_FOLDS`, `foldDone`): « La Criée » done once its three were; the current quest re-baselined; tested from each of the 13 old quests. **Debug levels** ([scenarios.ts](../src/sim/scenarios.ts)): every quest met on its own on lands 1–3, nothing forced or skipped; the smelter's own drill pair, Crown wind for the night, the contract led from the post's exit into the copper line and taken up once delivered (`starterLine`, `starterDone`), an overflow gate on the smelter's drill into the Heart (`ironSurplus`: D85's idle check). **Tests**: quests.test.ts (the scripted run 0–8 in 26 game minutes, the contract, the night, `unless`, the pacing, the folds), trade.test.ts (the contract, 11), tree-state.test.ts, views-reveal.test.ts; the smoke's quests step (a goal's « Indice », the night notice). **Play-through**: two players on seeds 1–3 of today's map, from a fresh base. The **outcome bot** — the « tutorial » debug level's moves, real commands, every quest met on its own — ends the quests at 0.7–1.0, 2.0–2.4, 3.1–3.4, 6.5–6.7, 9.0–10.0, 13.2 and 22.7–23.1 game minutes, « Les pièces de la Synthèse » left current (about 10 minutes more in quests.test.ts, Mechanics' research). **D90's UI bot, adapted** (the arrow in a guided quest; in a goal, « Indice » and its one marker, asked again once it goes; private no-HMR servers): the name in 0.1 min, « Premier coup de pioche » 2.3, « La fonderie » 5.7–6.7, « Tenir la nuit » 1.6–1.7 on 3 hints; then « La verrerie » jams on the bot's own half-laid ribbon (its routing, as in D90). From the « survey » base: « La Criée » reaches its last ribbon in 4.5 min, which the bot cannot lay into the side of the vein's line on that crowded base; « Données d'arpentage » 4.3–4.4 min on 8 hints. No console or page error in any run. What the bots found, fixed: the night notice covered the inspector's recipe buttons (it now waits while a side panel is open) and stayed once the night held (it now goes); a glass smelter fed by its own coal drill left « La Criée »'s branch hint on a router never unlocked (its plan first now); a post set with its exit against a ribbon spilled the contract's ore into it (the guide now points at that tile: « La sortie du comptoir mène déjà ailleurs »); nothing asked for the thin vein's drill, so once the contract was spent no wire came (two hints for it in « La Criée »); the bubble by the tree chip covered the purse; the « Tenir la nuit » level placed extra Crown turbines (it now plants the two `morePower` planted, earlier: `crownWind`). Left: the guide's memory and « Les vues » are per browser; `night` reads the wind as it blows now; a full screen covers the log's « Indice ». **Second pass** (2026-10-17, the owner's notes relayed by the lead: « le face cutter devrait être beaucoup plus rapide à obtenir, ce tutoriel est chiant »; « the solar panels from the core should do a less important part of its electricity production, and there should be a quest in the tutorial where you upgrade it. The quest where you start researching could use the core's data production rather than a data collector, which could be unlocked later »; on their base the starter contract had bought on for hours at the market's price, about −2 200 ◉/h). The defaults below were the session's own, all tunable. **The starter contract stops at its cap** ([systems/trade.ts](../src/sim/systems/trade.ts)). It stands with `starter` 0, held 'fulfilled', and buys nothing at any price. « Contrat de départ rempli » ([ui/contract-notice.ts](../src/ui/contract-notice.ts), and on its Crier row) offers two choices: « Continuer au prix du marché »: `continueStarter`, the logged `starterGo` command. It becomes an ordinary order capped at `BAL.starter.continueOver` = 110 % of the ask. « Arrêter », the default: the order is cancelled. Its other parts: Delivery takes a minute (`delayMin` 1). Its row reads « Contrat de départ · prix fixe 2 ◉ », with what is left as a bar. Every order shows its live cost, « −17,36 ◉/min » in coral (`orderCentsPerMin`, `orderUnitCents`), or « 0/min » while it holds. The purse's breakdown (D102) prices the contract at its own price: 0 once fulfilled. **No wait but building time.** The Face Cutter (`ex_quartz`) is a plan of the Survey path (← beacon, and ← trade_post since D107), taken with every plan. « Le quartz de la paroi » no longer waits on research. `tr1` hangs from the lab's plan alone. « Données d'arpentage » only starts Mechanics. « Les pièces de la Synthèse » asks for oscillators and circuits only; the gears wait for the press. The contract lands in a minute. **The Core's power** (`BAL.hearthCoreKw` 210/300/420/560 and `hearthSolarKw` 90/130/180/240 by level, `producerOf(…, hearthLevel)`): the steady core gives about 70 % of the noon power and the panel 30 %. Two drills hold the night at level 1, three do not, so « Tenir la nuit » is about the player's machines. The Électricité view splits core and sun, and its forecast keeps the core at night. The night notice now shows from 15 % of sun, with a new sentence. **« Le Cœur grandit »** (new quest 6, `hearth` objective, guide target `hearth`, `QUEST_ADDED`, `QUEST_TABLE` 3): level 2 (`BAL.hearthUpgrades[0]`, unlock `start`, 120 plates, 40 glass, 40 copper wire) by the player's own `hearthUp`. Its hints name what is short, then point at the card's button. « La Criée » no longer pays level 2's materials (its reward is now 150 ◉ and 40 copper wire), and Mechanics no longer gates the level. Level 3 and the expertises are unchanged. **The first lab runs on the Core's own data.** From level 2 the Core makes 2 Survey Data a minute (`BAL.hearthDataBase`, research or not; `heartDataRate`). « Données d'arpentage » wants the Core's data in the lab (`feeds` `from` the Core: `producersOf` counts the Core's outlet; spot `{ outlet: true }`). The data collector comes with « Traitement des données » (`an_data`, Tier 1, +1 a minute at the Core). The Core's card shows its data rate. Save 13 (`coreData`) gives `an_data` to a claim with the lab's plan, so its collectors stand. A Face Cutter under way or queued becomes its plan taken, with its data back to the Heart. Older quest saves count « Le Cœur grandit » done once a later quest was. **No token text** (D105). **Debug levels** ([scenarios.ts](../src/sim/scenarios.ts)). The tutorial level now: raises the Core (`coreUpgrade`); sets the lab east of the Core on a ribbon from its outlet and starts Mechanics, then the lab's next node at the end (D107: Tier 1 takes minutes); stops the fulfilled contract (« Arrêter »). The grown level learns `an_data` and sets its collector by the lab. D104's irregular deposits (handed over by that session) needed four more fixes: the glass smelter goes down with its sand collector and moves along the coal line if boxed in; the post's stub takes a side join; `farCopper`'s fallback rewires the drill; `copperHome` takes up the copper drills it starves. An excavator with no free rock in reach moves on. `idleAfter` no longer counts a lab with no project, nor the data maker behind it. **Tests**: trade.test.ts 27: the stop, « Continuer » at the market's price and its replay, « Arrêter », the live cost; quests.test.ts 25: 0–9 scripted in 8.4 game minutes; « Le Cœur grandit »; the table-2 saves; research.test.ts: the Core's data at level 2; persist/core-data.test.ts: save 13; scenarios.test.ts 8/8 on lands 1–3; the smoke's research step now runs `tr1`. **Time per quest** (the tutorial debug level's moves on seeds 1–3, every quest met on its own; minutes at completion): « Premier coup de pioche » 0.6–0.9; « La fonderie » 2.6–2.8; « Tenir la nuit » same minute; « La verrerie » 4.0–4.1; « La Criée » 6.0 (first pass 9.0–10.0); « Le Cœur grandit » 10.2 (the script waits for the contract's delivery first; a player can raise the Core at once); « Données d'arpentage » 10.3 (first pass 13.2); « Le quartz de la paroi » 15.5–16.0 (first pass 22.7–23.1). The scripted run ends the last quest at 8.4 minutes (26 before). **Shots**: the D101-shots folder, d101-{core-hint,core-card,core-done,power-day,power-night,crier-buying,contract-notice,crier-fulfilled}-{fr,en}.png |
| D102 | **The purse's ◉/h is the purse's real recurring change, read from an engine journal; a click on it says, line by line, what it is made of; the tokens are told plainly** (owner, 2026-10-16: the HUD read −799 ◉/h and « je n'achète rien, je vends »; then « Il faut que les ventes, achats etc. soient beaucoup plus clairs, et que le chiffre soit réel, parce qu'actuellement il y a écrit −700 mais je gagne de l'argent. Pareil pour les tokens, ce n'est pas clair ») | The figure was the trade ledger alone over ten minutes (`tradePerHour`). Each line was right, but the window lied: standing orders switch on and off with the price against their cap, the sales follow the market, so on the late debug base, inside one hour in which the purse rose by 18 059 ◉ (measured: the ledger's trade +18 659 and the tokens −600, to the cent), the ten-minute figure ran from +31 800 down to +167 ◉/h; and the tokens, the Compute Plan, the rent and the rental were not in it at all. A tutorial's standing order still buying read as « I'm losing money selling » | **The journal** ([sim/purse.ts](../src/sim/purse.ts)): each place that changes `sim.credits` books the same cents by kind — `sales`, `orders` (every trade ledger line), `tokens`, `fee`, `rental`, `rent` recurring; `spotSell`, `spotBuy`, `land`, `reorient`, `reward`, `grant` one-offs; runtime only (neither saved nor hashed, like `hearthIn`); the kinds add up to the purse's change to the cent, online and through the catch-up (purse.test.ts). **The figure** ([ui/purse-facts.ts](../src/ui/purse-facts.ts)): the HUD samples the journal once a game minute; the headline is the recurring kinds' actual cents over the last hour (the minutes seen until then; nothing before the first), an hour; its lines — each trade post's sales by item and its standing orders (from the ledger over the same minutes, « other trade » for what it no longer holds), the tokens with the servers' share, the fee, the rental, the rent — add up to it exactly, and one-offs show apart, never in it (purse-facts.test.ts pins the headline to the purse's change on a rig and over a real hour of the late base). **The card** ([ui/purse-card.ts](../src/ui/purse-card.ts), the D89 kit): the headline and a sparkline of the purse, the window, the biggest drain named; a section per trade post (its items sold, « 12/min », the ◉/h after the fee; its orders with their cost a minute now), « Recherche » (« Tokens IA : 1 600/min · 13 ◉/min » with a server chip of the servers' share; a line of words the first time, then the icons), « Terres »; a click on a line goes where it is managed (`App.goToFlow`: the order's line or the post's card flashed in the Crier, `CrierScreen.focusLine`; the tree on a running project; the lease's parcel); « Ouvrir la Criée » at its foot — the purse no longer opens the Crier itself (the guide points at the purse, then the card's button; M still opens it). **Tokens elsewhere** ([ui/token-facts.ts](../src/ui/token-facts.ts)): each HUD research chip shows its tokens a minute (the ◉ a minute and the servers' share in its tooltip); a running project's card in the tree, its tokens a minute, ◉ a minute and the servers' share as icons; beside the Compute Plan's switch, an hour without and with it at today's use, the cheaper lit. Before the servers' plan no token is billed (D105, `tokensBilled`): the tree's research cards and detail say « 0 · offert » / « offert jusqu'aux serveurs » (the table's count and the plan's name in the tooltip; rebuilt when the plan is taken), and a project opened then carries 0 tokens, so no chip, card row or purse line shows tokens for it. Esc, a click elsewhere or ✕ close the card; a bottom sheet on a narrow window (D96). The Crier's own header keeps the trade's figure |
| D103 | **The Gleaners: a small desert people that comes out at night and takes a few shiny things off the ribbons it reaches, never in the cirque nor on the mountains; fences keep it out and glazed ribbons keep it off; every item it takes is counted, shown at dawn and marked on its line** (owner, 2026-10-17: « pendant la nuit, un petit peuple alien du désert peut parcourir le désert et venir prendre certains items qui sont sur les tapis. Mais ils restent dans le désert uniquement, et ne viennent pas dans le cirque, ils ne montent pas les montagnes. Le joueur peut débloquer des sécurités pour cela (grilles, ou des tapis avec du verre autour qui protège l'objet à l'intérieur) »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | A base that grows past the Crown (the quartz line, D87; the far works) crosses open desert; the desert should be a place, alive at night, with a small cost to building out there and two ways to answer it. Harmless and curious rather than an enemy: no combat, no damage, a fraction of what passes. Nothing vanishes silently (§2's invariant): what they take is a flow the player sees, counts and can stop | **Who**: « les Glaneurs » / the Gleaners (lore.md), small lanky hooded figures with lantern eyes. **Where** ([sim/world/reach.ts](../src/sim/world/reach.ts)): free floor at level 0 past the Crown's foot (`crownOuter`; a land without it — the tests' flat world — has none); a pocket of such floor of at most `BAL.gleaners.pocket` (1 500) tiles closed in by rock, water, gaps, buildings, ribbons or fences is out of their reach; a ribbon tile beside reachable floor is *exposed*, one under glass or beside a closed pocket *guarded*; rebuilt with the graph at each compile (≈ 0.75 ms on the late base). **When**: the dark hours, `SOLAR` ≤ `darkSun` (0): 12 game minutes a day. **What and how much** ([sim/flow/chain.ts](../src/sim/flow/chain.ts) `gleanStep`): a ribbon chain's exposed cells; on `gleanRate` = min(`maxPermille` 50, `perTile` 2 × exposed tiles) ‰ of its steps, spread evenly over a 1 200-tick window of the world's clock (an error-diffusion line from the chain's own phase), they look at one exposed cell (a hash of the chain and the step) and take its item by their taste ([content/gleaners.ts](../src/content/gleaners.ts) `GLEANER_TASTE`: wire, glass, quartz, oscillators, circuits always; plates, gears, cells less; ore, stone, sand, coal, water and data never) — so a long exposed line loses up to 5 % of what passes by night (≈ 1.7 % of a day), a short dash across a corner little (a tiny exposure's tries are fixed per window: 0 or 1 item every two minutes, right on average across chains); no curio left behind (a new item would need the market's buyers: left out). **Offline-exact**: the theft is inside the flow phases (`ctx.glean`), so the catch-up runs it in its warm-ups: the night is part of a group's conditions (`Cond.night`, its regime key), a night group's period folds in the window (`Group.rotNight`), and the warm-ups record what is taken as a third statistics series (`ctx.gleaned`, `Regime.gleanSeries`); groups re-warm at nightfall and dawn; bit-exact on a steady line over two nights (`gleaners.test.ts`). No new hashed state, no command. **Counted and shown** ([sim/systems/gleaners.ts](../src/sim/systems/gleaners.ts)): `Sim.gleaned` per item (lifetime, saved, not hashed: the conservation identity becomes Δheld = produced − consumed − gleaned); at nightfall and dawn the counts (`dusk`, `dawn`, saved) and a `gleaners` event: `night` at their first take of a night (the notice that introduces them, once per base), `dawn` with what the night took (the morning report, each item by its icon); the lines' marks since dusk (`marks`); the absence report's `gleaned`; the statistics' « Pris par les Glaneurs ». **Protections** (research `xp_guard`, « Grilles et rubans vitrés », Expansion's third Tier 1 node, after the Great Silo; in the Synthesis): the **Grille** (`fence`, 1×1, 1 plate, laid by dragging like a ribbon, no node) and the **Ruban vitré** (`glazed_ribbon`, 60/min as the ribbon, 1 glass, the transport group: dragged over a ribbon it covers it). **Seen** (owner: « il faut qu'on les voie quand même, la nuit, venir prendre les objets »): every try the sim makes is known ahead (`gleanSchedule`), so a Gleaner comes out of the dunes to each one, leans over the ribbon at that tick and, when the sim's log says it took something, lifts the item off the belt and runs back into the desert holding it up, glowing — what one sees is what the morning report counts; by glazed ribbons one now and then taps the glass, by fences one paces outside; dark hooded figures with lantern eyes and a soft halo, small moving lights from afar ([render/world/gleaners-view.ts](../src/render/world/gleaners-view.ts)); a glazed ribbon wears a glass hood over its items; the Logistique view hatches the exposed tiles with a crescent and rings the guarded ones, and its card counts them and names the night's takings; a ribbon's inspector says « Exposé la nuit ». The late debug base covers its exposed plain ribbons with glass (its few swift and traction pieces out there stay exposed) |
| D104 | **Map 4's deposits come in whole 2 × 2 blocks on the world's grid, a drill's footprint: drills cover a deposit with none of its tiles left over and none of theirs on sand; each still a natural patch; a drill held over ore snaps onto the grid** (owner, 2026-10-17: « dans le cirque, c'est un peu frustrant comme les ressources disponibles ne sont pas des multiples de la taille des machines qui les extraient »; then, seeing them square: « the ressources shouldn't be perfect 2x2 or 4x4 squares, it's not the point, it still has to look natural. it's just that before, they were really frustrating sizes ») | A deposit was a disc tile by tile (a 5 × 5 square, a 37-tile round), so four 2 × 2 drills always left tiles over or stood half on sand, and the player could not see where a drill fits best. Map 4 had not been played yet, so it changed in place (no map 5); maps 1–3 stay tile by tile. A 3 × 3 deep bore sits astride blocks, and that is fine | `MapParams.oreBlock` (2 on map 4), `TerrainSource.oreBlock`. mapgen.ts `blocksOf`: a disc becomes as many blocks as it held tiles (rounded), grown as one patch from the block nearest its centre, each next block the one beside it whose distance plus a hashed wobble (`WOBBLE`) is least — it bulges and notches like a natural deposit; a first cut took the nearest blocks and gave squares and rectangles, which the owner turned down —, passing over a block one of whose tiles takes no ore (`takesOre`, read without settling: `groundAt`). Its richness is the disc's to the unit, the heart's blocks at full weight, the rest at 70 %, each block's tiles alike. This applies to the cirque's, the copper field's, the sites' and the common deposits and veins; the quartz face (D87) is a wall and keeps its tiles. Map 4's cirque deposits (`CIRQUE_V4`, `BLOCK_DEPOSITS`) sit at most a tile off the template, each patch's wobble seeded by its `shape` (the same for every player), picked by a scratch search for a compact patch that is no rectangle, its middle on the template's, three tiles off the others and the tutorial's distances whatever the shift. The per-player shift is a whole block along the Crown (`across`: across the deposit's bearing from the Heart), never towards it, so the tutorial's distances (the coal and the tired vein a pole away, a drill spot within r 18) are everyone's: a ±2 shift towards the Heart broke them. Thin open-ground deposits are one block or two (`OBSTACLES.ore`: `thin` 5, `thinRadius` [10, 8]): space or ore keeps r −0.41 … −0.57 on lands 1–5. [ore-blocks.ts](../src/sim/ore-blocks.ts): `oreSnap` (a 2 × 2 or 4 × 4 drill over ore moves its corner down to the grid; tools.ts `snapOrigin`, for the ghost, the click and R alike), `blockDrill`. The ore art keeps its organic stain (D99): a square stain with dashed block edges was tried and dropped with the square shapes — the snapping ghost shows where a drill fits; dev.ts `spotsNear` tries grid spots first (`OFF_GRID`), so the debug bases' drills (D82) stand wholly on ore (8–12 per cirque in the late base on lands 1–3, none astride: scenario-cirque.ts skips its rim pass on a land of blocks, and a drill tries every line with room before it counts a miss). Their mined share first did not rise (31–54 % on lands 1–3); then (same day) a drill on a deposit faces off the ore where it can (dev.ts `spotsNear`: its output onto ore counts as `ORE_SPOT` farther, so its line does not cross the blocks the next drills want), and last in `fillCirque` `plugDeposits` puts a drill on each block left whose output meets a passing ribbon (side or back) when every way on ends in the Heart, the trade post or a store and each line on it has room for the drill's pace, measured over a minute (D85): 46–65 % grown, 46–58 % late on map 4 (map 3 48–56 %). A higher ore cost for lines (12) gained nothing and lost land 2's quartz line: kept at 6. Still bounded: it is bounded by what the lines carry (D85: the coal into the smelters only) and by the lines' room round the deposits — a deposit hemmed in by the tutorial's lines takes no drill at all —, not by the blocks; the north-west iron's `shape` is the one of the valid patches the fill reaches the most of. The new layout broke the tutorial levels' glassworks and the late base's copper swap: fixed on the scripts' side (D101's session: kiln2 laid with its sand collector, a restored drill rewired, an excavator kept only where it reaches rock). Tests: [ore-blocks.test.ts](../src/sim/ore-blocks.test.ts); shots: `node scripts/block-shots.mjs` Since 2026-10-17 (D94's open desert) a candidate in the open desert is taken at half its chance, and within 84 tiles of the centre at 60 ‰, a thin blob (a block or two). |
| D105 | **Research costs no AI tokens until the claim can place servers** (owner, 2026-10-17: « les premières techs ne devraient pas coûter de tokens. Elles devraient commencer à en coûter quand le joueur peut commencer à placer des serveurs ») | Tokens were billed from the first node: a young claim paid CrierBelt by the minute for research before it could make a token of its own, a drain it could neither see coming nor answer | `tokensBilled(sim)` (systems/research.ts): true once the server's unlock (read off the content: `an_servers` today) is done; a project opened before that carries 0 tokens, and keeps what it started at (a project running when servers unlock stays free). The table's token costs are unchanged and apply from then on. Test: research/tokens-free.test.ts (the tutorial base's research free, the late base's billed) **Replaced by D118**: Tier 1 takes no tokens at all, from Tier 2 every node needs its tokens, a flow from the servers or bought from CrierBelt; `tokensBilled` is gone. |
| D106 | **The Electric Smelter: the smelter's plates and glass on power, no coal, at the smelter's pace, for a heavy draw; Tier 1, and the arc furnace's recipes are its own** (owner, 2026-10-17: « il faut un electric smelter, qui consomme beaucoup mais qui permet de faire ce que fait le smelter sans charbon. Tier 1 ou 2 »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | The cirque's coal bounds its smelters (D97: one coal drill for one smelter); the only way off coal was the Arc Furnace, at depth 6 of Mechanics and built of ceramic, gears, circuits and servomotors — far too late and too dear. A base that has power should be able to spend it on plates instead of coal, early, and pay for it at the grid | **Content**: `electric_smelter` « Fonderie électrique » / Electric Smelter (buildings.ts, after the compact smelter): a 2×2 `machine`, speed 1000 (the smelter's pace), `kw` 300 at work and `idleKw` 15 (a big consumer: a Core of level 2 gives 300 kW at night; about a good turbine's week on the crest), one input at the back and the output at the front, built of 25 plates, 10 copper wire, 4 glass. **Recipes**: the arc furnace's `arc_plate` and `arc_glass` (ids kept: saves carry them) now run on `ARC` = the electric smelter and the arc furnace, named « Plaque de fer (électrique) » / « Verre (électrique) », at the coal recipes' 6 s: 10 a minute in the electric smelter, 20 in the arc furnace (it made 30 plates at 4 s; its node's text now says twice the electric smelter); two recipes, so a new one waits for the player's choice (D83). **The tree**: `dn_esmelt` « Fonderie électrique » / Electric Smelting, Machines' Tier 1 at depth 2, between `dn3` and `dn_wiremill` (a lane holds one node a column: it stands in the lineage, and the twin wire mill hangs from it); it unlocks the building and both recipes; the Synthesis needs it (D93); the arc furnace stays at `dn_arc`, the fast version. **Its cost against coal** (the Crier's board, `c_E` = `BAL.market.energyCost`): 300 kW × 0.04 = 12 ◉ a minute against the smelter's 10 coal at the bid, so coal stays the cheaper plate; but it mines no coal, so it earns more per deposit tile. **Saves**: 11 → 12, `electricSmelting` (persist/migrate.ts): a claim holding the twin wire mill or the Synthesis (learnt or under way) gets the new node, so the nodes it holds stay met and its arc furnaces keep their recipes. **Art** (render.md §9, 14): an induction furnace — a crucible in a copper coil under laminated yokes, the melt orange-hot, bus bars on porcelain stand-offs from a teal inverter cabinet with a high-voltage sign, its terminal on the cabinet and the heavy cable into a gland, capacitor cans by the intake; no stack; cyan arcs while it works (`sparkIso`), none and a dark melt when switched off; not in `HOT` (a cold night pool). **Debug bases**: `moreSmelters` (scenario-cirque.ts) lays the best smelter on power learnt (`powerSmelter`: the arc furnace, else the electric smelter); today no level learns the node without the arc furnace (the grown base's research is unchanged), and the iron lines of lands 1–3 leave no room for those extras anyway. | D107 | **A sensible, readable tech tree: nothing is unlocked that cannot be used by then; Expansion becomes Logistics; Commerce's Tier 1 is a beginner's, finance waits for Tier 2; research short early, long from Tier 3; the tree in tier bands, its links routed round the cards, a hovered node's chain lit; the reconversion retired** (owner, 2026-10-17: « Il faut s'assurer qu'un joueur ne puisse pas débloquer, dans le tech tree, quelque chose qu'il ne peut pas utiliser. Usable: extracted (a machine known mines, collects, pumps or digs it: the land's common deposits, the ground's sand, water, stone, the Core's Survey Data), made (a known recipe on a buildable machine, from usable inputs) or bought (a depth, a sister colony that supplies it, a trade post buildable); what only some maps hold — the uniques, a cliff face's quartz — counts only as bought. « By then » is the node's closure; a research node knows every plan (D71). **Fixed**: the `workshop` plan ← sand collector and wire mill (was ← the smelter); `ex_quartz` ← the trade post too; `ex_sifter` ← Earthworks (`tr2`) too, so Basalt Bits and Heartrock Cutters follow the excavator; `dn_compact` ← Ceramics (`tr3`); `cm_plan` ← the Server Room (`an_servers`), Server Rental after it; `xp4` ← Land Registry (`tr4`). The plan path « Logistique » keeps its name: each plan path leads into the family of its theme (production → Machines, logistics → Logistics, energy → Energy, analytics → Analytics, trade → Commerce, survey → Extraction), as Énergie, Analytique and Commerce already shared theirs. **Moved** (ids kept): Commerce runs `cm_post2` « Second comptoir » / Second Post (new, Tier 1 depth 1: a second trade post, flag `post2`; `maxPosts` reads it, or `tradeL2` for older claims) → `cm_floor` (2) → `cm2` Standing Orders (Tier 1 d1 → Tier 2 d3) → `cm_plan` (4) → `cm_rent` (Tier 4 d7 → Tier 2 d4) → `cm3` (Tier 2 d3 → Tier 3 d5, the post's level 2 alone) → `cm_express` (6) → `cm8` Grand Post (Tier 3 d5 → Tier 4 d7) → `cmK` (9). Extraction: `ex_fastdrill` (Tier 2 d4 → Tier 1 d1, the family's first) → `ex_sifter` (Tier 1 d2 → Tier 2 d3) → `ex3` (4) → `ex4` (4), Drilling and Excavation from Basalt Bits. `xp_bridges` hangs from `xp_guard`, Tier 1's last. The Synthesis needs `ex_fastdrill` and `cm_post2` instead of `ex_sifter` and `cm2`. Unchanged: the nodes of Energy and Analytics, `an_data` (the data collector, `heartData` +1), `tr1` (its prerequisite the lab's plan), `dn_esmelt` (D106). **Times** (`DEPTH_COST`, depths 1–9): 1 · 3 · 8 · 20 · 60 · 180 · 480 · 1 440 minutes (were 3 · 15 · 45 · 120 · 240 · 480 · 720 · 1 440); Survey Data 6 · 20 · 90 · 240 (were 15 · 60 · 180 · 480; Tiers 3–4 unchanged: 960 · 1 920 · 2 880 · 5 760), so a Tier 1 lab runs on about 6 a minute (the Core's 3 and a collector or two) and the short times are the real ones; tokens unchanged. The trunk: Mechanics 1 min (6 data), the Synthesis 3 (20), Earthworks and Ceramics 8, Land Registry and the Second Desk 20, Atlas Lore 30, the Third Desk 3 h, Codex Lore 6 h (was 24 h). The expertises' research percentages (D93) still apply to the table: −15 to −35 % matters from Tier 3, where research is long. **Saves**: v14 (`tierOneWhole`): a claim holding the Synthesis (learnt or under way) holds `ex_fastdrill` and `cm_post2` too (a project of either learnt, the data it had paid back to the Heart), so its Tier 1 stays whole; nodes keep their ids, a project the cost it started at; `tierTwoOpen`'s fallback for claims from before the expertises reads the frozen `TIER2_UP_V10` (now in content/retired.ts) and only nodes still of Tier 2, so the Standing Orders or the Sifter learnt at Tier 1 open nothing ([persist/tree-d107.test.ts](../src/persist/tree-d107.test.ts), a late base saved before). **The screen** ([ui.md](ui.md#the-tech-tree-screen)): four tier bands with a gutter between them, wider gaps between cards (60 px) and lanes (38 px); the Synthesis alone at Tier 1's end, every family's last Tier 1 node joining it on one bus; links in right angles with rounded elbows through the gaps and along the channels between lanes, never across a card (`route`; [tree-layout.test.ts](../src/ui/screens/tree-layout.test.ts)), of three kinds (`line`, `cross`, `gate`); a node under the mouse lights its whole chain — what it needs and what needs it, through the gates no further (`chainOf`) — and fades the rest (on a touch screen, the selected node's); a research card shows what it unlocks, then its time and its data (compact: « 5,8 k »), then its tokens (« offert » before the servers, D105). The smoke's tree step checks a hovered chain; [scripts/tree-shots.mjs](../scripts/tree-shots.mjs) takes the whole tree and hovered chains at 1440 × 900 and on a tablet. **Reconversion retired**: the tree's « Reconvertir » buttons (the lanes' and the detail pane's family corner, `.rs-respec`, `.rs-fam`) are gone with their keys (`research.respec.*`, `tree.rs.family*`); `sim.respec`, `respecBlock`, their cooldown and refund (`respecOrder`, `respecTicks`, `respecRefund`, `BAL.respecRefund`, `respecCooldownDays`) too. The `respec` command stays in the union so older logs read, and does nothing. A save's reconversion under way (`respec:<family>`, no lab) still loads, runs out and pays its archive notes, which pre-pay research; the HUD names it, a toast ends it; `respecs` and `lastRespec` stay saved, inert; buildings whose node is gone stand dormant until relearnt. No save step ([research.test.ts](../src/sim/research/research.test.ts)). `research.branch.xp` reads Logistique / Logistics. **Notes**: a rule over content — a new node, item or recipe fails the build if it unlocks what cannot be used. a new node, item or recipe fails the build if it unlocks what cannot be used. Defaults chosen by the session and tunable: the times and data by depth, the Second Post as Commerce's first node (price alerts left aside: a new mechanic), Server Rental at Tier 2 with the Compute Plan. The debug bases follow (scenarios.ts, the tutorial session: `GROWN_NEXT` on Tier 1 nodes; the late base keeps its Wider Wharf, so its Compute Plan comes learnt and it runs the Research Queue alone) **Amended by D111**: the chain lights on a node selected, no longer under the mouse. |

Still nothing saved, no sim cost but the layout. **Its own Core** (owner: « pas de cœur non plus, ils devraient avoir un cœur, juste pas le même que le mien », « leur cœur devrait être plus petit aussi »): at each base's heart a 3 × 3 Core against the claim's 5 × 5, and lower — a stone plinth, a plank drum, a striped canvas cone, crates on its corners, crowned by its trade's piece (a mining camp's or a drifter guild's timber mast, a foundry's or an ember forge's chimney, a glasshouse's or a salt port's lantern, a wind citadel's or a scholar spire's dish); the chain's last line runs into it, its grid starts from it, the lift pad set beside it sells what it holds; a click on it (or on any piece of the base) shows the base's card (emblem, name, trade). **Goods on the drawn lines** (owner: « visual glitch where a ribbon from a PNJ base disappears »): a line laid as one record was culled whole with its first tile's 16-tile square, while its goods, placed tile by tile, rode on over bare sand; each line is now drawn in stretches of one square (`ribbonRuns`), its goods ride the very list drawn and show only where their stretch is (`goodAt`, `foreignRibbonShown`), checked by a test on lands 1–3 and in the smoke (`audit`). **Real chains, and bigger** (owner: « les bases de PNJ n'ont aucun sens. Every machine runs a recipe its building can make, fed each input by a line from the stage that makes it; the goods run into the store, the store into the trade post (where it sells: the drone lifts off from there) and the Core; what it buys leaves the post's one outlet for a depot (a silo, a great silo, a second depot) whose outlets feed the machines across from them in order. | D111 | **The tech tree zooms out to the whole tree: Ctrl/⌘ + wheel or a pinch about the pointer, a zoom bar with « Tout voir », the zoom keys; the cards simplify, the titles stay readable; a node's chain focused when it is selected, not hovered** (owner, 2026-10-17: « je veux pouvoir dézoomer dans le tech tree », then « il faut que le "focus" sur une tech se fasse quand le joueur sélectionne une tech, pas au hover »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | At 5 550 × 1 330 px (D107) the tree showed a sixth of itself on a 1 440 × 900 window: no way to see its shape, tiers and families at once | The canvas takes a CSS `zoom` from the whole tree in the view (`fitZoom`, about 0,2 on 1 440 × 900) to 125 % (`ZOOM_MAX`), about the pointer (`setZoom`: measured before and after, scrolled by the difference); drag still pans. **The focus** (amending D107): a node selected — a click, a tap, Enter — lights its chain and fades the rest, with its detail; it stays through zooming and panning until another is selected, the empty tree clicked or Esc (the screen's `back()`: the focus first, then the tree closes); the tree's own choice on opening is no focus; the mouse over a card only raises it. Ways in: Ctrl/⌘ + wheel (and a trackpad's pinch) — the plain wheel still scrolls —; a two-finger pinch on touch (D96); the zoom bar in the view's corner (« − », the percentage that goes back to 100 %, « + », « Tout voir »: `[data-tree-zoom]`); while the tree is open `-` / `=` (the world's zoom keys, `Screen.zoomKey`) and `0` (the tenth slot) the whole tree. The cards simplify (`zoomLevel`: `mid` under 70 %, icon and name on two lines; `far` under 36 %, the icon); the links' width and dashes and the cards' borders keep their size on screen (`--tz`); the tiers' and lanes' names are pinned over the view at reading size while the cards are small (`.tr-pins`). Tests: tree-layout.test.ts (`fitZoom`, `zoomLevel`), the smoke's tree step (nothing lit on opening nor under the mouse; a click focuses, Esc lets go with the tree still open; « Tout voir », a click focusing there, a zoom step keeping it, a click on the empty tree letting go, back to 100 % with the focus), scripts/tree-shots.mjs (the whole tree and a mid zoom, desktop and tablet; Ctrl+wheel keeps the card under the pointer) **The detail pane says a reason once** (owner: « no need to say it twice, the prerequisites line is enough »): under « Lancer » / « Débloquer », no line for a missing prerequisite (its ✗ in the list, the button greyed), a material short (its coral cost line) or the gate's plans (their bars); one terse line with its icon for what the pane does not show — two expertises to choose, the slots full, no idle lab, the purse in the red, the restart reserve, a plan not offered yet (`.rs-why[data-why]`); the Research Queue's note no longer repeats the missing names; toasts and tooltips keep their full reasons. **Tighter rows** (owner: « reduce the space between each row of the research tree, this is too much »): half D107's gaps — 6 px between the trunk's rows (10), 14 between specialities (26), 20 between lanes (38), bands reaching 5 px (8) —, the tier gutters 22 px (30) and pads 26 (30); the gaps between sub-columns, where the links turn, unchanged (60); the canvas 1 130 px tall (1 330). « Tout voir » gains little (19,0 → 19,2 %): the tree is 4,9 times wider than tall, so its fit is the width's. Shots FR and EN, 1440 × 900 and a tablet. |
| D112 | **The Gleaners come in groups and really take what rides past: a band of three to five glides in out of the dunes to a run of a line's exposed cells, waits there, and each takes the first thing to its taste that rides onto its cell — the item leaves the belt into its hand — then the band drifts back into the desert with what it took; they float, legless, hooded, a sack on the back** (owner, after playing: « les glaneurs apparaissent derrière la machine qui est derrière eux, et l'un d'eux semble ne pas réussir à réellement prendre l'objet du tapis. Et ils sont partis vers ma base, alors qu'ils devraient partir dans le désert vers là où il n'y a rien »: they now **sort with the buildings**, **reach only for the token the sim takes on that step** — it leaves the belt into the hand on the same frame — and **come from and go back to the empty desert**) (then: « actuellement, les glaneurs volent au-dessus du sable, mais j'aimerais qu'ils rampent dedans plutôt »: they **live in the sand** — they travel under it, rise out of it at the line to the shoulders, wait half-buried, and sink back with what they took) (then, 2026-10-18: « certains glaneurs restent pendant très longtemps devant un tapis plein à ne rien faire puis partent … il faut qu'ils aillent nécessairement vers un tapis où il y a des items, et si possible en priorité les items qui valent cher, et ensuite il faut qu'ils attrapent les items plus rapidement »: **a raid is called only for a line carrying something to their taste, a bigger band the more it is worth, and the band takes within seconds and is gone twelve seconds after**) (owner, 2026-10-17: « les glaneurs doivent avoir un style amélioré. Ne fais pas de jambes si c'est trop compliqué à animer. Ils doivent venir en groupe, aller vers un tapis où il y a des objets en mouvement, et réellement les récupérer (ils attendent qu'un objet passe et cet objet disparaît du tapis et se retrouve dans leurs mains, et ils s'en vont avec) ») | D103's thefts were single tries spread over the window, a share of what passed: what the views could show was one figure peering at a cell now and then, never a band at work, and a try could fall on an empty cell. A raid known ahead, whose takes are the items sitting on its posts, makes what the player sees exactly what the sim does, and a band waiting by a busy line reads at a glance. No legs: a floating cloak animates with a bob, a sway and waving wisps, without a walk cycle | `BAL.gleaners`: `window` 2 400 ticks (one raid a chain a window; ≤ `PERIOD_MAX`, so the night period `rotNight` = lcm(`rotL`, 2 400)), `raid` 600, `stagger` 70, `group` {min 3, max 5, per 8}; `perTile` / `maxPermille` retired. flow/chain.ts `raidSize`, `planRaid` (posts = a run of exposed cells round a hashed centre, `raidAt` in the window), `raidStep` (on a step, before the moves: each open post takes the item on its cell if to its taste; `raidHeld` a bit a post, hashed, in the chain's snapshot and its save entry as optional `raid`); compile.ts lays the raid out, groups.ts signs it. systems/gleaners.ts keeps the last 256 takes for the views. render: recipes/gleaners.ts redrawn (`drawGleaner` poses `glide`/`carry`/`reach`, `drawGleanerArm`, `drawGleanerShadow`), world/gleaners-view.ts draws each raid (arrive, wait, an arm out over an item on the post's cell, the grab from `recent` at the take's tick, leave together after the last take or at the raid's end); the glass and the fences get bands of two or three. `FL.gleaners()` lists the next `raids` and counts arriving / waiting / reaching / grabbing / carrying; scripts/gleaner-shots.mjs steps a raid. After playing: the figures' body, arm and held item are sprites in `objects` keyed `figZ` (8·(⌊x⌋+⌊y⌋) + 1.25, the world/stand.ts scale; a plan copy on `items`, the shadow on the floor), the glows dim behind a building or a cliff (`hiddenAt`); `Chain.raidWillTake(p, tick, night)` (flow/chain.ts, with `raidTakes`, the one rule `raidStep` runs) says what a post takes on the line's next step, read before it: the arm goes out only for that token, the hand follows where `ItemsView.seen` draws it on the post's tile (`watch`), and at the take the item appears where the token was last drawn; the head is never a post (its item may leave on that step); the view counts each take as it shows (`paired`, `onBelt`, `unforeseen`, `unseen`, `late`, in `FL.gleaners()`), and gleaners.test.ts checks tick by tick that the takes foreseen are the takes made; `desertBearing` (24 bearings, 14 tiles: buildings, ribbons, rock, the claim's land, away from the Core) gives each raid and visit its way in and out, chosen with the side the band waits on (`bandSide`: from a step out on each open side, the most open way leading away from the line; 1.8 tiles off on the line's near side, so no Gleaner hides its neighbour's token); in the sand: the figure is cut at the sand line (`cropTex`: views of the same canvases, `GLEANER_CUT` waiting, `GLEANER_PEEK` peeking) under a heap it pushes up (`drawSandCollar`, over the cut), the bulge and the closing furrow laid flat on the glass plane (`drawBulge`, `drawFurrow`: `TRAIL` marks at its own past places, fainter and narrower with age), grains (`drawGrain`) thrown up or sliding off; every way under the sand is clipped to free floor (`clipTo`); the fence visits are two furrows twice round a loop clear of it; the cards show one risen to the shoulders (`drawGleanerRisen`); the call: `raidBandNow` at the line's first step of its slot (`callBy` 40) — the most valuable thing to their taste on the line up to its last post (`gleanValue`, centi-◉) gives the band (`raidBandFor`: `band` 2, +1 at 5 / 12 / 40 ◉, up to its posts), none for nothing; `raidHeld` keeps the call and the band beside the posts' bits; then `arrive` 100 ticks of crawling, at most `take` 120 of taking (`stagger` 8); `RAID_TICKS` 220 replaces the 600-tick raid. No window draw for the value preference: it would break a night group's period offline; the band's size carries it instead. The view shows a band only once its line has called it, as many as called |

| D114 | **Water from Tier 1: the steam boiler, coal and water into 600 kW, two and a half times the coal generator's power per coal; the pump and the conduit in its Tier 1 plan; water pours down a terrace in a conduit, never up; « La vapeur » in the tutorial** (owner, 2026-10-17: « Il faudrait que l'eau serve assez tôt dans le jeu, dès le Tier 1 »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) — *700 kW since D143, which retired the coal generator: the one plant that burns coal* | Water came only from pumps behind Ceramics (`tr3`, Tier 2), carried by conduits made of ceramic, and its one use was the ceramic oven: nothing in Tier 1 drank it. The cirque's coal is short (D97: one coal drill a smelter), and the coal generator gets 30 kW out of a coal a minute. A conduit kept to its level (D77), so water up on the Crown (the tarn, D63) served only the peak | **Content**: `steam_boiler` « Chaudière à vapeur » / Steam Boiler (buildings.ts, after the coal generator), a 3×2 `generator`, onRock, its coal intake and its water intake at the back (each takes either), 30 plates, 6 glass, 6 copper wire; `gen: { kw: 600, fuel: 'coal', fuelPerMin: 8, water: 30 }` — 75 kW a coal a minute, 2.5× the coal generator; a pump (60/min) feeds two. It burns and drinks in proportion to what the grid asks after the wind, the sun and the Core (merit order), night and day alike, nothing on standby: the wind and the sun stay free, the boiler is the efficient fuel option, the coal generator the simplest. **The tree**: the plan `steam` « Vapeur » / Steam Power (Energy path, ← the coal generator; 20 plates, 4 glass, 2 wire) unlocks the boiler, the water pump and the conduit, which leave Ceramics (it keeps the ceramic oven, the assembly workshop and the accumulator); the conduit costs a plate a tile (a ceramic before). D107's usability checks a generator's water too (usable.ts). **Water runs down** (amends D77): a conduit hands its water on to the tile ahead one level lower — over a terrace's edge — never up and never two levels at once (compile.ts target rule, build.ts `stepRefusal`: `cliff`, « l'eau ne monte jamais, et ne descend qu'un gradin à la fois »); `routeLevel(…, fall)` lays such lines. A pump anywhere on the Crown's stream or tarn waters the floor below. **Engine**: `GeneratorNode` gains an optional second box (`waterItem`, `waterEp`, `water`, `wCarry`): `fueled` wants both, the missing one is the `NoFuel` reason the inspector names; refunded with its coal (compile.ts `refundNode`), held (state.ts), hashed and saved only when it drinks. The catch-up's dispatch is now online's exact ceiling of the average need (it was rounded up to 25 ‰: a half-called boiler burnt and drank about 3 % too much offline); a steady boiler matches online to the milli-item. **UI**: the card's water row (`card.water`), the inspector's equation (coal and water → kW) and a second meter; the Électricité view counts it a fuel source. **Tutorial**: quest 5 « La vapeur » (`steam_power`, after « La verrerie »; reveals the Steam plan): water reaches a boiler from a pump, coal reaches it, the boiler on a grid; 30 plates and 10 glass; seven hints, the pump's with an arrow to the water nearest the Core (`GuideSpot` `water`, readouts/water.ts `nearestWater`: on any level). `QUEST_TABLE` 4 (`QUEST_ADDED`): a save past it plays on, one still at « La Criée » meets it first. **Where the water is**: map 4's spring (D115): a stream down the Crown's terraces into a pool at the floor's edge, about 20 tiles east-south-east of the Core; maps 2–3 the tarn up on the peak (its water now comes down in a conduit), map 1 its lake. **Debug bases** (scenario-steam.ts, from the `survey` level on): a pump on the far shore of the nearest water, the boiler beyond it, a short conduit; a coal drill of the boiler's own on the cirque's coal, its line round the floor's rim (`rimRoute`, outside r 20 where it can — the middle is the trade post's, the lab's and the quartz lines', laid later) across the lines in its way on junctions; all wired. D85's `idleAfter` counts a source that feeds only generators on standby (the boiler's coal drill and pump) as on standby too. The grown base's glass silo is an extra now (D117 retired the unloaders it served): tried by the glass line's middle, then by the glass smelter, else left out — the boiler's drill on the coal's outer corner moves the base's later drills, which on lands 2–3 took its room. The step tries 24 boiler spots at most (a land where it fails costs no seconds). **Saves**: 15 → 16 (`steamPlan`): a claim holding the lab's plan holds `steam`, so its pumps and conduits stay awake; a boiler's water is an optional node field. **Art** (render.md §9, 15): a brick firebox and chimney with its plume, a riveted drum, a steam engine whose six-spoke flywheel turns while it burns, a teal dynamo; the plume and the fire go out when it stops. Not done: a second early use of water (an ore washer was weighed and left: the boiler is the use, and a washer would add an item and a third smelter recipe) | Tests: sim/steam-boiler.test.ts (ratios, full output, proportional water, no water no power, standby, refunds, online = offline over a day and a half, water pouring down a terrace and never climbing, the plan, save 15 → 16), quests.test.ts (the scripted colony plays « La vapeur » by a pond), scenarios.test.ts (the levels build it, nothing forced) |

| D115 | **Map 4's tarn gives way to a spring in a basin on a little plateau north of the Crown, the land's highest (level 6), whose stream comes south in falls the iso camera sees, across a ledge where the late base's potteries stand, into a small pool on the cirque's floor; the water is drawn with the mesas' care** (owner, 2026-10-17: « Il reste un lac vestige de ce qu'on faisait avant avec l'eau, sur la couronne. Level 6 is new: `MAX_LEVEL` 6 (grid.ts), `MOUNTAIN_LEVEL` 5 every mountain's, massif's and the Crown's cap (mapgen's caps, `REACH` 4 for maps 1–3, `REACH4` 12); the relief's `MAX_TERRACE` 6 (`BORDER` a tile wider), the walls' colours deepening up to `MOUNTAIN_TERRACE` 5 as before. Earlier cuts the owner turned down: two tiles wide from the old peak west to a pool on the east-south-east edge (diagonal, its falls on walls the camera does not see, its water out of nowhere); then a cone rising a level a tile, its water out of its top, its falls over settled rock (levitating), the quartz line climbing its terraces. Maps 1–3 keep their tarn. Mapgen: `peak` raises the summit, `crown` takes the higher of it and the Crown (the ledge flat at its level); `classify` reaches the summit past `crownReach` (`summit.reach2`: the rivers, chasms and deposits keep to the Crown's reach as before) and gives the basin first (`WET`/`FIXED`, still water), then the stream's tiles, `within` its segment: at `springLevel` — the lowest the land stands along the line from the head to the tile, so the water only falls —, `WET`/`FIXED` (never settled: a fall at each step down), its way in the biome code (a river tile's way wins over the floor's and the Crown's codes); its banks, the ledge and the summit `MASSIF` (never settled: the water between walls at least its height); the pool is the floor's lake (`floorGround`, as map 1's); `tarnWater` is dry where there is a spring. `MapGen.spring` / `TerrainSource.spring` ({x, y, level}: the basin's middle, level 6); `tarn` null on map 4. The late base's potteries (scenario-late.ts) stand on the ledge by the falls: `streamLedges` follows the stream from its tile nearest the spring, the first tile at each level it comes down to, then its pool; the oven on the ledge at its level, the pump on the stream's bank, the conduit, excavators on the Crown's foot, the ceramic down the terraces into a silo and on into the Core (lands 1–3: ceramic made over a day). The debug bases, for the spring's north layout (scenarios.ts, scenario-quartz.ts, dev.ts): the quartz line crosses a line on a junction when its way round is over twice its straight way (`routeOver`'s `cross`; land 2: the tutorial's boiler by the pool, its coal line along the floor's rim from the west, walled the west vein's way: the line went right round the Core); the quartz works are laid before the iron's overflow into the Core (on land 2 that line took the wire line's last free side); the debug turbines keep off the land above the mountains (`windSpots`: the summit is the windiest spot, and no mast's wire reaches it). The water's look (art/gaps.ts): `WATER` (the mesas' family: sand through the shallows, the deep towards the ink's violet, the ink's current lines); a broad river deeper two and three tiles from its banks and still water's middle (a capped distance to the bank, `DEPTH_MAX` 3, the same both sides of a chunk seam), feathered as the joins are (D94); the sand through the shallows along every bank and wet sand past it; `paintMarks`: ink current lines along the flow with paper highlights, glints on the deep, broken foam against a river's banks, foam where streams meet, at a fall's lip and boiling where it lands (`plunge`), short ripples on still water (no line along its depth); marks drawn a tile past the chunk in world order. Tests: mapgen.test.ts « the spring on the Crown » (a spring and no tarn, a plateau at least 6 high over every mountain, flat round the basin, its rim never below the water, its stream down in falls never climbing, into its pool, floor pump tiles near the Core, clear of the ore, the same for every player; map 3's cirque kept but the summit, the ledge and the banks; the mountains and massifs up to `MOUNTAIN_LEVEL`); water-seam.test.ts; scenarios.test.ts (the potteries' ceramic on lands 1–3). Shots: `node scripts/water-shots.mjs` (`TAG=before|after`; `ONLY=summit,basin` for the plateau) **The stream's channel (2026-10-17; owner, of the stream coming down the Crown: « the visuals are still a little bit broken »; of a fall: « a bit weird »):** the water was lost at the foot of the ledge's fall (a floating square, then nothing, then a flat band farther down), its falls landed on bare rock, a long straight dark line ran down the lower terrace. Causes: a raised stream tile is a rock column whose top is water, and where a bank beside it stood lower than the water, the relief's chamfers (the corner cuts that make diagonal fronts straight walls) halved its top (the level-2 tile under the ledge: a sliver, the rest a rock ledge); a tile a fall lands on counted the water above it as not water, so its back corners were cut (a pentagon beside the fall); and the line was the Crown's 1-to-2 terrace front running along the camera's diagonal, its wall edge-on. Fixes: mapgen `classify`, a bank within the stream's band (`halfWidth` + 4 quarter tiles) stands at least at the water beside it (`springLevel`; raised where the Crown or the floor there is lower, the pool giving up the tile beside the stream's last raised one): the stream runs in a channel and its column drops only along its way; terrain.ts `columnLook` gives a stream tile `TopLook.from`, the streams pouring into it from higher up; rock.ts `streamTop`: a channel — the middle deeper (`MIDDLE`), the far banks' inner walls (`BANK`), the near banks' inked lip, currents —, `from` keeping that side open and its corners whole, the landing's foam and rings (`landing`), the sheet as wide as the channel with veiled, wavering edges; a south-west front seen edge-on gets talus and its shade at its foot and a lit lip (any land, any map). Tests: mapgen.test.ts « its banks never stand below its water »; rock.test.ts « where a fall lands on the stream below ». Shots: `ONLY=foot node scripts/water-shots.mjs` (`TAG=before-l1|after-l1`, lands 1–3). **The basin's outflow and the streams up on the rock as one body (2026-10-18; owner: « cascade is still slightly broken here, there's a brown stripe inside the lake »; « it is also a bit broken when the water goes straight towards the camera »):** see D135. |

| D118 | **Research gated by the Core's level, Tier 1 on data alone, AI tokens a flow from Tier 2: no research has a minimum time — a project ends when its data has reached the lab and, from Tier 2, its tokens are in, from the claim's servers and from a flow bought from CrierBelt when the player launches it** (owner, 2026-10-17: « Aussi, il faut que le Tier 2 soit montré comme bloqué tant que le core n'est pas niveau 3, etc. Et il doit être séparé du Tier 1 clairement. Et pour les tokens, voici ce que l'on va faire. Ils ne sont pas mentionnés dans le Tier 1, et le Tier 1 n'a pas de temps. À partir du Tier 2, il faut avoir un nombre de tokens pour finir une recherche. Pour obtenir des tokens, soit on a son propre serveur qui en génère un flux par minute, soit on achète ce flux sur le marché. Lorsque la recherche est sélectionnée et lancée par l'utilisateur, on lui demande s'il veut acheter un flux de tokens sur le marché. Combien, et le coût qui va avec. Le temps pour débloquer la recherche dépend uniquement du temps pour acheminer la data au labo, et pour avoir le nombre de tokens requis. »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | D68 paid a project's data and tokens under an allowance over a minimum time: a node could not end before its minutes whatever the base brought, and its tokens were billed each minute as it ran, CrierBelt's at its price, with no say from the player; D105 made them free before the servers. Tier 2 opened on two expertises (D93) with nothing in the tree saying the Core's level 3 stood behind them, and the tier bands were a gutter apart | **Tiers by the Core** (`BAL.tierCoreLevel` [1, 3, 4, 5]; `tierCoreOpen`; `researchBlock` → `core`, after `prereq`, before `expertise`): Tier 2 waits for the Core's level 3, Tier 3 for 4, Tier 4 for 5. A new **level 5** (`BAL.hearthUpgrades[3]`: after Codex Lore `tr8`, 120 lenses, 60 thermal cores, 30 atlases; `hearthCap` 80 000, core 740 kW and panel 320 kW, the fog 92 tiles round), its north-west corner piece a placeholder: the uplink, a lattice mast with a dish and a cyan lamp, and a crown of lamps on the cornice (render/art/recipes, `HEART_CORNERS`, `heartLook`). Every level is the player's own upgrade paid in items, as D101's level 2, behind the trunk node that closes the tier before (the Synthesis → 3, Atlas Lore → 4, Codex Lore → 5); `validateContent` checks each tier's level exists, comes with its gate, never falls, and that Tier 2's is the Archive's. The expertises are kept: the Archive at level 3 and two chosen still open Tier 2. A tier where a claim already holds a node, learnt or running, stays open to it: no older claim loses a tier, and nothing is given. **Tier 1**: `DEPTH_COST` depths 1–2, `tr1` and `tr_synth` take 0 tokens and no pace (validated): a node ends the tick its data is in its lab (`advanceProject` takes what is owed out of a powered lab's store at once; an unpowered lab takes nothing); tokens are mentioned nowhere for it. **Tier 2+**: every project's `dur` is 0; a node's `minutes` is only the pace the launch dialog aims at (`effectiveCost` → `aim`). A project carries `tokens` (needed), `tokIn` (delivered), `buy` (tokens a minute bought). At each minute's end `tokenEpoch` delivers the servers' minute to the projects **in the order they started** (the oldest first, each to its need; what nobody needs is lost, or rented back with Server Rental), then each project's bought flow, `min(buy, need)`, billed at `tokenPriceK` from the purse — so the flow stops by itself once the project has its tokens — and the projects whose data and tokens are in finish there (`state.step`; the catch-up at the same place). Commands: `research` carries `buy`; `tokenBuy {id, perMin}` (`setTokenBuy`) changes it, capped at the need. A queued start (the Research Queue) buys nothing. **A purse in the red holds the bought flows only** (`researchHeld`, its toast and absence incident reworded); data and the servers' tokens go on; starting a Tier 2+ node needs the purse out of the red (`debt`), Tier 1 never costs. The default flow (`tokenBuyDefault`): the node's tokens over its pace, less the servers' rate it would get now (none while an older project takes them), up the ladder `BAL.tokenFlow` (500, 750, 1 000, 1 500, 2 000, 3 000, 5 000, 7 500…). The total cost of a node's tokens does not change with the flow (10 ◉ the thousand): a faster flow pays the same sooner, only the servers' share is free. Server Rental and the Compute Plan stay (both still act on the flow); D105's rule is gone (`tokensBilled`). An expertise of research « time » (Geology, Methods) now takes its share off the data and the tokens, which the time follows; Inference's first rank comes with Tier 2's first nodes. Read-only helpers for the UI: `tokenFlows`, `tokenEtas`, `tokenFlowSteps`, `flowStepAtLeast`; ui/tree-state.ts `projectTimings` (data ‰, tokens ‰, flows, when the tokens are in, what it waits for). **Offline**: the same `tokenEpoch` and `finishProjects` at the boundary; a powered lab's owed data is a sink in the catch-up (`sinkKeys`: its level fixed, a FULL throttle passing the inflow), the arrival of its last item an event (`nextEvent`): research.test.ts's twins agree bit for bit (`hash()`) with a server and two bought flows, and a Tier 2 project waiting on its data, then on its tokens, finishes at the same minute. **Saves**: v18 (`researchFlows`): a project under way keeps the data it paid and loses its minimum time; a Tier 1 one (`TIER1_V17`) loses its tokens and finishes as soon as its data is paid; a Tier 2+ one keeps its tokens as those it needs and counts what it had used as delivered (the allowance's share of its time run, or what was counted if more); the tokens used and not yet billed are forgiven; a project from before tokens is rebased at load on today's table (its share of data and of tokens taken as in). Tests: persist/research-d118.test.ts (a save 17 mid-research at Tier 1 and at Tier 2). **The Core's data cap** (the lead's addition, owner: « pour la data, le cœur ne devrait pouvoir stocker que 200 max »): the Core holds at most `BAL.hearthDataCap` = 200 of each data item (`kind: 'science'`), whatever its level; other items keep `hearthCap`. `ReservoirNode.caps` / `capOf(item)` (compile.ts `hearthCaps`, `Sim.hearthCapOf`) are read by its accept (lines bringing data wait), `steady.ts`'s FULL pool and the catch-up's bounds, room, times and trim; the Core makes no file past it (`dataTick`), and at the cap offline its own files refill what leaves first, the lines sharing the rest, as online. A save above it keeps it and takes none until under. Tests: research.test.ts (the Core alone to the cap online ≈ offline bit for bit, a save at 300 untouched; a line backing up, nothing lost). The Stock panel's data rows fill toward 200, « plein » there, with a line « Données : 200 au plus de chacune » under the section. **UI**: the tree ([ui.md](ui.md#the-tech-tree-screen)): each band from Tier 2 headed « Tier 2 · Cœur niveau 3 » with the Core's look at that level, padlocked and coral, the band hatched and its cards locked while the Core is short; a wider gap with an ink double rule and a padlock disc between Tier 1 and Tier 2; the pinned names the same; a locked node's detail « Cœur niveau 3 requis »; Tier 1's cards and detail data alone, Tier 2+'s data and tokens with their ≈ ◉, no time anywhere; a running project's tokens row, its flows now and − / + on its bought flow. **The launch dialog** (ui/launch-dialog.ts, the D89 kit): « Lancer » on a Tier 2+ node shows the tokens needed, the servers' flow and the share it would get, a slider over the ladder from the default, the ◉ a minute and the total, the expected end (the later of its tokens' and its data's, the data at the base's production), « Lancer » / « Annuler »; Tier 1 starts at once. The HUD's chips: two small bars, data (turquoise) and tokens (lilac, Tier 2+), each after its mark, what it waits for or when its tokens will be in, its tokens a minute; coral « tokens en pause » while its bought flow is held. The purse's card: « Flux de tokens acheté » (its tokens and ◉ a minute, the servers' share on its chip). The lab's inspector: tokens delivered of those needed. Shots: scripts/research-shots.mjs (the grown base's locked Tier 2, a Tier 1 detail, the late base's launch dialog and a running project, FR and EN). **Debug bases**: the tutorial level opens with its lab's next node a second in (Tier 1 is done once its data is: the Core's backlog); the grown base, its Core at level 2, sees Tier 2 locked; the late base raises its Core to 5 (every tier open) and starts the Research Queue (`an_queue`) on its two servers and the default bought flow: its tokens in within ten minutes, its Mechanics Data still to come (the Fabrique's lab gets it) — research/d118-bases.test.ts; scenarios.test.ts 8/8 with D85's checks. **Amended by D122**: Tier 1 runs on tokens too (2 000–5 000 a node, the Core's compute from its level 2), every node pays resources at launch and opens the launch dialog, and the Core's data cap and the data's place in the flow are gone. |
| D120 | **Every building has a sound signature: fuller when it stands, softer when it is selected** (owner, 2026-10-17: « Il faudrait un petit sound design pour chaque machine, quand on la construit, le même son ou similaire quand on clique dessus ») | The base's machines all sounded alike (the same placement thunk, the same selection glint); a sound per machine tells the ear what was built or picked, and hearing the same sound at both moments teaches it | `src/audio/signatures.ts` (pure): 19 signatures, mapped by building kind (`KIND_SIGNATURE`) and overridden by id (`ID_SIGNATURE`), so a new building gets its kind's (the table test covers every building); `src/audio/signature-sfx.ts`: one recipe each from `tone` / `hiss` (plus an amplitude modulation for grinds, thrums and grit), short (0.2–0.6 s to −26 dB), its tonal parts on the chord in force in a register fixed per signature. **Build**: when the building stands — at once on the claim, when its kite lands off it (near the camera only); the shell reads `place` commands off the colony's log and watches the sites. A machine's build has a set-down thump and takes the `place` thunk's place; a line piece's or a pole's light click joins it, and a ribbon drag's ticks stand for it. **Pick**: a building selected (a site keeps the glint), 60 % as long, ~8 dB softer. **Restraint**: one per signature and variant per frame (counts merged), two a frame at most, each spaced (`SigLimiter`: 250 ms per build, 450 ms for line pieces, 180 ms per pick and 120 ms between any two picks); faded by the distance to the screen's centre (`spatialGain`: a third at the edge, nothing half a screen beyond; the player's own act never under 35 %) and panned; on the effects slider; silent while sound is locked or hidden (nothing replayed later). Levels measured offline in Chrome (`scripts/sound-levels.mjs`): machines ≈ −13.5 dBFS built, ≈ −22 picked; line pieces −26 / −32 ([audio.md](audio.md), "Building signatures") |
| D121 | **A leap takes its length in time: a line has a cell over each tile it passes over — a skybridge's gap, a junction — so an item crosses at its line's pace** (owner, 2026-10-17: « la vitesse sur le bridge, la junction, est plus rapide que sur les tapis d'entrée et de sortie, c'est bizarre ») | The engine moved an item from a skybridge's start to its far end, or from the ribbon before a junction to the one after it, in one step, and the view eased it over the whole way: measured at 60 frames a second, a plain line ran 0.1 tile a tick on its ribbons, 0.2 through a junction and over a 1-tile gap, 0.3 over 2, 0.4 over 3 (×2 to ×4: 3.2 px a tick in plan on the ribbons, up to 12.8 over the deck); a swift line likewise. The engine's way was chosen, not the view's: the view cannot slow an item the engine has already moved on without drawing every item downstream late (the engine says where each item is, D57) | **Engine** ([compile.ts](../src/sim/graph/compile.ts), [chain.ts](../src/sim/flow/chain.ts)): after tracing, a tile in `graph.jumps` is followed in its chain by a cell for each tile it passes over, coded `spanTile(tile, way)` (`SPAN_TILE × (1 + way) + tile`: past the world's tiles, one per way, so the two lines through a junction each have theirs); `Chain.lastTile` (its last world tile) gives the head's way on. Throughput stays the line's (one item a step); latency and capacity are the leap's real ones (a 4-tile skybridge: 4 steps, 3 items over its gap). Offline as online (the same chains); saves: an older save's lines are found by their world tiles and their items go back on them (`deserialize`, [persistence.md](persistence.md)). **View** ([item-flow.ts](../src/render/world/item-flow.ts)): a piece a tile — the start rising, a stretch of deck a tile, the far end coming down; the north–south line's cell over a junction rides its hump, the east–west line's runs under it. The cells over a leap are left out of the Logistique view's paths, as the leaps were; its badges and the panel's « next jam » read `lastTile`. After: 0.1 everywhere on a plain line, 0.2 on a swift one ([item-speed.test.ts](../src/render/world/item-speed.test.ts): both axes, gaps of 1–3; bridges.test.ts: the cells, an item's time across, a full line's 60/min, online = offline, saves old and new). **Backed up** (owner, the same day: « quand la file est pleine, les objets devraient pouvoir patienter sur le pont »): the owner's build (port 5184, at 8bf2ea9) predates D121 — no cells over the gap, so a full line left the deck empty. With D121 a blocked line fills the cells over the gap too, one item a tile, as a ribbon of its tier holds (bridges.test.ts: a blocked line, all 13 cells full, the 4 over the leaps, online = offline, then unblocked). Its tokens were drawn on `itemsOver`, over every building, so one waiting behind a tall building in front of the deck showed through it. Now the model says which bridge an item is drawn over (`OVER` in item-flow.ts: a junction's stays over the buildings); an item over a skybridge's span (its ramps, its deck, the ribbons' last and first stretch at its ends) sorts among the volumes just after the span's piece it rides ([belt-lift.ts](../src/render/world/belt-lift.ts) `spanZ`, by its front-most point when that is on the span too; the pieces' keys `rampZ` and `deckSquareZ` in [stand.ts](../src/render/world/stand.ts), which `addDecks` lays them by), and in plan sits over every plan sprite, the deck's among them (`PLAN_Z.spans`). item-speed.test.ts: a token still on each square at the deck's height, keyed just after its square, a silo in front keyed after the one it hides; [bridge-shots.mjs](../scripts/bridge-shots.mjs) before / after (iso near and mid, plan) |
| D122 | **No data: research is paid in AI tokens at every tier and in resources paid from the Core at launch; the lab takes no input; the Core's level-2 corner is a compute bay giving research a token flow** (owner, 2026-10-17: « On va enlever le principe de la data. Le laboratoire n'a plus besoin d'entrée, et on n'a plus besoin de générer de la data. La monnaie pour débloquer les recherches sera uniquement les tokens, et des fois on aura besoin de certaines ressources qui font sens, type engrenages, cuivre etc. à la place de la data. »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | Research ran on data items carried by ribbon to the lab's three inputs (D68, D71, D84: Survey Data from the Core's outlet (D86) and the collectors, Mechanics Data from the test bench, atlases and codices from the scriptorium), on top of tokens from Tier 2 (D118): a second production chain whose only use was research, a 200 cap at the Core for it (D118), Tier 1 on data alone and a tutorial step that was a ribbon from the Core to the lab | **Content**: the items `journal_1`, `journal_2`, `atlas`, `codex` (kind `science`) are gone, and the buildings `data_collector`, `data_relay`, `test_bench` (`RETIRED_COSTS`, `RETIRED_NAMES`), the recipes `journal_2`, `atlas`, `codex` (the scriptorium keeps resin canvas), `SCIENCE` / `scienceCost`. `DEPTH_COST` is `{ tokens, minutes }`: Tier 1 2 000 and 5 000 tokens at a pace of 2 and 5 minutes (Mechanics 2 000, the Synthesis 10 000 over 10), Tier 2+ as before. A node's `cost` is its **resources** (`res`, one to three kinds) paid from the Core at launch, picked so they hint at what it unlocks (the Electric Smelting copper wire and plates, Mechanics plates and wire, the Server Room circuits and oscillators, Tier 2 gears, circuits, servomotors, ceramic, Tier 3 lenses and thermal cores, Tier 4 resonators, nacre lattices, robot arms; D123 swapped some for its steel, bricks, pipes, cables, copper plates): validate.ts checks that its prerequisites and the plans make them (`makeableWith`), usable.ts that they can be had before it; every node takes tokens and a pace. The lab (`observatory`, 3×3) has no port; the autonomous lab computes 5 000 tokens a minute like a server (`server` on a lab); the Core lost its out port. **The Core's compute**: `BAL.coreCompute` 0, 1 000, 1 500, 2 000, 3 000 tokens a minute by level (`Sim.coreComputeRate`, no power, no ribbon), mounted at level 2 as the compute bay on D86's corner; « Surcadençage » / Overclocking (`an_data`, it brought the data collector) adds 500 (`coreCompute`), Data Mining 2 000; the Data Relay `an_relay` is retired; `tr6` is « Optique » / Optics, `tr8` « Résonance » / Resonance, Analytics' Tier 1 « Traitement »; the Core's level 5 costs 20 resonators instead of 30 atlases; `BAL.hearthDataCap`, `hearthDataLevel`, `hearthDataBase` are gone. Expertises: aspect `data` → `resources`, research « time » takes off tokens alone; Archiving is earned by research learnt (12, 30, 55) and raises the claim's own tokens (`tokenRate`: the Core's compute, the servers, the autonomous labs). The Crier's colonies no longer trade data (oscillators, lenses, nacre lattices in their lines). **Engine** (systems/research.ts): `researchBlock` gains `unaffordable` (the Core short of a resource) and `reserve` (CrierBelt's restart reserve, D36, as a plan); `debt` only refuses a bought flow from a purse in the red (`startResearch`). Launching pays the resources (counted as used); cancelling hands them back. `tokenEpoch` gives each project whose lab had power during the minute (`Project.lit`, ‰·ticks summed like the servers' output: `labTick`, online a tick, offline a segment) in the order they started: the free pool — the Core's compute, then the servers' minute —, then the bank (`Sim.tokenBank`), then its bought flow; Server Rental pays on the servers' unused share only; `TokenStats` gains `fromCore`, `fromBank`, `core`. A queued node starts at a project's end or at the minute's end its resources are in (`queuedReady`). Labs compile as power-only nodes (`busyLabs`); `dataMakers`, `dataTick`, `dataAcc`, `labAccepts`, `owedItems`, `dataPermille`, `LAB_CAP`, `hearthCaps`, `HEART_OUTLET` and the catch-up's lab sinks and data inflows are gone; archive notes pay tokens. Online ≈ offline bit for bit (research.test.ts: the Core, a server, the bank, two bought flows, a queued start). **Saves**: v19 (`dataRetired`): a project that paid data counts it as tokens delivered at `RETIRED_ITEM_TOKENS` (Survey Data 1 100, Mechanics Data 5 900, an atlas 18 800, a codex 101 400: their value at CrierBelt's price, generous), a Tier 1 one given Tier 1's tokens (`TIER1_TOKENS_V19`), the excess banked (`tokens.bank`), no resources owed; a project in today's shape is left; `an_relay` forgotten (`RESEARCH_RETIRED[19]`); `dataAcc` dropped. The remap banks every data item held anywhere — the Core, stores, labs, ribbons, buffers, deliveries — instead of losing it (`RemapNote.converted`, a line on the absence report) and takes the collectors, relays and test benches down, refunded; a lab's store goes to the Heart. Tests: persist/data-d122.test.ts (the step; the tutorial base at Tier 1 and the late base at Tier 2 forged as saves 18 with data in the Core, on a ribbon, in a test bench mid-test, a collector, a relay, the Data Relay learnt, projects mid-way), and research-d118, core-data, docks-d108 through the chain. **UI**: the tree's cards and detail show each node's resources (coral while the Core lacks them; a `poor` state) and its tokens ≈ ◉ at every tier; the launch dialog opens at every tier — resources have / need, tokens, the Core's compute and the servers as free flows (and the bank), the bought flow locked at 0 from a purse in the red, the end from tokens alone; the HUD chip is one token bar (« sans énergie » for an unpowered lab); the Stock panel loses its data rows and cap; the Core's inspector says « Calcul du Cœur · N tokens/min »; the absence report « Données converties en tokens »; 42 i18n keys gone, 38 new. `FL.feedLab` → `FL.bankTokens`. **Art**: the compute bay (`computeBayIso` / `computeBayTop`, `HeartPiece` `'compute'`, `COMPUTE_BAY_LEVEL`) where the data outlet stood, the lab's sample bay where its hopper stood, the autonomous lab's racks, the scriptorium's canvas bolts; the retired buildings' drawings, the four data glyphs and `portDrawn` are gone. **Quests**: `field_journal` is « Le laboratoire »: every plan, the lab powered, Mechanics launched (the `{ outlet }` spot is gone); « Le Cœur grandit » mounts the compute bay. **Debug bases**: the tutorial's lab east of the Core, unfed; no collectors (the grown base, the Fabrique's `J`); `Builder.affordNode`; the late base runs the Research Queue on the Core, two servers and a bought flow, the Fabrique's lab the Solar Farm (research/d122-bases.test.ts). Docs: [research.md](research.md) |
| D123 | **The basic resources make more: copper plates, steel, bricks, pipes and cables — five Tier 1 products, each wanted by the buildings, the Core's levels and the research that come after it** (owner, 2026-10-17: « Il faut rajouter des trucs à faire avec les ressources de base.
