Costs: 25 research-unlocked buildings of Tiers 1–3 (electric smelter, twin wire mill, excavators, ceramic oven, assembly workshops, accumulators, pylon, server, cooled server, sifter, backfiller, battery charger and generator, deep bore, giant drill, high-pressure pump, great turbine, solar farm, geothermal plant, forging press, arc furnace, scriptorium); the Core's level 3 adds 120 bricks, 40 steel, 30 copper plates (gears 150 → 100), level 4 150 steel, 300 bricks, 60 pipes, 60 cables (circuits 200 → 150); research's resources (D122) ask for them where a node's closure makes them. The late debug base lays both works where its cirque leaves room (`basicsWorks`, src/sim/scenario-basics.ts: early in its build, each whole or rolled back and reported `steel` / `bricks`, which its test lets go). What stood before refunds its old price: `costFloor` (saved, optional; absent = a pre-D123 save, floor = its `nextId`), `COSTS_BEFORE_D123` ([content/past-costs.ts](../src/content/past-costs.ts)), `refundCost` ([sim/refund.ts](../src/sim/refund.ts)) wherever a refund is counted; no save version. | D124 | **A skybridge never passes over a tall building, and a tall building never stands under a bridge's span** (owner, 2026-10-17: « Il faut empêcher de placer un bridge par-dessus une machine, à moins que cette machine soit vraiment plate. Le bridge n'est pas très haut, donc actuellement en isométrique il traverse la machine ») | Before: a pair leapt anything, so in the isometric view the low deck cut through a machine. Now **every building has a height class** (`BUILDING_FLAT` in [registry.ts](../src/content/registry.ts); content: `FLAT_KINDS` in [buildings.ts](../src/content/buildings.ts), overridable per def with `flat`): **flat** are ribbons of every tier, routing blocks, junctions, bridges, conduits and fences; **tall** is everything else — machines, drills, pumps, stores, generators, turbines, solar plants (not judged low enough), labs, servers, the trade post, masts and pylons, the Core — and any new kind until it is listed. `canPlace` refuses with `bridge_over_tall` (FR/EN text) a bridge whose span to the pair's partner (the first bridge ahead of its way, within `4 + mods.bridgeSpan`, on its level; and the one behind it, now paired with it) holds a tall building, and a tall footprint with a tile under any pair's span, on all four ways. The drag planner never plans such a leap: `leap` marks the tall tiles `bridge_over_tall` and the run stays refused, the drag laid in part up to it (the planner follows the dragged path, it does not re-route it: the player drags round). **Saves are sacred**: pairs already standing over machines stay (nothing is removed), the check is for new placements only; logged placements re-check the same way, so replay is unchanged. The debug bases lay no pair over a machine (D85's money checks hold). Mast and pylon count as tall although slim: simple, and the safe default | [belt-plan.test.ts](../src/sim/belt-plan.test.ts): the check both ways, the planner stopping at a tall tile while a flat fence is leapt, an old pair over a mast loading unchanged |
| D125 | **Machine waste: a smelter's metal recipes and the steel mill put out slag, the press scrap, every few crafts, through the same output as their product; the line carries both and the player sorts it — burnt, sold cheap or recycled; the incinerator bursts when it burns; « Les scories » in the tutorial** (owner, 2026-10-18: « l'incinérateur doit avoir une petite animation quand il brûle quelque chose. Aussi il faudrait que certaines machines produisent des déchets en alternance avec leur vraie production, qu'il faille traiter »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | Every line carried one item from producer to consumer: a smelter fed a press or a mill and nothing ever needed sorting, so the sorter and the incinerator (D51) were tools for mistakes, and the incinerator's flame lit only for items a ribbon handed in (D67) — a sorter's side set against it, the layout waste sorting makes, never flared | **Content**: items `slag` « Scories » and `scrap` « Chutes » (kind `waste`, tier 1, appended; 0.2 ◉ and 0.6 ◉, depth 300 and 200: sellable for little; a foundry supplies them, mining camps and salt ports buy slag for fill, ember forges scrap to remelt). `RecipeDef.waste` = `[item, every]`: `iron_plate` and `arc_plate` a slag every 10 crafts (the tutorial's first smelter: one a minute, gentle), `copper_plate` and `arc_copper` every 5, `steel` every 5, `gear` a scrap every 2, `pipe` every 5; glass melts clean. **No ash**: the coal generator and the steam boiler have no item output, and giving them one would stop a base's power on a full bin — slag and scrap are enough to sort. Recycling, the third way out: `slag_brick` « Brique de scories » at the brickworks (2 slag + 1 water a brick, 4 s, `ex_brick`) and `steel_scrap` « Acier de récupération » at the steel mill (3 scrap + 1 coal a steel, 12 s, `dn_steel`, itself a slag every 5) — two recipes each now, so they wait for a choice (D83); D70's three at most and D107's usability hold (`recipeMakes` counts the waste as made). Validated: a waste item is some recipe's waste, never a product; `every` ≥ 2 divides each of its machines' crafts a minute and `WASTE_BEAT` = 10 (a smelter's ten a minute). **Engine**: `MachineNode` counts its crafts (`wc`); the craft completing the count drops one waste into a bin (`wBuf`, `wItem`, `BAL.wasteBufItems` = 2; full, the machine waits, `Blocked`), and the bin goes out **first, through the product's output**, a whole item: a waste at the head of a line into a machine that does not take it jams the line like any wrong item. Hashed only where there is any; an empty bin holds no item; saved in the node's snapshot; refunded with the rest (`refundNode`, `setRecipe`), held in `totalHeld`, counted in `produced`. The Core takes waste like any item (its own room, the Stock panel's « Déchets » section saying where it goes instead). **Offline**: the count is state, so `every` divides the lines' usual rates — a scrap every six gears made a press fed by one smelter repeat in nine minutes, past the warm-up (approx); every two keeps three (exact); the run's `push` set holds the waste. **Incinerator**: `SinkNode` keeps a render-only record of its last burn (`burnTick`, `burnItem`, `burnPort`, `burns`; not hashed or saved; a warm-up's rollback clears it); the pit bursts when it really burns — the flame up, four inked sparks out of the throat (two hot, two in the item's colour), a puff of smoke a beat later, the flame's tip tinted by the item for half a second (`world/pit-burst.ts`) — for a sorter's side or a machine set against it (at `burnTick + 1`) as for a ribbon (when its item has fallen in), idle when nothing burns, glowing at night; a pit fed every two seconds or faster keeps its flame up between flares (`steadyHeat`) and bursts at most every half second; three pooled burst slots a pit, laid once with its sprites, culled with it (render.md); the shell plays the incinerator's signature soft (D120's picked variant at 45 %, at most once every 1.5 s a pit, in view only). **UI**: the inspector's equation shows the waste beside the product (« [plate] 10 + [slag] 1 /min »), its buffers the waste bin, a machine blocked by its waste names it; the building card's recipes list it. **Tutorial**: « Les scories » (`slag`, goal, `any`) after « La Criée » — the copper wire for a sorter is to hand by then —, revealing the Sorter and Incinerator plans: slag led to an incinerator (`feeds`: a waste's makers count as its producers) or 5 slag sold; 30 plates, 10 copper wire; `QUEST_TABLE` 5, `QUEST_ADDED` (done when a later quest was). **Debug bases** (`scenario-waste.ts`): `sortWaste` lays a sorter set to the product and an incinerator on its side on a mixed line (the tutorial's plate line, the late base's smelter → steel mill, a silo's line), `filterFeed` filters a router's side toward a machine to its input (the grown base's gear press, the steel line's pipe press), the Fabrique's press turns its scrap onto the sell line (an inverted sorter); D85's checks and the money tests hold on lands 1–3. Neighbouring foundries and ember forges stand an incinerator beside their hub. **Saves**: the items appended; a machine's buffers keep their contents (its recipe's slots unchanged), its bin empty and its count fresh; the remap re-points or refunds a bin. Tests: waste.test.ts, quests.test.ts (the scripted run sorts the slag), scenarios.test.ts, pits.test.ts. **D139** turned this: the Core takes no waste in, only the steel line leaves any, and the tutorial's slag is the starter contract's. **D151** took the recyclers out (owner: « don't use slag or scraps in machines, the point is that it's very useless »): no recipe takes waste in, and it is burnt or sold cheap |

At each boundary, inside `tradeEpoch` after the posts' sales, `autoSales` sells each item's whole items beyond `autoSellKeep` — its threshold, never above the Core's cap, raised to an open plan's or a queued research's need of it (never sold into a shortage), never below the restart reserve (D36) — at the epoch's bid less `BAL.coreSellFee` (D126); booked like a post's sales (`t.sold`: the market sees the supply; `earned`, `soldTotal`) and as an `autoSell` ledger line from the Core, a recurring `autoSell` kind of the purse's journal (D102). Tests: [autosell.test.ts](../src/sim/autosell.test.ts) (bounds, refusals, replay; the sale at 60 % of the bid; a full Core's blocked drill against an auto-sold one running on; what it keeps; saves; online ≈ offline over two hours, bit for bit), purse-facts.test.ts (the `auto` lines in the sum) |

The six families' ids stay as **themes** (`THEMES`): an expertise improves every category of its theme (the Foundry, the Workshop, Ceramics and Optics are Machines'; the Synthesis Analytics'; Land Registry Logistics') — no saved shape changes for them. The engine answers `gate` (after `prereq`, before `core`) while a gate is unfinished (`unmetGates`); a node learnt stays learnt in a category now locked. Validation `categoryProblems`. Save 20 (`quartzWorkshop`): a claim holding the Face Cutter's plan holds `dn_quartzshop`. drills go down first on their deposits and may stand in another building's elbow room, turned the next way round when their outlet faces rock; extractors look a parcel and a half round for their ground; the plainest chain is glass from sand, else a mine on the largest deposit; the retries: the post on the Core's other side, then (one copy) the lines laid from the mines down; a map-4 base's hub is the parcel of its square whose deposits are the largest (`depositsIn`, radii² summed). **What it does, said**: `NLayout.lines`, a line per good it sells, raw good first, read off what stands (back from the store through each machine's main input, `mainInput`), so the card says what the ribbons carry; The band over the base: « Sept-Cornes · Fonderie » (ui/land-facts.ts `neighbourTrade`, `neighbourBanner`); the land panel's trade tag, its lines in the tooltip. **Lands 1–9 of map 4**: 98 bases, every one selling, 5.8 machines a base (5.5 before), about 35–40 ms to lay one (51 before), two that lose their Core's line; ten lines — sand → glass 39, iron ore → plate → gear 25, iron ore 8, copper → wire → cable 7, iron → plate → steel → pipe 4, copper → plate 4, coal 4, stone 3, copper ore 3, copper → wire → coil 1 —, some 58 % working the ore they stand on. | D135 | **The water up on the rock is one body, drawn alike from any of its tiles: a stream runs as one channel whichever way it runs (straight at the camera too), its banks only where water meets land, its depth by the distance to them, its marks spaced along it, a fall's lip and sheet as wide as its water; in plan it lies at its level, as in iso** (owner, 2026-10-18, of the spring's outflow: « cascade is still slightly broken here, there's a brown stripe inside the lake »; of a stream on a massif: « it is also a bit broken when the water goes straight towards the camera like this »; defaults decided by the session, tunable in water-top.ts) | Each raised tile drew its own piece of water from its 3 × 3 alone (D94, D115): a channel along its own way with its deeper middle, a wall on its north and west banks, the same two marks on every tile of a look, the rock tiles' wedges plain blue, a three-tile corner in the lake's teal. Running straight at the camera (a stream stepping on diagonally) every tile drew a chevron of bank across the water, an X of seams where its middle met the wedges, its own mark; where it widened, steps; a fall's sheet stopped short of each tile's corners, a lip's foam across sides that do not drop. At the outflow the stream's bank wall ran into the lake. In plan the rock map counted the water up on the rock as the floor: every stream on a massif sat at the bottom of a canyon of grey steps, not as in iso | New `art/water-top.ts`. A raised tile's top (rock.ts `TopLook`: `kind`, `cells`, a stream's `flow` / `fall` / `from`, `mark`) reads the 7 × 7 round it (terrain.ts `waterCells`: still water `w` / `d`, a stream's way `0`–`7`, `x` a tile at another height a fall joins on — the foot of its fall, water or not, or a stream pouring in —, so the channel runs straight to its lip and on from its landing); from it, the same in every tile of the body: the **outline** is the floor's (paint-kit `march`: samples at the tiles' middles, 4/2/1 in 16, a stream's at least `KEEP` 0.62 as the floor's rivers, dry land's at most 0.45; saddles joined) — plan and iso draw one outline; a stream a tile wide runs in a channel about 0.64 of its tile, rock either side on its own top (its grain drawn); a lake's corners round off; a width changes over about a tile; the **depth** is the distance to the nearest bank (`depthColor`: still and running ramps over `STEPS`, round-capped strokes along the banks in the tile's frame, banks within a tile and a half), the running water's share feathered over the lake's out of the streams' tiles (gaps.ts `FEATHER`, now exported: opaque passes at 1/7 … 7/7) so an outflow carries its lake's colour and depth on; the **banks** inked within the tile and a hair (`INK_BLEED`), a far bank's wall (`BANK` 0.075, where its inner face turns to the camera, within the streams' share) only along banks — none across water, none along a stream running at the camera (its banks edge-on); the water bleeds 0.03 tile (`BLEED`) past a whole tile's edge under the next tile's, the same colour; the **marks** (terrain.ts `waterMark`, hashed from the world tile): a stream's every third tile along its way, its lanes out of step, none on a lip or a landing; still water a ripple on a tile in three; a **fall** (rock.ts `sheetOf`, water-top `lipSpan`): its sheet, lip curl and splash span the water at the lip; where the next tile's fall carries on, no veil and no gap (two side by side: one sheet); each slab's sheet runs a hair over the slab below's (no seam down a tall fall); the lip's foam only on sides that drop. A rock tile gets a top wherever the water or its ink reaches it (`reaches`) and keeps its rim, talus and layers (relief-view.ts, `paintRelief`). **Plan** (terrain.ts `paintChunk`): the floor's water first (gaps.ts `paintWater`'s new `only`), then the rock map with the water, streams and reclaimed ground up on the rock as columns at their level (`raised`), then that water over it. Drawing only: no save, map or `MAP_VERSION` change. Gone: the 3 × 3 rules of D115's channel and outflow (`poolPart`, `WEDGE`, `EXIT`, `MIDDLE`, `BLEND`, `DEPTHS`, `streamTop`, `streamWedges`, `TopLook.wet` / `deep` / `run` / `still`). Cost: a sweep of eight mid-zoom views over the water baked 943 relief frames against 743 (the tiles near water up on the rock are keyed by what is round them); nothing else changes. **Where a fall lands** (owner, 2026-10-18, of the first cut: « if there's a waterfall, there should be water where it lands. Right now sometimes water falls on a side but doesn't continue lower »): a river's biome code is the way of the segment it was traced on, and where it turns its next tile that way can be dry — 21 of the 51 sheets on map 4 hung over sand or rock at a cliff's foot while the river went on beside it, two were false falls (a stream turning at its level along a ledge, its way pointing over it), and the floor's water, painted apart from the water up on the rock, stopped short of the cliff. gaps.ts `exitOf`: a stream runs on into the tile beside it that holds water turning least from its way (straight on, ±45°, ±90°; its own level first; never uphill nor back into a tile running into it), else the next along its way; its `flow`, `fall`, `from`, the `x` cells, the plan's lip foam and plunge follow it; the floor's pass takes in the streams falling into it (terrain.ts `fallsToFloor`) so its water reaches their foot. All 61 falls now land in water. Tests: water-top.test.ts (« where a fall lands »: a river turning as it falls pours into the water beside its way and the floor's water reaches the cliff's foot; a stream turning at its level by a ledge does not fall). Left: a stream widening on the camera's diagonal still turns over about a tile, an angled step (the floor's outline; a longer taper would need it smoothed along the stream, and the flow codes do not tell a lane's head from a bend); a river that runs up onto a massif from the floor (mapgen, D94) ends against its lower neighbour with an inked lip. Tests: water-top.test.ts (a stream each of the eight ways, a widening, a bend, a lake and its outflow: every two raised tiles that meet read the same outline and depth along their shared edge, a tile with no top has no water at its edge; a straight stream keeps its width; no bank from a lake's middle down its outflow; marks every third tile, never two in a row; two falls side by side make one lip, a lone lip centred on its channel; the foot of a fall joined on; in plan the floor's pass leaves the raised stream out); rock.test.ts (the stream tests on the new top). Shots: `node scripts/stream-shots.mjs` (`TAG=before|after`, `OUT=…`, `ONLY=…`, `ZOOMS=near,mid,close,far`; spots found from the map: the outflow, a stream each way, falls towards the camera and two side by side) |

| D138 | **A young base catches up in seconds: a fuel generator's dispatch in 25 ‰ steps and a drill's rate in quarter items a minute, online and offline alike; a warm-up hashes only in phase with its start; a silo's lock follows its real level offline** (owner, 2026-10-18: « Do this task here » on the lead's follow-up « fast catch-up for young bases, silo lock »; defaults decided by the lead) | The tutorial level took 45.6 s of CPU to catch up 8 hours (968 warm-ups, 18.5 s for 2), already at 95c58c7; base20k while its silos fill 10.8 s for 4. No warm-up (4 800 ticks) found their cycle, and the sun moved the share every minute, so every minute took another full, averaged warm-up. Hashing the group at every tick was half a warm-up. And a warm-up locked the real silo it pushed into (`give` in virtual mode), while offline nothing freed a silo that ran dry until the next recompile (online `update` frees it the tick it empties) — which also kept an averaged jump stepping its old regime for nothing | [power/solve.ts](../src/sim/power/solve.ts) and the catch-up's `computeNetModes`: `dispatch` = ⌈need·1000/F⌉ rounded up to `DISPATCH_STEP` (25 ‰, constants.ts) — a generator burns at most a step of its full rate above the need (the same online and offline, so a steady load still burns the same to the milli-item), and its whole items repeat within four minutes. [graph/compile.ts](../src/sim/graph/compile.ts) `drillRate`: every extractor's rate (deposit-capped, Ore Cutting's multiplier included) floored to `DRILL_STEP_MU` = 250 mu an epoch, a quarter item a minute (a drill on four tiles of 2 270: 9 a minute, not 9.08; the deposit card still shows each tile's cap). [offline/steady.ts](../src/sim/offline/steady.ts) `warmUp`: the state is hashed only at ticks a whole number of `rotOf` turns from the start (the key holds `now % L`, so only those can match; a repeat at t and t + p repeats at every later pair, so the first in phase is found, the same period, the transient under L ticks longer — the same flows; on the tutorial the results are identical with and without it, and a night's group, L = 2 400, hashes twice instead of 4 800 times). [flow/nodes.ts](../src/sim/flow/nodes.ts) `ReservoirNode.vLocked`: a virtual session locks and reads its own copy, from `Cond.locks` (the group's silos' locks, in the regime's key) or a snapshot; [offline/catchup.ts](../src/sim/offline/catchup.ts) `relock` (each step, before `classify`): a silo whose item ran out with none on its way in is freed, a free one holding an item is locked to it, and the groups at it re-warm; an averaged jump no longer steps its old regime (identical results, checked); a throttled virtual pool hands out an item only once it holds all of it (the D139 session found a post's exit taking half an item from it and holding it staged for good). Measured (CPU, Node, the tree of 2026-10-18 evening): tutorial 2 h 18.5 → 1.7 s, 8 h 45.6 → 4.0 s (968 → 88 warm-ups); grown 8 h 5.2 → 1.4 s; late 4 h 1.5 → 0.9 s; base20k filling 4 h 10.8 → 6.6 s (the same save and `hash()`); base20k's week unchanged (1.3–1.5 s). Online ≈ offline, worst gap of an item's flow, before → after: tutorial 2 h 3.6 → 4.0 %, 8 h 2.0 → 3.5 %; grown 8 h 7.5 → 7.0 %; late 4 h 3.6 → 4.5 % (the glass lines' gaps were there before). No save change. Tests: [young-d138.test.ts](../src/sim/offline/young-d138.test.ts) (the steps, a dry silo freed offline as online, an empty one locked by its line both ways), power.test.ts, the bench's young base (8 h < 8 s) and filling base20k (4 h < 20 s under vitest) |
| D139 | **The Core takes no waste in; the machines' waste is the steel line's, the early game clean; the tutorial still teaches the sort, on the starter contract's slag** (owner, 2026-10-18: « scraps shouldn't be able to enter the core », then « don't make smelters and early game machines create slug or scraps. that's for later, but the tutorial should still show how to sort them with the sorter »; defaults decided by the session, all tunable) | D125's slag and scrap went into the Core like any item: its room filled, nothing else, so a base could pour every mixed line into the Core and never sort. And the tutorial's first smelter put out a slag every ten plates from its first minute | **The rule**: the Core's accept set is every item but kind `waste`, by content (`CORE_TAKES`, `isWaste` in [content/registry.ts](../src/content/registry.ts)), set on its node at each compile ([graph/compile.ts](../src/sim/graph/compile.ts)). It holds for a ribbon, a routing block's side and a machine's outlet set against an intake alike: the refused item waits at the door and its line backs up (the reservoir's existing `accepts`; flow/nodes.ts unchanged). Offline the same accept (a virtual reservoir reads `accepts` too): a line jammed at the Core is a steady state. **Every other way in**: the debug grant (`Sim.grant`) refuses waste and logs nothing; no quest reward may be waste (`questProblems`); the Crier's spot purchases and the orders land at a post, never in the Core. A removal's refund (a machine's bin, a ribbon's or a block's items), a delivery whose post is gone and a save's orphan still go home: nothing vanishes, and they are the player's own few items, never a line. Waste the Core held before stays, sold « Au comptant » or auto-sold (D127); no saved shape changed. **A routing block laid on a ribbon tile keeps the item standing there** (compile.ts `intoFork`; it went home before): a sorter set down where a slag waits at a door takes it in and deals it aside, the one gesture that clears a door. **Waste only later**: `steel` and `steel_scrap` (a slag every 5) and `pipe` (a scrap every 5 crafts) keep theirs. The smelters' metal (iron and copper, coal or electric) and the press's gears are clean, and their descriptions follow; recycling is unchanged (slag bricks, recycled steel; taken out by D151: waste has no use). **The tutorial**: CrierBelt's starter contract (« La Criée ») brings its ore with its slag, one free with every five ore (`BAL.starter.waste`: 8 in all). It comes in the same lots, counted as bought at no cost, and joins the post's cargo so the exit hands it out in turn; a trade epoch that grows a cargo says so, and the catch-up recompiles. The wire mill refuses the slag and the copper line stops at its door. « Les scories » (D125's place, after « La Criée »; quest table unchanged) now says so: the slag is the contract's, the Core takes none either, and the steel mill and the press will leave some later. The answer is a sorter laid over that last tile, set to the ore, and an incinerator at its side, or the slag led to the post's entrance and sold. Its arrow points at the door where something waits refused (`GuideSpot` `{ refused }`: the Core's, else a machine's). The Synthesis' bench takes its plates by a plain router again. **Shown, not told**: over each door where something waits refused (an intake of the Core; a machine's input, its recipe not taking it) hangs the refusal's badge, in every view: the item on a paper disc, ringed and struck in coral ([render/world/views-view.ts](../src/render/world/views-view.ts) `doors`, read every 8 frames from [sim/readouts/core-door.ts](../src/sim/readouts/core-door.ts); `drawRefusal` in art/view-icons.ts). On the stopped line, the inspector's « Sur le ruban » adds the badge and « Le Cœur ne prend aucun déchet » or « Refusé à l'entrée », how to sort it on hover. The Core's inspector shows a « Refusés à ses entrées » section while anything waits, and the Stock panel's waste note says the Core takes none. **Debug bases**: the starter's slag is sorted on the post's exit line before its first lot lands (scenarios.ts `starterLine`; the sorter and pit go with the line), and « Les scories » is met there. The late base's steel line sorts the mill's slag off before the pipes join it, and the press's scrap off the pipes' own line (scenario-basics.ts). The swift line's skybridge never lands where a routing block deals into it (`sideFed`). Every level is green on lands 1–3, none carries waste into the Core, and the neighbouring bases' lines carry none. **Offline**: the contract's slag makes its post's exit a two-item one, within an item of ticking as two purchases are (D62's tolerance; trade.test checks it so). Offline may leave its last slag split between the container and the ribbon, which is noted for the catch-up's rework. Tests: waste.test.ts (the refusal from a ribbon, a routing block and a machine; the sorter over the jam; a sorted line; online ≈ offline jammed and sorted), quests.test.ts (the contract's slag sorted at the wire mill), trade.test.ts, replace.test.ts, building-facts.test.ts, scenarios.test.ts |
| D140 | **One entrance a kind of input: every machine has at least as many entrances as the most kinds of input a recipe of its takes, so each comes in on a line of its own; the chip fab gets a third, at the back** (owner, 2026-10-18: « if a machine require 3 ressources, you need to put 3 entrances to it. like the electronical stuff »; defaults decided by the session) | The chip fab ran the electronics workshop's recipes on two entrances, but its circuit takes three kinds since D129 (a copper plate, two copper wires, a glass): a third kind had to share a line with another, merged before the fab. Every other machine already had one a kind (the workshop's third on its side since D129, the etcher's three at the back, the assembly and optics workshops' three; a scan of the content found the chip fab the only one short) | **Content**: `chip_fab` gains `P(0, 3, 'S', 'in')`, at the back-left — **the default chosen**: the etcher's way, three entrances in a row at the back with the outlet over the middle one; a 4×4 has at its back the room the 2×2 workshop lacked, which is why the workshop's third went on its side. It is listed **last**, after the outlet, so ports 0–2 keep their index. **Rule** (content/validate.ts `entranceProblems`, run by `validateContent`): a building with fewer `in` ports than the most distinct kinds of input of any recipe naming it fails the build — « building chip_fab: 2 entrances, but recipe circuit takes 3 kinds of input (one entrance a kind, D140) »; kinds, not quantities (two wires ride one line); a fluid counts (its conduit takes an entrance). **Saves**: no step and no `SAVE_VERSION` bump — a save holds no machine's port: the content table has building ids, not their ports; ribbons are keyed by tier, last tile and length, machines' state by entity, and `portItems` is only the Core's and the posts'. A fab already standing has its third entrance at load. What already faces it: a ribbon passing across the tile it faces (a line hugging the back) is not joined, the entrance has nothing connected; a ribbon that ended against that wall (a dead end before), or a store's or a machine's outlet set against it (direct insertion, owner 2026-10-12), now hands in as at any entrance — only what a recipe of the fab takes goes in, the rest stays on the line or in the store as it stood; a router beside it may now give there too. Nothing is lost either way. **Art**: a third load lock over the new intake, iso and plan (render.md §9 note 19); its mark, the building card's diagram and the lit and switched-off looks come from the content. **Bases**: no debug base lays a chip fab (the late base's electronics are workshops and an etcher), so no scenario changed; a neighbouring base builds a family's first machine (`machineOf`), so its circuits would be the workshop's, three entrances already — D134's « circuits and motors are never laid » is room on the land, not entrances, and stays; `connect` (sim/dev.ts) reaches the new entrance as inlet 2. Tests: [chip-fab-d140.test.ts](../src/sim/chip-fab-d140.test.ts) (its ports; three lines, one an entrance, make circuits for the Core; a fab standing with a line under its new entrance loads and runs as the one that never saved, `hash()` equal, the line's plates all at the Core; a store against the new entrance hands in glass and keeps iron plates, nothing lost), validate.test.ts (the rule; a fab one short fails); shots: `scripts/chipfab-shots.mjs` |
| D141 | **A line's lift keeps its line's pace: a traction ribbon goes at the pace of the fastest ribbon it meets on its line, and never under a plain ribbon's 60/min** (owner, 2026-10-18: « la partie jaune des tapis ascenseur sont très lents et causent des bouchons »; the lead's brief: the lift never the bottleneck of its line, 60/min at least, a swift or express line's pace on such a line; defaults decided by the session) | A traction ribbon carried 30 items a minute (D45), half a plain ribbon, so every line that climbed or came down a terrace was halved at its lift and backed up behind it: on a ridge, a plain line and a swift line alike carried 30 a minute, full behind each lift, a half and a quarter full past it (`scripts/lift-shots.mjs`, `TAG=before`). Of the two ways out the brief offered, neither was clean: one lift at a fixed faster pace (240, so as never to slow an express line) is an express belt at Tier 1 on the flat — a traction ribbon stands on flat ground too, and costs plates and wire, no circuit; lift tiers matching the ribbon tiers mean two more ids, their art, their unlocks, two more tray slots, the drag matching tiers and a save remap, for the same outcome. The bridge has gone at its line's pace since 2026-10-13 (owner: « the speed at which routers, bridges etc convey the items should be the same as the input ribbon »): the lift now follows the same rule, one piece for every tier | **Pace**: content `traction_ribbon.belt` is `{ ticksPerTile: 6, perMin: 60 }`, its own pace — a plain ribbon's, the floor of its line's (it was 12 / 30). graph/compile.ts `linePace`: a chain whose tier is a lift gets the pace (`perMin`, `ticksPerTile`) of the fastest ribbon it meets on its line — the ribbons feeding it and the one it hands on to, looked for through bridges and other traction ribbons — never under its own; the fastest by a total order (pace, then building index), so the visit's order never shows. It keeps its own tier, so its chain stays its own: a chain's key (tier, head, length) is what it was, and a saved line loads with its items on their tiles, the lifts' too — no new id, no `retired.ts` line, no `FIELDS` entry, no `SAVE_VERSION` bump (a save holds no pace). The ribbon paces divide each other (60, 120, 240) and step on the world's clock, so a lift steps whenever its line does and a full line moves through it as one block across the joins (`willShift`); its step period is a ribbon tier's (10 or 5 ticks), dividing `CYCLE_LCM`. graph/groups.ts's group signature hashes a chain's `perMin` beside its tier (a lift's pace is not its tier's). **Drag** (D73): a step still takes the traction ribbon whatever tier is dragged; one already standing on the step, the same way, is now kept when a faster ribbon is dragged over the line (it was refused as `occupied`: a tile left out at each step) — it takes the new pace by itself. **Look**: an empty elevator car's way back takes `returnTicks` = min(`RETURN_TICKS` 5, `RETURN_SHARE` 0.4 × a step of the faster of its two tiles' lines) (render/world/belt-lift.ts): on a full line the next item reaches the car half a step after the last one left it, so the car is home first at every tier (at 5 ticks it snapped to a swift line's items). **Words**: the description says it (« Il va à l'allure de sa ligne… jamais moins vite qu'un ruban (60/min) »); the building card's rate and speed read « l'allure de sa ligne, 60/min au moins » (`card.rate.lift`, `card.speed.lift`). No research node, quest or tip stated its rate. **Guard**: content/validate.ts checks every ribbon's step period (600 / gcd(600, perMin)) divides `CYCLE_LCM`, a conduit's `ticksPerTile` too (ribbons ignore theirs: the camouflaged ribbon's is 9), and a lift no slower than a plain ribbon. Defaults chosen without asking: the floor is the lift's own 60 (a lift on a camouflaged line, 40/min, goes 60); the fastest of all it meets on its line, not only what feeds it (a lift between a plain ribbon and a swift one goes swift: faster than its feeder never slows anything, and the rule reads the same both ways); a run of traction ribbons laid by hand on the flat behind an express ribbon runs express, as a bridge pair does — cheaper a tile than express ribbons, the express ribbon's research still needed for the pace, accepted as the bridge's already is. **Bases**: the debug bases' lines over the Crown (the quartz line, the late base's) carry their line's 60 now; the quartz base keeps its two cutters (20 a minute, D133). Tests: [lift-pace.test.ts](../src/sim/lift-pace.test.ts) (a plain line over a ridge carries 60 a minute end to end and is as full past its lifts as behind them; a swift line 120, an express line what it carries on the flat; the fastest ribbon met, the floor alone; a faster drag keeps the lifts; offline as online, `hash()` equal; a saved line loads with its items on its lifts and nothing goes to the Heart; the step periods; the card), belt-lift.test.ts (the car home within half a step at every tier), validate.test.ts; shots: `scripts/lift-shots.mjs` (`TAG=before|after`) |
| D142 | **A water port is pipework: a pump's outlet and a water drinker's water entrance are drawn as a flanged steel stub with a valve, a conduit's end at a port as a bolted coupling; the content names each drinker's water entrance, and the look follows the water** (owner, 2026-10-18: « the conduit and waterpump exit should have a specific style that looks more like conduit. and everything that uses water »; defaults decided by the session) | A pump's outlet and the water intakes of the steam boiler, the brickworks and the ceramic oven were the ribbons' hatches (a turquoise box, a coral nozzle, an LED), and a conduit ran into them as a ribbon does: nothing showed that a line was plumbing, nor which of a drinker's two entrances wanted the water (either takes it, D114) | **Content**: `PortDef.fluid?: true` ([content/types.ts](../src/content/types.ts)), written `PW(…)` in buildings.ts: the pump's and the high-pressure pump's outlet, the steam boiler's right intake `(2, 1, S)` (D114's « water intake on the right »), the brickworks' and the ceramic oven's second entrance `(1, 1, S)` — the one `scenario-basics` already watered and the late debug bases water on seeds 1–3, but for seed 1's boiler. **Rule** ([content/validate.ts](../src/content/validate.ts) `fluidPortProblems`, run by `validateContent`): a building that gives a fluid (a pump; a recipe putting one out) has every outlet fluid, none other has a fluid outlet; one that drinks (`gen.water`, a recipe taking a fluid) has exactly one water entrance and a dry one for each other kind of input (D140's count less the water); none other has a water entrance. **The engine is untouched — the default chosen**: the sim never reads the flag; either entrance of a drinker still takes water and a conduit or a ribbon still joins any port, so no base built before changes: no command, replay, save shape, `SAVE_VERSION` or migration. Refusing a ribbon at a water entrance (or a conduit at a dry one) would have starved every base that waters the other entrance — the seed-1 debug base's boiler, the late base's potteries, which pick the nearer entrance, and any player's. **The look follows the water** instead: [render/world/water-ports.ts](../src/render/world/water-ports.ts) `wetEntranceOf` reads what joins each entrance in the graph (a conduit, or a pump set against it: water; a ribbon, an outlet: dry) and [art/pipe-ports.ts](../src/render/art/pipe-ports.ts) `wetEntrance` names the entrance that wears the pipework — the content's, unless water comes in by another, or a dry line takes the content's while another is free. The atlas bakes each drinker's variants in every turn (`wetKey`, iso and plan; the boiler's flywheel frames shared with its own look), the buildings view picks one (it is part of the building's `sigOf`), the switched-off look (D83) draws the same variant (`drawOff(…, wet)`); the lights view keeps reading the default art: the valve's lamp sits exactly on the hatch's LED point, in its colour. **Art**: in iso a steel stub out of the wall in the conduit's colours — a dark collar, a glazed band of the conduit's teal with its glint, a pale flange bolted in brass whose mouth shows the water when it faces the viewer, a valve standing on it, its handwheel turquoise on an intake and coral on an outlet (the hatches' code), its hub a cyan lamp; in plan the stub and its water line, the flange and the handwheel's ring and spokes (no chevron, no lamp). Where a conduit meets a port — an outlet behind it, an intake it runs into — its end gets `COUPLING_KEY` (`drawCoupling`: two steel flanges with a gasket and brass bolts across the line past its rails, the water line into a socket), laid at that edge in both views (`couplingSides`, part of the conduit's `sigOf`), on a hatch too. Show, don't tell: no label. The build ghost and the build bar's icon are the plan sprite: they show the water entrance before anything is joined. Tests: [pipe-ports.test.ts](../src/render/art/pipe-ports.test.ts) (the rules; every building's art draws pipework exactly on its water ports in both views and every turn, a variant on the entrance it names; a variant's lamps are its own look's; the plan's pipework and the coupling bake none), [water-ports.test.ts](../src/render/world/water-ports.test.ts) (a boiler watered by its coal intake wears the variant; couplings at the pump's outlet and the intake only), validate.test.ts; shots: `scripts/pipe-shots.mjs` (`TAG=before|after`; `SHEET=1`: the atlas's pictures, every turn, variant and unlit look) |

It reveals the turbine's plan only; its brief drops the generator and says a steam boiler will hold the night too, later, with the base's coal and some water; its reward is a turbine's materials (`kit: 'wind_flower'`). **The default chosen**: the steam boiler stays « La vapeur »'s, two quests on, where its pump, conduit and glass come one at a time after « La verrerie » — moving it into the night quest would teach three buildings and water before glass; solar with storage was set aside, the panel being lab research (`en2`) and the accumulator Tier 2's, neither in the first hour. the states hard to tell apart (done teal, ready barely different from locked, the Core's lock only a band's hatching), nothing saying « this next »; the gates' pills in every header repeating the lines; on a phone a 390 px window into the canvas. **References** (from the games themselves; a web search turned up little): *Factorio* — the state colours (researched, available, not yet) and its focused view of a technology (what it needs, what it opens, the icons of what it unlocks), its search filtered to what can be researched → the badges, the available-now filter, the chain focus kept from D107; *Civilization VI* — eras as columns read left to right, the whole tree's height on screen and the eras scrolled sideways, the path to a chosen goal numbered → the stage columns, the zoom that fits the lanes' height, `pathTo`; *Oxygen Not Included* — a clean left-to-right graph, bands of one colour per branch, orthogonal links → the lanes, every link left to right; *Dyson Sphere Program* — a strict grid, square icons, links snapped to its channels → the columns and the gaps links turn in, the far zoom's tiles; *Satisfactory* — the HUB's tiers gated by the Space Elevator's phases, a milestone wall → the walls and their locks, the navigator's segments; *Stellaris* — no tree but cards that say what they unlock and their category's colour → the cards' icons first; *Anno 1800* — progress gated by a population tier, not a node → the Core's level as its own lock; *Frostpunk* — categories with level gates → a category's padlock on its header; *Against the Storm* — tiers with a level requirement read before the content → the rail's heads (the Core's level, the key, Tier 2's expertises) | [screens/tree-layout.ts](../src/ui/screens/tree-layout.ts) (new, pure; the layout, the zoom's helpers, `chainOf` moved out of research.ts): `treeLayout` — `zones` (the Heart's pillar, the plans, the lab's pillar, each tier), `lanes` (the six themes in `laneOrder`: of every order, the one whose links between lanes skip the fewest lanes, the content's nearest on a tie — today Énergie, Extraction, Logistique, Machines, Analytique, Commerce), `cells` (a plan path in its theme's lane, `PATH_THEME`; a tier's categories stacked in their lane, a later tier's ordered by where their gates stand), `walls` (before Tiers 2–4, the one before Tier 2 wider, D118; each its key `TIER_GATE` in its tier's last column, the lock level with it); a research node's column is its longest chain of prerequisites of its tier, any category (so every link runs left to right); `route` turns in the gap nearest the target, else round a channel; the category gates straight along their lane where level. `openZoom` (the lanes' height in the view, 45 % at least), `zoomLevel` (near ≥ 75 %, mid ≥ 40 %), `pathTo` (every need not done, in the order the tree reads). [research.ts](../src/ui/screens/research.ts): the stage (`.tr-stage`: the rails `.tr-rail` — `.tr-colhead`, `.tr-tierhead[data-tier][data-locked]` — and `.tr-lanerail`, positioned at every scroll and zoom by `paintRails`; the tools: `.tr-nav` with `.tr-navseg` and `.tr-navwin`, `[data-tree-filter]`, the zoom bar), the canvas (`.tr-zone`, `.tr-band`, `.tr-wall`, `.tr-key`, `.tr-cat` and `.tr-cathead` — a plan path's is `.tr-lane[data-kind="plan"]`, in `PLAN_PATHS` order —, the gates' arrows, links of kind `root` / `lab` / `line` / `cross` / `key`), the cards' badges (`.tr-badge`, `data-core`) and path numbers (`.tr-step`, `paintSteps`), the pillars, the legend in the header, the detail's « Pour y arriver » (`.rs-path`, `.rs-step[data-step-id]`: the same numbers, the Core's levels and the expertises in their places) instead of the prerequisites' ✓ / ✗ and the gate bars; the zoom kept in `filon.tree.zoom.v2` (0: the tree's own). The plans' block, the gate's caption, the tier bands, the rule and the pins (`.tr-pins`) are gone. the plan pieces redrawn as a tube (no rails, LEDs or chevron). **Depth** ([world/stand.ts](../src/render/world/stand.ts) `pipeZ` = 8·(x + y) + 0.6, on the floor and the rock): after its diagonal's columns, flat pieces and the items riding a ribbon there, before a building of the diagonal, a deck over it and all of the next diagonal — in front of a building's south and east faces, behind its north and west ones; | D151 | **Waste has no use: no machine takes slag or scrap in. Research unchanged: `ex_brick` and `dn_steel` still unlock the brick and the steel, their texts never named the recyclers, and D107's checks stay green. **What waste is still good for, kept on purpose**: burnt in an incinerator (the player's own way out, D51, and what « Les scories » teaches); sold for next to nothing at a trade post's entrance (D131), or, what the Core holds (from before D139, or handed back by a refund), « Au comptant » at the Crier and auto-sold (D127): 0.2 ◉ a slag, 0.6 a scrap, the price of getting rid of it, not a living. The remap (D47) finds the two ids gone from a save's recipe table and clears each machine set to one: no recipe, and the player picks the one left, which the inspector offers. It hands the Heart the machine's buffers and its waste bin, and now, for every machine it resets, the inputs of a craft under way, as `refundNode` does in play (until now they went unseen). A retired recipe's counts come from `RETIRED_RECIPES` in [retired.ts](../src/content/retired.ts), which holds each one's name and its inputs a craft, since a save's table holds ids, not counts.
