| D153 | **CrierBelt's delivery orders and the claims' standings: past the tutorial CrierBelt posts timed orders for the base's own goods — two or three at a time, drawn from what the base can make at its Core's level, paid 10–20 % over the bid with no fee once filled, sometimes with a special reward —; the player accepts one (« Accepter », a command), and what a trade post's entrance brings of its item goes to it before the sale; a missed order simply closes, what it got paid at the market. The claim's value is ranked every world hour against the forty claims of the Crier (« Classement des concessions »), whose values follow a seeded growth curve** (the lead's brief, 2026-10-19: the tutorial ends at 42–63 minutes and leaves only the next research and the next Core level; the owner agreed to delivery orders and a ranking; D146's numbers; the defaults decided here, all in `BAL.orders` / `BAL.ranking`) | Goals for a base past its tutorial, short and long, and a market that matters. **Accepting, not filling automatically**: an order the player takes is a choice and a commitment (the window starts at « Accepter »), and an order nobody wanted never diverts a line's goods from the post's sales. **A missed order's penalty is its premium, nothing else**: it closes, and what it got is paid as the post would have sold it (the bid less CrierBelt's fee) — nothing vanishes (no confiscation), no reputation to manage; the player loses only what accepting promised. **Paid in full once filled** (not item by item): one notice, one sum — CrierBelt's voice, « Commande livrée : … versés ». **Through a post's entrance only** (D131: it takes every tradable item), the base's real logistics: the Core never gives (D34), so the first order teaches the player to lead a line of quartz to the post — and the post then keeps selling it (D146's problem 7: 2 500 quartz idle by hour 3, nothing telling the player it sells). **Off the market**, as the starter contract (D101): an order's goods move no price, and its price (the bid when posted + 10–20 %) stays under a purchase's (the ask + the fee), so no order is filled at a profit from the Crier's own goods. **Feasible by construction**: a good is drawn only if a recipe making it and a machine running it are unlocked (a raw good: its extractor's nodes), from the Core's level its row names — never something the base cannot make; the size grows with the level (300 · 450 · 900 · 1 800 · 3 600 ◉ at reference value), the window by the good's class (D146: raw and plates 20–40 minutes, glass 30–60, wire and parts 60–120; every window outlasts a night), an event order one draw in ten (×3, ×1.5 the window, 1 500 ◉ or more, always a reward). **The first order**, right after « Les pièces de la Synthèse » and before any slot: 120 quartz in 40 minutes, +20 % and a 150 ◉ bonus — sized on what a router on the quartz line brings the post in that time (the pacing bot fills it in 14–15 minutes, about 670 ◉ and the bonus): the tutorial's next step without touching the quest table, its own notice card. **Special rewards**: a bonus in ocelles (by level: 100 · 150 · 400 · 1 200 · 3 000 ◉, an event's three times it), a building kit (a building's materials ×n to the Core), or — an event's — a research node the tree offers, learnt at once. **Never AI tokens** (owner, 2026-10-19: « there can't be ai tokens reserve »: servers give tokens or they do not; nothing banks them), so no reward adds to `Sim.tokenBank`, which stays only for old saves' converted data. « A plan unlocked early », the brief's example, is a research node: every plan is held before the tutorial ends (« Le laboratoire » needs them all). An emblem or cosmetic reward is left for later (the emblem's parts are not lockable yet). **The standings**: the forty claims have no books, so their values follow the seeds — a ladder at the player's founding from 1 500 ◉ up by ×1.194 a rank (the leader ≈ 1.5 M ◉), each growing linearly, the foot 40 % a day, the top 4 %, × its profile's growth, a ±6 % day-to-day wobble —, counted from the player's founding as the market counts its colonies' growth. A new base (≈ 1 000 ◉) is last; the tutorial's (≈ 3 000) passes a few in the first hour; the pacing bot stands 35th, 27th–28th, 24th–25th at hours 1, 2, 3; the late debug base (≈ 52 000 ◉) about 20th; each rank asks ×1.19 more and the leaders grow by tens of thousands a day: hard to top, steady to climb. Hourly, on the world's clock; a statistic like the value it ranks | **Engine**: [systems/orders.ts](../src/sim/systems/orders.ts) (state `sim.deliveries`; `drawOrder` from `hash32(worldSeed ^ salt, playerSeed, epoch, k)`; `orderGoods`/`canMake`; `acceptDelivery`; `fillAtPost` called by each post in `tradeEpoch` before its sales; `deliveryEpoch` after the posts: paid, rewarded, missed, withdrawn, posted), the goods and kits in [content/orders.ts](../src/content/orders.ts), the numbers in `BAL.orders`; [systems/ranking.ts](../src/sim/systems/ranking.ts) (`rivals`, `rivalValue`, `standings`, `rankEpoch` from `Sim.tradeEpoch` after the value's sample; `sim.ranking`), `BAL.ranking`. The command `acceptDelivery` (exec, replay); the events `delivery` (posted, filled, missed, withdrawn) and `rank` (a climb, the claims passed), dropped by the catch-up with the trade's; a `delivery` ledger line and purse kind (a one-off). Offline-exact by construction: every step at the boundary in `tradeEpoch`, the same call online and offline, drawn from the seeds and state exact at a boundary, filled from the posts' entrances; a research learnt as a reward rebuilds the graph. **Saves**: `deliveries` (items by id) and `ranking`, optional, `FIELDS` plain, no `SAVE_VERSION` step; the standing orders hashed, their history and the standings not. **UI**: the Crier's « Commandes » tab (cards: the item and count, a bar of what was delivered, the time left draining, the pay and how much over a sale, the reward as a chip, « Accepter »; the history) and « Classement des concessions » first in the Gazette (the rank, the claims around it, the gap as a bar); the HUD's Orders chip (a bar per order accepted, a badge of the offers); the first order's notice card ([ui/order-notice.ts](../src/ui/order-notice.ts)); toasts for a new order, one filled (and its reward), one missed, a climb in the standings. **Pacing** ([pacing.md](pacing.md#what-delivery-orders-add-d153)): the orders player (lands 1 and 3) is paid 1 550–2 330 ◉ by orders over three hours and, with the quartz branch the first order makes it lay, sells 1 300–1 900 ◉ more an hour from hour 2; its purse ends hour 3 at 3 700–6 500 ◉ instead of 290–1 310; the milestones move by four minutes at most. Tests: [orders.test.ts](../src/sim/orders.test.ts), [ranking.test.ts](../src/sim/ranking.test.ts), the pacing test's `orders` set; the smoke's order accepted and filled through a post; [scripts/orders-shots.mjs](../scripts/orders-shots.mjs). **Left**: an emblem or cosmetic reward; the absence report does not list the orders filled or missed while away (the purse and the Crier's history do); the pacing bot's builders find no room for the quartz router on land 2 |
| D154 | **Blueprints: an area copied (Ctrl/⌘+C and a box, or the building inspected), pasted as a ghost on the cursor — turned by R, mirrored by M when every building in it has a mirror twin — and laid through the player's own commands, paid whole or not at all, what does not fit skipped and shown; a library of named blueprints kept in the base's save (K); an upgrade tool lifting an area's ribbons a tier (N, once a faster ribbon is learnt); Ctrl/⌘+Z taking back the last 32 placements, ribbon drags, pastes, upgrades or demolitions** (the lead's brief, 2026-10-19: « as bases grow, laying the same smelter row by hand is the biggest friction »; the owner agreed to blueprints, copy-paste and area upgrades; the defaults decided here) | By the second and third hour (D146, [pacing.md](pacing.md)) a base lays the same works again and again — a row of smelters with their ribbons, routers and pole — tile by tile. **From the start, no node**: copy and paste are a comfort, not a reward; they come with building itself (the tutorial's `build` reveal, as the other tools), and the tree is untouched (D107's validation stays as it was). The upgrade tool means nothing before a faster ribbon: it shows once the Swift Ribbon (`xp_swift`, Tier 1 Logistique) is learnt. **Paid whole or not at all**: a paste that runs out half-way leaves half a smelter row nobody asked for, which reads worse than a refusal that names what is missing; the ghost already says it before the click (the tip's chips in coral, D-tip). The same for an upgrade. **What does not fit is skipped and shown**: one piece on water or on another building does not spoil the rest of a big paste; the ghost draws it coral, the tip counts it, the toast says how many and why. **Mirror only with twins**: most 2 × 2 machines take in at one corner and give out at another; mirrored, their ports would fall where no turn of the building puts them, and silently turning them would break the line the blueprint was. A blueprint of lines, routing blocks and symmetric machines mirrors; one holding such a machine says which and stays as it is. M is the Crier's key otherwise (« miroir » / mirror in both languages); the free letters were J, K, N and Y. **The library in the save**, not beside it: an export carries it to another device (Safari's 7 days, M7), a base is the player's one place (D1), and the save's rules already hold it (an optional field, `FIELDS` plain). By content ids, so a reordered content table leaves it alone. Not hashed: the base's notes, not its state. **Undo in the shell, through commands**: the engine stays without history (a replay sees ordinary `remove` / `place` / `link`), and the shell knows what a gesture was (one drag, one paste, one box) | **Engine** ([sim/systems/blueprint.ts](../src/sim/systems/blueprint.ts)): `capture` (every building touching a box but the Core, its place from the box's corner, rotation, recipe, a routing block's items by side, the wires between two pieces), `layout` (mirror left ⇄ right then turn clockwise, a router's or distributor's sides turned with it; the cursor on the middle as `originFor`), `mirrorRot` / `mirrorBlocker` (a building's twin: the rotation whose footprint and ports are its mirror image's), `planPaste` / `planPieces` (each piece through `placementRot` and `canPlace` — `unaffordable` alone is no refusal: one budget sums what fits net of what it replaces, `short` what the Core lacks), `lay` (nothing when short; else the pieces that replace something first, then buildings, poles, line pieces, each a `place` with `turned`; a piece already standing as it should is kept, its settings set by `recipe` / `forkFilter`; then the wires by `link`), `planUpgrade` / `upgradeArea` (each tiered line piece to the next faster tier unlocked, from the content: plain ribbon → swift → express; the one conduit has none), `blueprintCost`, the library (`saveBlueprint`, `renameBlueprint`, `deleteBlueprint`, `cleanBlueprint`). No new rule for placing, no `paste` command: its pieces are logged one by one. `Sim.capture`, `planPaste`, `paste`, `planUpgrade`, `upgradeArea`, `saveBlueprint`, `renameBlueprint`, `deleteBlueprint`; commands `bpSave`, `bpRename`, `bpDelete`; `Sim.blueprints` (saved as `blueprints`, optional, absent when empty; `FIELDS` plain; read back through `cleanBlueprint`, a malformed one dropped; no save version). `turns` exported from build.ts. **Input**: `Tools` states `copy` (a box → `capture`, the clipboard, in hand at once as a paste), `paste` (`rot`, `mirror`; a click → `paste`; its plan cached per cursor, turn, tick and table), `upgrade` (a box); `Preview.area` and `Preview.plan`; `ToolResult.copied` / `short` / `upgraded` / `empty`; every gesture that lays or takes down recorded by the undo stack ([input/undo.ts](../src/input/undo.ts): what it put up, what it took down with its place, settings and wires; undone by `remove`, `place` with `turned`, `link`; the older steps re-pointed at the ids put back; `DEPTH` 32, this session and this colony only; not undone: wires, marks, settings, land, research; what came down comes back empty and only if its ground is free and the Core can pay again; a ribbon off the claim's land gives back its price, not the kites' surcharge). Keys: `CHORDS` z / c / v with Ctrl or ⌘ (undo, copy, paste; preventDefault), K the library, N the upgrade tool, M mirrors with a blueprint in hand. **Shell / UI**: `BuildMode.copy` / `paste` / `upgrade` / `mirror` / `undo` / `library` and their toasts; the library panel ([ui/blueprint-panel.ts](../src/ui/blueprint-panel.ts): the copy in hand to name and save, each blueprint's icon over a little plan, its size, cost chips, « Prendre », rename in place, a two-click delete; « Copier une zone » and « Coller » for a finger); the build bar's « Gabarits » tab (K) and the Logistique tray's « Améliorer » slot (N); the tray steps aside while a blueprint tool is in hand; the tip's three counts (to lay, already there, refused) and the whole's cost; the ghost's copy box (turquoise), upgrade box (amber) and a paste's dashed frame. **Measured** (the late base, 225 pieces copied, taken down and pasted back): engine plan 0.6 ms, paste 2.0 ms, the next tick's recompile 2.5 ms (CPU, [bench/blueprint-perf.test.ts](../src/sim/bench/blueprint-perf.test.ts)); in Chrome the click's own work 1.1 ms and one frame of 31 ms after it (the views rebuilt), the frames before and after at 16.7 ms. Tests: [blueprint.test.ts](../src/sim/blueprint.test.ts) (capture, turn and mirror of settings, twins, a smelter line pasted turned makes what the original makes, skipped pieces, the whole paid or refused, kites off the land, the library through save and load, the upgrade, replay), [undo.test.ts](../src/input/undo.test.ts), [bindings.test.ts](../src/input/bindings.test.ts); the smoke's step (Ctrl+C, a box, R, a click, Ctrl+Z); [scripts/blueprint-shots.mjs](../scripts/blueprint-shots.mjs) |
| D158 | **A junction's deck comes down only on line pieces; its tunnel takes anything; junctions chain along their tunnels; its rotation turns its deck** (owner, 2026-10-19: « the junction should prevent putting something other than a ribbon on the two sides where the junction brings the items, but not on the other two sides. You can have items going below multiple junctions in a row ») | **The reading.** A junction is a little bridge (owner, 2026-10-13): one line rides over its humped deck — its ramps bring the items up and set them down on the two sides along the deck — and the other passes under it, through a tunnel. « Items going below multiple junctions in a row » can only be the tunnel's line, so the two restricted sides are the deck's ends and the free ones the tunnel's mouths. (The other reading — the side each line leaves by — would keep a junction from standing next to another, against « multiple junctions in a row ».) **Before**: D73 refused any two junctions side by side, so a drag across lines side by side always took a bridge pair, and a junction could hand straight into a router or a machine on any side, its ramp coming down on a building. **Why its rotation turns its deck**: every junction was drawn north–south whatever its rotation. On fixed axes the rule would let rows chain east–west only (a drag south across lines side by side still bridged), and a plan or a blueprint (D154) laid turned would keep a junction against a building legal in two turns out of four: the late base's Fabrique, laid in any of four turns, had no legal turn left. So the deck follows the rotation — north–south for rot 0 and 2 (the look every junction had), east–west for 1 and 3 — and R turns it in hand. **What counts as a ribbon**: every ribbon tier, the traction ribbon and the buried one, and a skybridge end (the planner sets bridge ends right after junctions); not a conduit, a routing block, a building, a mast, a fence or another junction. Nothing empty is refused: a junction may be laid before its lines | **Placement** ([systems/build.ts](../src/sim/systems/build.ts)): `deckSides(rot)`, `overDeck`, `deckEndOk`, `deckBlocks` — both ways: a junction whose deck end holds something else, and anything (any footprint) at a junction's deck end — refused as `junction_deck` (D73's `junction_chain` is gone); `turns` (a junction turned a quarter is another junction, half round the same: `replaceable`); `placementRot` turns a junction's deck to the other axis by itself when only that one fits, unless the player turned it (R). **Drags** ([systems/belt-plan.ts](../src/sim/systems/belt-plan.ts)): a planned junction's deck runs along the line it crosses, so the drag runs under it and a drag across lines side by side lays a row of junctions, either way; along the drag when only that axis fits; a planned junction at another's deck end is refused (`junction_deck`, leapable by a bridge pair); a leap next to planned junctions takes them in, so a row by a pond is leapt whole as before. **Engine**: nothing to change — `compile.ts` `across` already looks past a run of junctions and D121 gives the line a cell under each (offline exact: the twin in [junction-deck.test.ts](../src/sim/junction-deck.test.ts), bit for bit, flowing, backed up under the row, flowing again). **Saves**: version 24, `junctionsUpright` ([persist/migrate.ts](../src/persist/migrate.ts)): each junction a save holds set upright (rot 0), so it keeps its look. What stands at a deck end from before the rule stays and works (grandfathered): the junction's inspector panel « Son pont » ([ui/inspector/junction.ts](../src/ui/inspector/junction.ts)) shows which line goes over and which under, and a warning row with that building's icon. **Render**: one drawing a deck axis — the east–west one is the north–south drawing reflected across the tile's diagonal, not turned (`JunctionFrame`, `JunctionTopFrame`: the near wall with the arch faces the viewer, the slices run far to near); the atlas bakes junctions per rotation (`ORIENTED`, art/terminals.ts); `junctionFrames(rot)`; item-flow.ts rides or passes each junction of a row on its own (`junctionOver`); junctions-view.ts lays the passage along the tunnel (`underPass`). D130's parts and keys are unchanged. **UI**: the tip names the refusal and shows the junction, its deck coming down (glyph `deckEnds`) and the ribbon; the ghost draws a junction in hand's two deck ends (turquoise arrows, coral with the tile framed where blocked) and, for a piece refused at a junction's deck end, that junction's deck coming down on it; the card's legend and the junction's description say it. **Debug bases**: the plan kit has `+` (deck down the plan) and `=` (across it), turned with the plan; the Fabrique's glass bus runs a row higher, so its junctions have a ribbon at each deck end; the post's « junction at an outlet beside a router » fallback (scenarios.ts) can no longer be laid and is skipped. Tests: [junction-deck.test.ts](../src/sim/junction-deck.test.ts) (every side of every rotation both ways round, a 2 × 2 footprint, turning by itself, a row carrying items at pace, the twin, a grandfathered router), [belt-plan.test.ts](../src/sim/belt-plan.test.ts), [persist/junctions-d158.test.ts](../src/persist/junctions-d158.test.ts), [junctions-view.test.ts](../src/render/world/junctions-view.test.ts), [item-flow.test.ts](../src/render/world/item-flow.test.ts); shots: `scripts/d158-shots.mjs`. Noted in passing (not D158's): a silo handing out by both outlets into lines counts a few more items burnt offline than online (`consumed` only, the hash agrees: 1 008 against 1 000 over an hour, with or without junctions) |
| D159 | **A notice stays as long as its words take to read: a toast at least 3 s and 60 ms a character (12 s at most), held while the pointer is on it, put away at a click; the night notice stays until it is read** (owner, 2026-10-19: « the notifications shouldn't go away so fast, i couldn't read the stuff about the core's solar panels. Also it's my first time seeing this even though i've been playing for dozens of in-game days and nights ») | Every toast went after 2.6 s whatever its length (a 140-character notice too), and the night notice (D101) shows about a game hour (1.5 minutes) before a sunset and went by itself at nightfall, often unread. It showed for the first time on the owner's base because D143 took the coal generators out (refunded at load): its night cover fell under « Tenir la nuit »'s 80 %, which is when the notice speaks | [ui/toasts.ts](../src/ui/toasts.ts): `READ_MS` 3 000 + `PER_CHAR_MS` 60 × the text's length, at most `MAX_MS` 12 000, a caller's longer time kept; `pointerenter` holds it, `pointerleave` re-arms it, a click puts it away (`.toast` takes the pointer, hud.css). [ui/night-notice.ts](../src/ui/night-notice.ts): no longer closed at nightfall; « Compris », the Électricité button, or the base holding the night close it (once per base, as before). Open (pacing's design question 1, D146): `nightCover` reads a fuelled boiler at its full output whatever its coal line can keep up |
| D160 | **Skybridge ends go in pairs, in order along their line** (owner, 2026-10-20: « if you put three or more bridges in a row, it makes something weird. bridge blocs should go in pair ») | Every end leapt to the next end of its way within reach, so in three ends in a row the middle one was both the first leap's far end and the second's start: drawn with a ramp down and a ramp up at once, its line leaping twice. A third end could also be refused instead; a pair rule reads simpler (no new refusal, nothing to learn) and keeps every layout placeable | **Engine** ([graph/compile.ts](../src/sim/graph/compile.ts)): `isFarEnd` — an end is the far end of the pair behind it when the nearest end behind within reach (its way, its level) is a start; a start leaps to the nearest end ahead within reach; a far end leaps nowhere and hands on to the tile ahead as a ribbon does (the next pair's start included). Three in a row: a pair and a lone end; four: two pairs. The views follow `graph.jumps` (decks, spans, items: unchanged code). **Placement** ([systems/build.ts](../src/sim/systems/build.ts)): D124's height rule reads the same pairs (`farEnd`, `partnerAhead`). **Drags** ([systems/belt-plan.ts](../src/sim/systems/belt-plan.ts)): `leap` lays a pair only when nothing of its way stands or is planned between its ends and the nearest behind its start within reach is a pair's far end or none — pairs now chain in one drag (two obstacles a tile apart: two pairs), and none is laid that a lone start behind would take as its far end. **Old saves**: no change of format; the same layouts pair by the rule. A line laid before over two leaps in a row compiles differently: its saved chain matches none, so its items go to the Heart (`deserialize`, nothing lost); its middle end is now a far end handing on to the tile ahead — empty where the second leap was, so items wait there until the player lays a ribbon or a second end. Tests: [bridge-pairs.test.ts](../src/sim/bridge-pairs.test.ts) (the rule by three, four, five ends and out of reach; the planner's chained pairs and none behind a lone start; a line over two pairs, a cell over each gap tile, its pace; offline bit for bit; an old save over two leaps, its items to the Heart) |
| D161 | **Far parcels from Tier 1, and every parcel 30 % dearer.** Owner, 2026-10-19: « you should be able to buy parcels that are not connected to your base from Tier 1. Parcels should also be a bit more expensive ». Far Claim (`xp_far`, id kept) moves from Tier 2's Territoire (after the camouflaged ribbon, D119) to the end of Tier 1's Logistique, after Fences and Glazed Ribbons: depth 2 (5 000 tokens), 40 iron plates and 40 copper wire (was 30 steel, 80 wire). The Territoire keeps leases, pylons, long bridges and the camouflaged ribbon, and waits for Far Claim through its gate (D128). A far parcel needs nothing more at Tier 1: held, it is the claim's land (no kite delay there); the plain ribbon (free) and the pole cross free land; turbines stand on it; the glazed ribbon keeps its line home from the Gleaners. Only a neighbouring base's land needs the Tier 2 camouflaged ribbon. Prices: `BAL.land`'s base, every tile price and `depositPerValue` × 1.3 (base 26 000, floor 78, rock and sliver 33, water 117, river 26, salt 91, vent 3 900, dunes 20, deposits 325): a bare parcel 517 → 673 ◉, the quartz vein's 500 → 648–656 ◉. Growth (1 100 ‰), rent (30 ‰ of the price an hour) and resale (market − 20 %, D119) unchanged, so they follow; the starting square stays free; no surcharge for a far parcel. The research lab quest's reward 600 → 700 ◉, so it alone still pays the vein's parcel (D90). The pacing bot leaves Far Claim until Tier 2 opens (its base buys no far parcel), so Tier 1 runs as before; re-run in [pacing.md](pacing.md). |
| D162 | **The neighbouring bases cost little memory: each is drawn in a look by the zoom — far, from half-size copies on a small sheet of their own; in detail, from the claim's atlas pages only where they are on the GPU already, copies of the rest — dropped once well out of view, and laid out ahead in idle time** (the lead's brief after D148: « bring the memory cost of the neighbouring bases back down now that the fog is gone ») | D148 put all 13 bases within 320 tiles in sight: drawn from the claim's atlas they brought most of its 13 pages onto the GPU (their frames fill 1.7 pages' worth, spread over 11), plus three 2048² dress pages; a first layout cost a frame up to 41 ms | Render only (sim and saves unchanged). world/neighbour-lod.ts: `zoomLook` (zoom × density: detail from 0.9, far under 0.75, held between; the atlas is baked at 2×, so a half-size copy is as sharp up to 1), `planBases`; laid within 34 tiles of the view, dropped 10 s past a 69-tile ring. world/neighbour-art.ts: the far and detail sheets (1024² pages, skyline packing: art/skyline.ts, `DynAtlas` `fit`), renewed past 6 pages, the detail one let go 20 s after the last base in detail, a retired sheet destroyed once nothing draws from it. Buildings view: `layForeign(…, art)` retextures a base's sprites (not its cast shadows, nor its pits' flame), `dropForeign`; lights, wires `dropForeign`; `warmForeign` (shadow masks, lamp places) in idle time. Sim: `Neighbours.layoutStep` / `laidOut`, the tries a generator (`tries`), the same base either way (neighbours.test.ts). Debug `FL.app.neighbours.plain` draws them as before. Measured (frozen builds, headless Chrome 1440 × 900, dpr 1, fresh base; before → after, textures the neighbours bring up, mipmaps counted): near (2 bases) +191 MB → +32 MB; mid 0.5 (4 bases) +191 → +16 MB; far 0.125 (13 bases) +298 → +27–32 MB; the late base +64–107 MB → +16–32 MB, no atlas page brought up. Zoomed out at once: their worst step a frame 43–45 → 15–23 ms, their total 255 → 85 ms, a first layout in a frame 44 → 0.1 ms (done in idle time). Same picture: paused A/B shots, ≤ 0.28 % of pixels differ (mean ≤ 0.27/255), on their ink edges. Tests: render/world/neighbour-lod.test.ts, render/art/skyline.test.ts, sim/neighbours.test.ts (stepped layout). [render.md](render.md#the-neighbouring-bases-cost-d162) |
| D163 | **Auto-sell keeps the Core's cap: its threshold at most 90 % of it, and an item nobody buys fills to the cap and backs up, seen** (owner, 2026-10-20: « the core somehow has more than 10k of an item stored. And the auto-sell doesn't work I think ») | D127 lifted the Core's cap for an auto-sold item (`UNCAPPED`) so its lines never backed up, and allowed a threshold up to the whole cap (the default 10 000). An item with no buyer (a bid of nothing: prices are pure supply and demand) was never sold and piled up past the cap without end; and a working one held the cap and a minute's arrivals over it, so the Stock read more than its cap either way | `autoSellCaps` and `UNCAPPED` removed: the Core's usual cap for every item. `autoSellMax` = `BAL.autoSell.maxShare` (900 ‰) of the cap; `autoSellKeep` never above it, so an older save's threshold at the cap sells again. The Stock's auto-sold row says « Personne n'en achète » in coral when the bid is nothing, and shows full at the cap. Tests: autosell.test.ts |
| D164 | **Auto-sell sells what arrives beyond the Core's cap, at the bid less half; no threshold** (owner, 2026-10-21: « the core can only store 10k of each item. If an item arrives and would be 10'001, it's sold instead with a tax of 50% ») | Replaces D127's threshold and D163's 90 % ceiling. The Core takes a switched-on item without its cap (`autoSellCaps`: `UNCAPPED`, as D127 did), so its lines never back up. At each tick's end `autoOverflow` moves what stands beyond the cap to `trade.autoPend`, so the stock never shows more than the cap. The minute's `autoSales` sells the whole items set aside at the bid less `BAL.autoSell.fee` (500 ‰); a part waits, and a bid of nothing gives nothing, said in coral in the Stock. The Stock's row loses its threshold mark and chip; the ◉ is an on/off switch. Saves: `autoSell` keeps its shape (an older threshold reads as on, stored as 0), `autoPend` is a new optional field; no version bump. Logs: `at` 0 or null, an older threshold read as on. Offline: the item is never FULL, and the catch-up calls `autoOverflow` after each step: bit for bit with arrivals only, within a few items when the Core also hands the item out at its cap. `cm_autosell`'s text says the new rule. Tests: autosell.test.ts rewritten |
| D152 | **Conduits share their water: a conduit dragged off the side of another draws its water there — a tee — and the tee shares it evenly among the ways that take it, a full way's share going to the others; two lines join where one runs into another's side** (owner, 2026-10-19: « we also need conduits that share the water between multiple paths »; defaults decided by the session) | Since D114 a pump's line reached one drinker: a conduit tile had one way on, a pump one outlet, so a boiler and a brickworks each needed a pump of their own, although one pump (60/min) waters both (30/min each). Joining already worked (a tile fed from several sides starts a new chain and takes its feeds in turn); splitting did not exist. Two designs were weighed: automatic tees, or a « répartiteur d'eau » piece in the Logistics plans. The tees read simplest — the player drags a pipe off a pipe, as real pipework branches, with nothing new to learn, buy, research or save, and D145's art already drew tees — and the owner's words ask for conduits that share, not a box | **The rule** ([graph/compile.ts](../src/sim/graph/compile.ts), after the feeds are known): a conduit tile that nothing feeds — no conduit runs into it, no outlet hands into it — draws its water off a conduit beside it on its level that runs across the edge between them: the one behind it first (a line dragged off a pipe's side), else on its left, else its right (a drag's first leg one tile long that turns at once); never off one running the same way beside it (two lines side by side stay two), nor off one that draws from it; tiles in ascending order. The tee hands water out of that side (`Graph.tees`: tile → sides; `Graph.drawsFrom`: branch → tee); the branch is fed from there (`feedDirs`, so a bend reads); a tee ends its chain (`next` is cut there), and its chain's head gets one out-edge per way: its way on first (a conduit or a port), then its branches N E S W. Up to three ways out (a cross). **The sharing** ([flow/node.ts](../src/sim/flow/node.ts) `shareFluid`, [flow/chain.ts](../src/sim/flow/chain.ts) `split`): what the head offers this tick (as before: its due water, up to `thrMu`) is split evenly among the ways, each capped by what it accepts; a way that takes less hands the rest to the others, round after round (max-min fair); what does not divide goes a milli-item a way in turn from `now % n`. Every way gets some each tick it can take any, so each branch is one steady stream (a conduit's runs merge; greedy turns would cut them into a run every other tick). Integers only; the phase contract holds (start-of-tick reads; the head's FIFO `take`n once per way, the sum never past the offer). **Offline**: no new node and no new state — the rotation is `now % n` over the chain's out-edges, which `buildGroups` already folds into `rotL` (any node with several out-edges), and the edges are in the `signature`; a twin test (a pump feeding a steam boiler and a brickworks through a tee, a day and a half, `catchUp(…, { noDirect: true })`) agrees bit for bit (`hash()`). **Saves**: none changed. No piece, no id, no field: a base's existing lines load as they were, and only where a conduit tail already lay against another's side with nothing feeding it — a dry line until now — does it start drawing water (the views draw the tee there). **The drag** ([systems/belt-plan.ts](../src/sim/systems/belt-plan.ts)): a conduit drag of two tiles or more that starts on a conduit and leaves it by its side keeps that tile as it runs (`BeltStep.tee` `split`), and one that ends on a conduit's side keeps it too (`join`) — only on a conduit with a way on (one that ends there still turns into the drag, a bend). **UI**: the placing tip shows the conduit, the tee's mark (a line parting in two, or two meeting) and the water, no words ([tip.ts](../src/ui/tip.ts), `Preview.tee`); the conduit's inspector on a tee has « Partage de l'eau » ([inspector/logistics.ts](../src/ui/inspector/logistics.ts) `teePanel`): the tee's water a minute, and a row per way — an arrow, ahead / left / right with the compass, the building it ends in (else the conduit), a bar of its share and its water a minute, sampled from the tee's per-way counters (`Chain.shared`, the views' only, never hashed); the Logistique view edges water lines in the water's deep teal, starts a branch's line at its tee and rings each tee with the valve's mark ([world/views-view.ts](../src/render/world/views-view.ts)); the conduit's card says it (one clause). **Art**: a tee that shares its water wears a sharing valve on its crown — a squat bolted bonnet, its stem and a brass handwheel (the ports' valve, `valveWheelIso`, neither turquoise nor coral: it shares) — in iso (`PIPE_VALVE_KEY`, its own taller frame, after the branches) and from above in plan (`CONDUIT_VALVE_KEY`); a join keeps D145's plain tee. A bend with a branch off its outer side is drawn as a straight from its feeder on to the branch, its way on the branch (`pipeOf` returns the plan's pieces too, `PipeOf.plan`). No lights. **Not done**: the pump's own several outlets still share greedily (`DrillNode.split`'s `allocRotating`, unchanged so as not to move any base's flows). Tests: [tees-d152.test.ts](../src/sim/tees-d152.test.ts) (the allocator, the compiled tee, an even split and a full way passing its share, a cross in thirds, a join of two pumps, the drag, a save, the twin), [water-ports.test.ts](../src/render/world/water-ports.test.ts) (D152); shots: `scripts/tee-shots.mjs` |
| D155 | **A pressure gauge: a fitting set into a conduit line whose dial reads its water network's pumps against its drinkers — green enough, amber tight, red short — and whose inspector says what to add** (owner, 2026-10-19: « we also need a special thing to attach to a conduit that will tell the player the pressure in it (if it's too low, need a better pump or more pumps) »; defaults decided by the session) | Since D152 one pump waters several drinkers, and nothing told the player whether the water is enough: a short line only shows as drinkers stopping for water | **The reading** ([readouts/pressure.ts](../src/sim/readouts/pressure.ts) `readPressure`): a water network is a connected set of conduit lines (chains of fluid joined by edges: lines running into each other, tees); its pumps those whose outlets hand into it, its drinkers the buildings it hands into — a boiler at full output (`waterEp`), a machine whose recipe takes water at its speed (a brickworks 30/min), an incinerator taking its line's throughput. Supply: each pump's `rateEp` × its grid's satisfaction now (and at full power, `nominal`); demand: all the drinkers working at once — a plan of the base, steady, not the moment's. Pressure ‰ = supply × 1 000 / demand (capped at `PRESSURE_MAX` 2 000); states by `BAL.pressure` (`ok` ≥ 1 250, `tight` ≥ 1 000, else `low`; `dry` with no water, `idle` with nothing drinking): a pump for one boiler is enough, for a boiler and a brickworks tight, for three boilers short. `pressureFix`: how many pumps or high-pressure pumps (`hp_pump`, 240/min: « a better pump » already existed) bring it to `ok`, and whether pumps lack power. It reads, never changes the sim, holds no state: online and offline alike. **The piece** (`pressure_gauge`, « Manomètre », appended, unlocked by Steam with the conduit; content `gauge`): kind `conduit`, 1 × 1, no `belt` of its own — compile.ts gives it its conduit's tier (`paceAt`), so a line through it stays one chain and keeps its water, and the upgrade tool (which tiers line pieces by their pace) never takes it for a faster conduit (validated). Set by a click (`isDragDef` false) onto a conduit only (`needs_conduit`), turned as that conduit runs (`placementRot`), replacing it; a conduit drag along the line keeps it, a conduit clicked on it takes it out (refunded). **Art**: in iso a bolted bonnet on the crown, a stem, a round dial facing the viewer — dark steel bezel, cream face, the scale's coral, amber and green arcs, ticks (`drawPipeGauge`, `drawDial`); in plan the dial facing up (`drawConduitGauge`); the needle a sprite of its own per state colour (`gaugeNeedleKey`), turned by the buildings view to the reading (`readGauges`: at a new graph or every half second of game time; `gaugeAngle`). A tee's valve gives way to a gauge on the same tile. **Inspector**: the dial with its needle and the state in a word at the top (`gaugeHero`); « Pression »: the pumps' icons and a bar of their water, the drinkers' and theirs, then — short or tight — each pump kind and its count to add (« +1 »), the high-pressure pump faded with the tree's mark while still to learn, a struck bolt where pumps lack power (`gaugePanel`). **Not done**: water lines coloured by pressure in the Logistique view. Tests: [pressure-d155.test.ts](../src/sim/pressure-d155.test.ts) (fed, tight, starved, dry, idle; what to add; a brickworks' ask; two networks; the content; placement, turn, one chain kept; the same flows with and without a gauge; online = offline bit for bit; reading changes nothing) |
